== Changelog ==

Every change to SEO Pro Stack. readme.txt lists the latest version in short.

= 1.3.213 =
* Added (Fluent Boards starter data, Website Setup board; GitHub issue #1607): a Setup Bing Webmaster Tools task (starters/fluent-boards.json, after Setup Google Search Console): import the site from Search Console or add it and verify it with a CNAME record, submit sitemap_index.xml, give the team access, and optionally Rank Math's Instant Indexing (IndexNow); done when the site is verified and the sitemap's status is Success. The Bing sentence leaves the Search Console task, the Accounts and Keys Checklist gains a Bing line, and Setup Google Translation now follows the Bing task. Add starter data adds the task to existing boards (only missing tasks are added); the changed Search Console text reaches new boards only.

= 1.3.212 =
* Changed (Watermark pictures, Keep Rank Math's site identity in sync; GitHub issue #1586): the picture chosen as Default social image (rank_math_social_image) is never watermarked, like the site icon and logos. SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::unmarkable() refuses it for whole-library marking, re-marking and uploads, and choosing a marked picture there (SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::social_image_chosen(), on seoprostack_settings_changed) puts its kept original back and ticks Never watermark. A removal that fails is logged and shown in the one-time admin notice, not fatal; other pictures are marked as before.
* Fixed (Keep pages cached, on by default; GitHub issue #1595): a fill opens custom menu links without a trailing slash at their slashed address. Footer menu links such as /about, /faq and /directory answer 301 to /about/ and the rest, so the fill spent a request on each redirect and left the page itself uncached after a purge unless the slashed form was linked too. Where permalinks end with a slash ($wp_rewrite->use_trailing_slashes), SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages::clean() now gives each cacheable address without a file extension its slash (slashed(); .html and .htm stay as they are) before removing repeats, so /about and /about/ count once, as /about/, where either first appears.
* Fixed, on by default (Plugin fixes; GitHub issue #1599): Readabler's Cognitive Disability Mode now outlines headings and links on sites whose Readabler settings were never saved; before, it seemed to do nothing. The profile added mdp-readabler-highlight-titles and mdp-readabler-highlight-links to the body, but FrontStyles::root_css() printed --readabler-highlight-titles-style: ; and --readabler-highlight-titles-width: px; (and the same for links), so outline: var(...) var(...) var(...) was invalid and computed to none; the var() fallbacks do not apply to a property that is set. Same cause as #1590: TabDesign collects each Design field's default only when the settings it has at that moment have the feature or its profile on. Highlight Hover, Highlight Focus, Reading Guide, Text Magnifier and Virtual Keyboard (colours, and virtualKeyboardLayout '' for the Blind Users profile) had the same gap. SEOProStack_Plugin_Fixes::readabler_design_defaults() (was readabler_mask_height(), same option_ and default_option_mdp_readabler_design_settings filters) now gives every Design setting that is missing or empty Readabler 2.0.20's own default (READABLER_DESIGN_DEFAULTS, read from TabDesign's fields with every feature on), with the text magnifier background spelt rgba (Readabler's rbga is invalid). Nothing is stored; a saved value is left alone.
* Fix: a secret settings field (such as a Cloudflare or GitHub token) cleared while its save is still running shows "Saved (hidden)…" once the save ends, instead of the empty field's placeholder until a reload; text typed during the save is still kept (WP Plugin Starter 1.0.66, GH#758 there; GitHub issue #1589).
* Developers: core files synced with WP Plugin Starter commit 37a1b52 (GitHub issue #1589, which named ffb0c87): scripts/deploy-sites.sh and scripts/lib/deploy-remote.sh (no install while another starter plugin's deploy lock is live on the host, --allow-concurrent-plugins to skip that check; a host kill (137) of the load check or cron list is rechecked at the end of the run and reported UNVERIFIED rather than failed; a killed one-off cron event is run again only while it is still listed; --ignore-log-line TEXT for a site's known harmless log line; "Nothing installed" when no install is found), scripts/check-deploy-log.sh, scripts/lib/plugin.sh (one reader for a {PREFIX}_VERSION define that spans lines, used by scripts/release-bump.sh, scripts/preflight-release.sh, scripts/provenance.sh and scripts/rename-plugin.sh), scripts/check-hooks.js (a deprecated call whose joined name it cannot read no longer counts as deprecating every hook with the same start), admin/js/seoprostack-admin.js, .github/workflows/release.yml (actions/setup-node 7.1.0, as CI uses), DEVELOPMENT.md and RELEASING.md. The starter's changelog asks for no change in this plugin's own files.

= 1.3.211 =
* Faster (Keep pages cached, on by default; GitHub issue #1593): with TranslatePress, a fill opens the main pages in every other published language too. TranslatePress serves each language under its slug (/en/, /fr/), which LiteSpeed caches as separate pages, but SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages planned the default language only: on a French/English network, wp seoprostack keep-cached --dry-run listed 150 French pages and, of the English ones, only /en/ (the header's language switcher link), and uncached builds took 7 to 8 seconds on a 9-language site (/ 8.3 s, /fr/ 6.9 s). of_site() now adds translations() of the main pages after them and before the rest of the site, page by page (each page in every language before the next), within MAX (1,000 addresses), so with many languages the last main pages are left out in every language. Addresses come from TranslatePress's own TRP_Url_Converter::get_url_for_language() (SEO Pack's translated slugs included) where it runs for the current site; a network site planned from the main site, where TranslatePress keeps the main site's settings, gets them from its own trp_settings (publish-languages and url-slugs, the default language's slug too with add-subdirectory-to-default-language), as the converter makes them without translated slugs. A network fill's first round stays in each site's own language; the second adds the translations, and network() now plans a site whose list no longer fits the queue whole in a later batch. post_changed() queues the post's page, the home page and the posts page in every language (with_translations()); the refill after LiteSpeed Cache's Pages purge rule stays in the site's own language. Checked on one hosting account's TranslatePress networks (LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3, TranslatePress 3.3.8 with Business 1.8.8 and SEO Pack): on 2, 3 and 9-language sites the settings-based addresses matched the converter's for all 3,333 page and language pairs checked; plans of 150 main pages grew to 294 addresses (2 languages) and 436 (3), and to the 1,000 cap with 9 (143 in the site's own language, about 107 in each other), and 1,350 conversions took 1.6 s.

= 1.3.210 =
* Fixed, on by default (Plugin fixes; GitHub issue #1590): Readabler's ADHD Friendly profile and Reading Mask no longer cover the whole page and take every click on sites whose Readabler settings were never saved. Readabler (checked with 2.0.20) gives the page $options['reading_mask_height'] ?? 0 (FrontScripts.php) and collects that height's default (100) only when TabDesign::fields_reading_mask() lists the field, which needs Reading Mask or the ADHD Friendly profile on in the settings it has at that moment; with the General tab never saved they are not, so the page got readingMaskHeight "0". SEOProStack_Plugin_Fixes::readabler_mask_height() (option_ and default_option_mdp_readabler_design_settings) gives settings without reading_mask_height Readabler's own '100'. Nothing is stored; a saved height is left alone.

= 1.3.209 =
* Changed (Ask before licence checks, Calls to other sites; GitHub issue #1584): our own plugins' calls are never held, limited or blocked: plugins, must-use plugins and themes whose folder starts with "seopro", and calls to the site in SEO Pro Stack's Author URI or its subdomains, which serves their licences and updates (owner's decision; they keep their calls light). SEOProStack_Licence_Calls::own_call() decides; pre_http() lets such calls through before any entry is made, entries stored before are left out of the Licence checks dialog (asked or opened), the Plugins screen row links and the count (choosable_entries()), and SEOProStack_Outbound_Calls::never_block() returns true for them. New seoprostack_own_call filter (docs/HOOKS.md).
* Fix: a secret settings field (such as a Cloudflare or GitHub token) shows the right placeholder after an AJAX save: "Saved (hidden)…" after saving one, the field's own placeholder after removing it with a space, and a save still pending never replaces text typed since (WP Plugin Starter 1.0.64, GH#716 there; GitHub issue #1572).
* Developers: core files synced with WP Plugin Starter commit 9e44e08 (GitHub issue #1572, which named b87b475): the release tooling fixes (scripts/build-release.sh, scripts/release-bump.sh, which now stops when README.md's heading for the version is not exactly ### X.Y.Z, scripts/deploy-sites.sh, scripts/lib/deploy-remote.sh, whose web PHP log baseline is now taken after the account-load wait, scripts/lint.sh), scripts/check-hooks.js (ignores calls inside PHP comments, checks each hook's listed type, reads multi-line version defines and hook names joined with "."), the CI workflow, the Starter sync workflow (a replacement issue keeps a no-auto-dispatch hold; this replaces the plugin-only fix the pull request first carried), STANDARDS.md, DEVELOPMENT.md and RELEASING.md.
* New (wp seoprostack health; GitHub issue #1577): copied Kadence/WPTT font-cache paths are checked on multisite too. A network copied to a new install inherits the network option downloaded_font_files, naming the source server's files, and the stylesheets Kadence then writes in wp-content/fonts named those paths, so headings lost their font; the check had returned not_run on every network (24 directory networks, 12 November 2026). It now reports both on any site's --url, and repairs them for the whole network only with --repair --network: entries are rebased to this install's wp-content/fonts files or dropped so the font loader downloads them again, the option write is verified, and WPTT stylesheets (<md5>.css) whose font URLs are server paths are removed so Kadence writes them again on the next uncached page view (single sites too, with --repair). --repair without --network leaves the shared cache unchanged and says so; font files are never deleted and copied paths never followed.
* New (Rank Math defaults; GitHub issue #1578): wp seoprostack rank-math identity [--dry-run|--adopt] [--format=table|json] lets a copied site hand its Rank Math identity over to Keep Rank Math's site identity in sync. SEOProStack_Rank_Math_Defaults::sync_identity() replaces only fields that are empty, already Site Identity's or still its last write (rank_math_identity_previous), so a copy kept the source's knowledgegraph_name, website_name, knowledgegraph_logo(_id) and open_graph_image(_id) for good (sync off on all 24 directory networks, 12 November 2026; a per-site script wrote them). The command lists each field beside Site Identity's value; --adopt, with Rank Math defaults and the sync on (it turns nothing on), writes those values, image URL and ID together, checks the save and records them as synced. Fields with no Site Identity value keep theirs, recorded as synced. The sync now also compares stored image URLs without their host or sites/<id>/ folder (identity_targets(), claimable(), image_key()), so a domain search-replace, which rewrites Rank Math's URLs but not the base64 record, no longer strands a synced logo or social image.
* Fixed, on by default (Plugin fixes; GitHub issue #1579): removing a file attached to a Fluent Forms upload field works again for visitors who are not logged in on pages cached for over a day. Fluent Forms prints file_delete_nonce (wp_create_nonce('fluentform_file_delete')) into the page, and Fluent Forms Pro's Uploader::deleteFile() (checked with 6.2.15) always verifies it, unlike uploads and submissions, whose validateNonce() is off by default (fluentform/nonce_verify): on a directory subsite, POST admin-ajax.php action=fluentform_delete_uploaded_file with an invalid _fluentform_file_delete_nonce, not logged in, got 403 "Security check failed". New SEOProStack_Plugin_Fixes::fluentforms_file_delete_nonce() (wp_ajax_nopriv_fluentform_delete_uploaded_file, priority 9, before Fluent's 10) replaces a refused nonce with a fresh one in $_GET/$_POST/$_REQUEST and in Fluent's shared request object (wpFluentForm('request')->set()), which copies the superglobals when first made. Fluent's canDeleteAttachment() and file token checks still apply; logged-in users keep the check, and so do sites that give visitors nonces of their own (core's nonce_user_logged_out filter returning a non-zero identity for fluentform_file_delete).

= 1.3.208 =
* Fixed, on by default (Plugin fixes; GitHub issue #1573): REST requests from cached pages keep working for visitors who are not logged in once the page's nonce has expired. Kadence Blocks Pro's query loop (dist/query.js) sends kbp_query_loop_rest_endpoint.nonce, printed in the page, as X-WP-Nonce for its filters and further pages, and other plugins' scripts send wp_rest nonces the same way. A nonce lasts 12 to 24 hours, while LiteSpeed Cache keeps public pages for a week by default (cache-ttl_pub 604800) and Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages gives Cloudflare a day. rest_cookie_check_errors() (rest_authentication_errors, priority 100) still checks a nonce sent with no login and answers 403 rest_cookie_invalid_nonce ("Cookie check failed"): on a directory subsite, GET /wp-json/wp/v2/kadence_query/query with an invalid X-WP-Nonce got 403 and the same request without it 200. New SEOProStack_Plugin_Fixes::rest_anonymous_nonce() (priority 101) answers that error, when nobody is logged in, as core answers a request without a nonce: wp_set_current_user(0) and true, without the no-cache header core added for the error. Logged-in users keep the 403.

= 1.3.207 =
* Faster: SEO Pro Stack's own front-end scripts and stylesheets load as minified copies (GitHub issue #1531). Release builds now carry a .min copy beside each file in assets/ (scripts/build-release.sh, from WP Plugin Starter 1.0.62), starting with its file's licence and Provenance lines, and SEOProStack::asset_url() returns it unless SCRIPT_DEBUG is on, so a Git checkout still loads the readable files. Post reactions, Language switcher, Email protection, Modal links, Dark mode image contrast, Wikipedia previews and Fluent Forms by key's panel and modal load through it: the 11 files go from 138 KB to 79 KB (post-reactions.js 53 to 30 KB, dark-image-contrast.js 34 to 14 KB). PageSpeed Insights listed post-reactions.js under Minify JavaScript on a directory home page. The two small stylesheets WordPress prints in the page (post reactions, the form panel) print their .min copy too.
* Developers: core files synced with WP Plugin Starter 1.0.62 and its unreleased GH#715 and GH#725: the release build's minified copies and the preflight check for them, Node set up for them in CI and the Release workflow, scripts/lint.sh changelog and hooks run by CI, the deploy log classifier and load check retry fixes, and SEOProStack::asset_url() documented as returning a non-empty string (wp_register_script() needs one for PHPStan). The starter's scripts/check-hooks.js (scripts/lint.sh hooks, from this plugin's check) replaces scripts/check-hooks.mjs and .github/workflows/hooks.yml; it also checks hook names built at run time and the latest release's supported hooks.

= 1.3.206 =
* Faster: Image loading's Kadence rows script no longer makes the browser lay out the page while it is still being read (GitHub issue #1567). Each waiting row's or column's one-line script called check(), which read getBoundingClientRect() at once, so the browser laid out everything parsed so far there and then; PageSpeed Insights (mobile) on one Kadence home page listed it as the largest attributed Forced reflow item, 87 ms, for a row near the end of the page, nowhere near the first screen. With ResizeObserver, check() now hands the row or column to a one-shot observer whose callback runs after the browser's own style and layout pass and before it paints: there it is measured, and one within 1,250 pixels of the window is shown (sps-near) in that same frame, or else handed to the IntersectionObserver as before; without ResizeObserver, as before. size() is split into pick() (reads) and put() (writes), and every observer callback and the DPR change now read all reported rows before writing any, so one row's new picture does not make the browser redo styles to measure the next. Through a local proxy swapping the script on two live Kadence home pages (phone 412×823 at 1.75, desktop 1350×940): the same copies fetched at the same points, a column 2,136 pixels down on desktop shown at load just after the first paint (17 ms after it live, 30 ms with the change), rows further down fetched only once scrolled near, and no page errors. Lighthouse on a phone (5 runs each, devtools throttling) on the other page: Total Blocking Time and the largest paint no worse (medians 47 and 1,808 ms against 50 and 1,854 ms).

= 1.3.205 =
* Fixed, on by default (Plugin fixes; GitHub issue #1528): Kadence sliders and carousels pass Lighthouse 13's Agentic Browsing check "Accessibility tree is well-formed". Splide gives each slide the role tabpanel (with dots) or group (without) when it mounts, and Kadence Blocks prints slides as <li> in <ul class="splide__list">, where neither role is allowed (axe aria-allowed-role), so every page with a Kadence Blocks Pro Advanced Slider, Advanced Gallery carousel or row background slider scored 2/3. New SEOProStack_Kadence_Slider_Markup (render_block_kadence/slider, kadence/advancedgallery and kadence/rowlayout, priority 40, front end only) prints the ul.splide__list and its direct li.splide__slide children as div with every attribute kept, as Kadence's and Splide's styles select by class; lists inside a slide are left alone, and markup whose ul and li do not nest is returned unchanged. Filter seoprostack_kadence_slider_markup.
* Faster: Dark mode image contrast loads its script only once the page is dark (GitHub issue #1564). assets/dark-image-contrast.js (34 KB, 9 KB with brotli) loaded deferred on every page with Kadence Pro's dark mode switcher, so every visitor in light mode, the default and the only mode PageSpeed Insights uses, fetched and parsed it, though check() and scan() measure nothing until the body has color-switch-dark; PageSpeed Insights listed it under Minify JavaScript (5 KiB of 11 KiB on one Kadence home page). SEOProStack_Dark_Image_Contrast::enqueue() now prints, through a src-less handle (seoprostack-dark-image-contrast), a footer script that sets window.seoprostackDarkImageContrast and adds the file as an async script at once when the body already has color-switch-dark, otherwise the first time it gains it (a switch, or Kadence Pro's saved choice, applied at DOMContentLoaded), 400 ms later, the wait the script itself gives colour transitions after a switch. The file's address passes through script_loader_src as before. Started on a dark page, the script measures as on a page that opened dark, and follows later switches itself.

= 1.3.204 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached no longer refills the whole site after every saved post (GitHub issue #1551). SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::litespeed_post_purged() started a whole-site fill (main pages, then LiteSpeed Cache's crawler or up to 1,000 pages) whenever LiteSpeed Cache's Pages purge rule (purge-post_p, on by default) was on, but that rule purges the PGS tag, set only where is_page(), so on a directory with thousands of listings each saved listing refilled every page for a purge that emptied the Pages, the post and its lists. Now only purge-post_all starts the whole fill; with Pages, new page_urls() queues the published Pages without a password, newest first, up to COVERED_MAX (50), once a request, with post_changed()'s post, home and posts page, a minute later. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3, default rules): wp post update queued /about/, /sample-page/, the home page and the post, not full (the password-protected Page left out); with purge-post_all on, full as before.
* Added: Cloudflare edge time, under Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages (Speed; 1 day by default, or 2 hours, 1 week, 30 days or none). While a token and zone are set and Cloudflare's last answer did not refuse them, anonymous GET and HEAD front-end pages send Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control: max-age=86400, stale-if-error=86400, so Cloudflare keeps them until a change empties them rather than for its default 2 hours; addresses with a query string get 5 minutes at most. Not sent when logged in, or for previews, 404s, search, feeds, password-protected posts, DONOTCACHEPAGE or a Cache-Control with no-store, no-cache or private, and taken off as the page ends when LiteSpeed Cache marks it not cacheable. Filter seoprostack_cloudflare_edge_time. Hosting needs now points to it instead of a short edge time (GitHub issue #1545).
* Faster, more private: Email protection now covers the Kadence theme's header, mobile header and footer social buttons (GitHub issue #1557). Kadence prints them with echo from the kadence_header_social, kadence_mobile_social and kadence_footer_social actions (callbacks at 10, theme 1.6.0) with no filter, so an Email button's mailto: address stayed readable in the HTML, and where Cloudflare's Email Address Obfuscation is on, Cloudflare rewrote it and added its own cdn-cgi email-decode.min.js, a parser-blocking script that PageSpeed's Network dependency tree insight lists as a critical chain. Home pages checked on 11 November 2026: four Kadence sites had two or three such links each (header and footer social), their only source of Cloudflare's decoder. New SEOProStack_Email_Protection::social_start() (priority 9) starts an output buffer and social_end() (priority 11) prints it through protect(), as content is; when another buffer is open above it, every buffer is left as it is and the buttons stay plain, so nothing is lost. Not in the Customizer preview, whose selective refresh redraws the buttons without the restoring script.
* New, on by default: Measure Kadence’s scrollbar without holding up the page (Speed; SEOProStack_Kadence_Scrollbar, kadence_scrollbar; GitHub issue #1552). The Kadence theme (1.6.0) prints, on wp_footer at priority 2 (Kadence\Base_Support\Component::action_add_scrollbar_offset_script()), a script that sets --scrollbar-offset from window.innerWidth minus the html element's clientWidth; reading clientWidth while the page is still being read makes the browser style and lay out the whole page early, which PageSpeed Insights lists under Forced reflow. On template_redirect, with the Kadence theme (or a child theme of it) and not on feeds, embeds or AMP pages, swap() replaces that callback with script(), which sets the same property from a ResizeObserver, after the browser's layout and before its first paint, and only when the width is not 0 or changes: Kadence's transparent headers read it with a fallback of 0, and full-width blocks read it through var(--global-vw, 100vw), so unset gives what 0 gives and phones need nothing set (a test site with Kadence 1.6.0 had full-width blocks the same size and place with it on and off, with a 15px desktop scrollbar and on a phone). It also follows window and page size changes. Without ResizeObserver it measures at once, as Kadence did. Lighthouse mobile, 5 runs each through a local proxy on a Kadence home page: forced reflow 237 ms to 115 ms (Kadence's share 123 ms to 0, no other script's share rising), Style & Layout 787 ms to 760 ms, TBT and LCP unchanged within noise; setting the property even when 0 cost a second style pass (Style & Layout 803 ms, LCP +38 ms), so 0 is left unset. Speed plan row with the Kadence theme. New filter seoprostack_kadence_scrollbar (true). Changing the setting empties the page cache.

= 1.3.203 =
* New: the Website Setup board's starter data names every account and key a site needs, and has a task for each (GitHub issue #1554). A review of 23 directory sites' boards found the accounts spread over separate tasks with no list of them, Google Cloud Translation (TranslatePress's machine translation, on and with a key on those sites) and Cloudflare Turnstile (network-active there) with no task at all, and Search Console as one sentence in the Google Cloud task; and nothing said that a site copied from a template or demo keeps that site's keys, which bill its owner. New tasks: "Accounts and Keys Checklist", at the top of the stage: each account (hosting, domain and Cloudflare, business mailboxes such as support@, Amazon SES, Google Cloud, Search Console, payments, and other plugins' own tasks), opened in the business's name with two-factor authentication, and the places a copied site's keys are kept (FluentSMTP, LiteSpeed Cache's Cloudflare token, Turnstile, TranslatePress, the payment plugin, wp-config.php), to be replaced with the business's own. "Setup Spam Protection (Cloudflare Turnstile)", after the Cloudflare cache task, only where Simple Cloudflare Turnstile is active: creating the widget, the plugin's test response (it adds Turnstile to no form until that succeeds) and the forms to protect. "Setup Google Search Console", after Google Cloud: a Domain property verified by a DNS record in Cloudflare, the sitemap, and team access. "Setup Google Translation (Cloud Translation API)", after Search Console, only where TranslatePress is active: enabling the API in the site's Google Cloud project, a key restricted to that API and to the referrer TranslatePress shows, TranslatePress's Automatic Translation settings with a daily character limit, and a test. The Google Cloud task names Cloud Translation, and the Amazon SES task says to delete a copied site's FluentSMTP connections once the business's own works. `wp seoprostack starters add fluent-boards` adds the new tasks to an existing board; other content on it is not changed. The README's setup steps mark the tasks added only with a plugin (scripts/readme-setup-tasks.js).
* Fixed: a Customizer save, a widget or widget area save, or a change to the site title, tagline, site icon, the theme's Customizer settings or Kadence's global palette now empties the page cache (GitHub issue #1546). Neither LiteSpeed Cache nor WP-Optimize purges after these: on a LiteSpeed host its customize_save_after callbacks held no purge, and its Purge All hooks (purge-hook_all) covered theme switches, menus, terms and links only, so a new logo, header or palette stayed off cached pages for LiteSpeed's public TTL (7 days there) and off Cloudflare's copies too. SEOProStack_Page_Cache now purges every page through purge_all() on customize_save_after and when updated_option or added_option reports a changed blogname, blogdescription, site_icon, sidebars_widgets, widget_*, theme_mods_<stylesheet> or kadence_global_palette, once a request, and only from wp-admin, the REST API or WP-CLI (not WP-Cron, a front-end page writing an option, or an import). Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages and Keep pages cached follow LiteSpeed Cache's litespeed_purged_all as after Purge All. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3), counting litespeed_purged_all from WP-CLI: one purge each for a new tagline, a theme mod, a widget option, the palette and customize_save_after, one for a title and tagline changed together, none for the same tagline saved again, another option, or a change from WP-Cron.
* Faster: a saved post empties only its own pages at Cloudflare, and saves purge LiteSpeed's copies fresh, not stale (GitHub issue #1547). SEOProStack_Cloudflare_Purge::litespeed_purged_post() emptied the whole site at Cloudflare whenever LiteSpeed Cache's Pages purge rule (purge-post_p, on by default) was on, but that rule purges the PGS tag, which LiteSpeed Cache sets only where is_page() (src/tag.cls.php, 7.9), so on a directory with 10,000 listings every saved listing emptied 10,000 Cloudflare copies for 33 Pages. Now only purge-post_all empties the site; post_changed() sends what new post_pages() lists from LiteSpeed's rules (RULES, without Pages, where LiteSpeed Cache is not the page cache): the post's permalink (once unpublished, the one it had, with _wp_desired_post_slug for a trashed post), the front and home page, the posts page for posts, each published Page, its viewable term archives, post type archive, author archive and year, month and day archives, as files, plus each list's pagination (<list>/page/) as prefixes, never a list's whole folder, as a listing's own address can sit inside one. New post_moved() on post_updated adds a published post's old permalink when it changes. New post_saved() on wp_after_insert_post lists post_pages() again once the block editor's REST save has set the terms (WP_REST_Posts_Controller::handle_terms() runs after transition_post_status), so a post moved to another category also empties the new category's archive, and the new address of a %category% permalink. LiteSpeed's Recent Posts widget rule (purge-post_pwrp) is not followed: the pages showing the widget cannot be listed. New filter seoprostack_cloudflare_purge_post_urls ($files, $post). New prefixes() keeps folders, without the scheme, in the same per-site queue as addresses; targets() sends them as prefixes. MAX_URLS rises from 30 to 90 (three requests of MAX_ITEMS); more becomes a whole-site purge. Serve Stale: with LiteSpeed Cache's purge-stale on, every purge of a request is sent stale (\LiteSpeed\Control::init(), after_setup_theme), and a server serving the stale copy while it rebuilds could give the old page to Cloudflare for its whole edge time. New SEOProStack_Litespeed::fresh_after_saves() on litespeed_conf_load_option_purge-stale returns false where new saves_content() finds a save, from the request alone: post.php editpost, trash, untrash and delete; edit.php bulk edit, trash, untrash and delete (action or action2); admin-ajax.php POST inline-save, customize_save, save-widget, widgets-order, trash-post, untrash-post, delete-post and delete-page; POST to nav-menus.php and widgets.php; REST POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE under /wp/v2/ or /batch/v1 (pretty or ?rest_route=). Updates, Purge All and WP-CLI keep Serve Stale. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3, Serve Stale on): a block editor save (REST POST /wp/v2/posts/7, application password) answered X-LiteSpeed-Purge: public,fc6_Po.7,… without stale, where the same save from WP-CLI queued public,stale,…; 16 requests checked with \LiteSpeed\Control::is_stale() gave 0 for every save listed above and 1 for the front end, REST reads, other REST routes, heartbeat, GET admin-ajax, opening the editor and the posts list. With LiteSpeed's default rules (f, h, p, pwrp, a, t, pt, m) a post update queued its page, the home page, its category, tag and month archives and four Pages as files and four /page/ prefixes, sent (Cloudflare stubbed) as one files and one prefixes request; a slug change added the old address; a trashed post queued /probe-post/, not its __trashed address; a draft queued nothing; All pages on, or 98 published Pages, queued the whole site; without LiteSpeed as the page cache, RULES gave the page, home, category and tag.

= 1.3.202 =
* Fixed, on by default: Fluent Forms fields with no label now have an accessible name (SEOProStack_Form_Field_Names; GitHub issue #1529). Fluent Forms writes aria-labelledby="label_<field>" on every input, select and textarea but prints that label only when the field has one, so a form that uses placeholders left screen readers with an unnamed field (PageSpeed "Select elements do not have associated label elements"). On fluentform/rendering_field_html_* for select, text, email, number, url, password, date, phone and textarea fields, the dangling reference is replaced by aria-label from the placeholder, the admin label or the field name. Fields with a label are unchanged.
* Faster: pages showing pictures that waited for their 480 pixel copy alone now list it within minutes, not at their next cache expiry (GitHub issue #1542). 1.3.201 made the copy without purging the page, so a cached page kept offering the 768 copy for up to a week (LiteSpeed's public TTL), and a picture seen while the 50-picture queue was full was not looked at again until the page was rendered again, which a cached page is not. SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::look() now remembers, through new SEOProStack_Picture_Pages::note(), the singular post a page shows when it queues a picture for SMALL alone, even when the queue was full, in the seoprostack_picture_pages option (at most 20, the first seen; not autoloaded); asked before the main query, as when a plugin shows the picture on init, note() waits for the wp action, as the picture is looked at once a request. When a run() made anything and no picture waits for SMALL alone any longer, those posts are taken (SEOProStack_Picture_Pages::take(), which empties the option) and purged and opened again with the pictures' own pages: the refreshed pages list the new copies and queue any picture still lacking it, so a full queue drains page by page. Archives, the home page as a list of posts and other non-singular pages are not remembered; they list the copy at their next refresh, as before. A run now makes SMALL alone for up to 9 pictures (SMALL_BATCH), or every size for up to 3 (BATCH), each counting 3; the 20-second budget for starting a picture is unchanged. On one site, _seoprostack_small_tried times showed three pictures' 480 copies, with their WebP and AVIF copies, made in 1 to 2 seconds in all, and server cron runs a batch about every 5 minutes, so a 44-picture page took over an hour at 3 a run. Uninstall removes seoprostack_picture_pages.

= 1.3.201 =
* Faster: Image loading gives pictures a 480 pixel wide copy (GitHub issue #1535). WordPress makes uncropped copies 300 (medium) and 768 (medium_large) pixels wide and none between, and the browser takes the smallest srcset file at least as wide as the device pixels it paints, so a picture shown 301 to 480 device pixels wide took the 768 copy, sizes="auto" included. PageSpeed Insights (mobile, 412 px at DPR 1.75) on one Kadence site's home page: six grid pictures painted at 217 CSS pixels (380 device pixels) were fetched at 768w, 18 to 54 KiB each more than needed, 211 KiB in all ("Improve image delivery"); 41 KiB on another; a directory site whose theme registers 400 and 600 pixel sizes was not affected. With Image loading on, SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::register() adds seoprostack-480 (480 wide, any height, not cropped; not in image_size_names_choose) on every request, admin included, so uploads get it. Older pictures: on the front end, a wp_calculate_image_srcset filter passes each picture shown with a srcset to new SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::want_small() (want() keeps its arguments and queues every size); when missing() finds seoprostack-480 registered, absent and smaller than the picture, and _seoprostack_small_tried is older than a week, the picture joins the seoprostack_picture_sizes queue with the value -1 (SMALL_ONLY): run() makes that size alone (only_these() keeps the sizes asked for), marks _seoprostack_small_tried, lets WebP and AVIF images make its copies (convert_now()) and purges no page, so pages stay cached and list the copy at their next refresh. A hero or slide picture (SEOProStack_Hero_Picture) that lacks only seoprostack-480 is queued the same way; one that lacks other sizes too gets them all and its page refreshed as before (#1406). Pictures a page needs every size of run first, and when the queue (50) is full the last picture waiting for the 480 copy alone gives way to one. Watermarked pictures and pictures 480 pixels wide or less get none. Uninstall deletes _seoprostack_small_tried. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, ImageMagick 7.1.2): three uploads (1667×2500, 2560×1344, 2000×1000) got 480x720, 480x252 and 480x240 copies with their WebP and AVIF copies; with the 480 copy removed from one picture's files and metadata, one visit to a page showing it queued it as -1, one run made evergreen-b-480x252.jpg (16,254 bytes; WebP 14,110, AVIF 12,209) and emptied the queue, and the page's srcset then listed 480w; a hero call for a picture lacking only the 480 copy queued -1, one lacking 1536x1536 too queued 0.
* Faster: WebP and AVIF images keeps a file's AVIF copy only when it is smaller than its WebP copy (GitHub issue #1536). The rewrite rules (and Copies at their own addresses) send the AVIF copy whenever there is one, so an AVIF copy larger than the WebP copy cost browsers that accept AVIF bytes for nothing. A read-only count of _seoprostack_nextgen records on 11 November 2026 found 2,200 of 34,540 files on one site (5.2 MB more than their WebP copies), 55 of 842 and 25 of 808 on two others. convert_attachment() now makes each file's WebP copy first and passes its bytes to convert_file(), which keeps the AVIF copy only when it is smaller than both the original and the WebP copy (adopt_compressx_copy() too), and removes an up-to-date AVIF copy that is not, recording 0 as for a copy not smaller than its original. When making the WebP copy fails, a WebP copy still there is the limit (new webp_bytes()), as it is still sent. An AVIF copy recorded 0 is tried again when the file's WebP result differs from the one recorded (made again at another quality, or WebP copies switched off). Existing copies are checked at their picture's next conversion (a quality change, new sizes, or the Make WebP and AVIF copies bulk action); no bulk job. With Copies at their own addresses (names_copies()), a copy already at its address is held to the original only: pages cached before the conversion may name it, nothing else answers there, and a <source> whose file fails is not replaced by the <img> (the 480 pixel job, #1535, converts the whole picture and purges no page); new copies follow the rule, and source() lists, in the AVIF <source>, each width's WebP copy where it has no AVIF copy, rather than the original JPEG or PNG. New remove_copy() checks that a removed copy is gone (a wp_delete_file filter returning '' keeps it, and wp_delete_file() returns nothing before WordPress 6.7); one that stays is recorded -1, failed, and tried again. The AVIF quality description now gives what was measured with this encoder path (speed 7, Quality by picture size on) on 47 picture sizes from five of the owner's sites, scored with SSIMULACRA 2 and Butteraugli against the source: AVIF 80 about as sharp as WebP 90 (median 0.6 points sharper, 0.95 of its bytes, larger on 10 of 47); AVIF 70 a little softer (median 2.4 points, 0.76 of its bytes). It said "AVIF at 70 looks about the same as WebP at 90". Defaults are unchanged. Test site (as above, whose AVIF encoder makes larger files than the hosts counted): of 23 files, the 9 from 800 pixels up, where Quality by picture size gives WebP 80 and AVIF 60 or less, kept no AVIF copy as it was not smaller than the WebP copy (joinery-a-683x1024.jpg: WebP 33,238 bytes, AVIF 39,424 without the limit; evergreen-b.jpg: 40,924 and 44,393); a request with Accept: image/avif,image/webp got image/webp, 33,238 bytes, and another size image/avif, 30,519 bytes. An up-to-date 39,424-byte AVIF copy recorded before was removed at the next conversion; with WebP switched off it was made again, and removed again when WebP was switched back on. With Copies at their own addresses, a 39,424-byte AVIF copy of joinery-a-683x1024.jpg, up to date, stayed (recorded 39,424) when the 480 pixel job made joinery-a-480x720.jpg (WebP 35,984, AVIF 30,519), and was removed once the site went back to copies at the same address; the picture's AVIF <source> listed the 683, 200 and 480 AVIF copies and the WebP copies of the 768, 1024, 1366 and full sizes. With a wp_delete_file filter returning '', the copy stayed and was recorded -1; the next conversion removed it and recorded 0. A WebP result differing from the record made one AVIF encode; an unchanged one, none.

= 1.3.200 =
* New: the Website Setup board's starter data has a task, "Setup Backups, Updates and Uptime Alerts", after "Setup Payment Processing". The board covered the accounts a site needs but not how it is kept running: an audit of 23 directory sites' boards found no task for backups, for who installs updates, or for knowing when the site is down. The task, with Hostinger's own guides linked, says to check that recent backups are listed (daily for a site that takes orders, bookings or messages), to generate a backup before any big change (entry-level plans need an upgrade for this; until then, download the latest automatic backup), to download one backup once and check it holds the files and the database, never testing Restore on the live site, to choose one updater on hPanel's WordPress Security page (Smart auto-updates, which turns WordPress's own off, or Native auto-updates) so two never compete, to install waiting updates once a month after a backup and check the home page, a form and wp-admin, and to create an hPanel Monitoring alert sent to the support mailbox. `wp seoprostack starters add fluent-boards` adds it to an existing board after the payments task when that task is in the same stage and not archived, otherwise at the top of its stage. Other content on existing boards is not changed.

= 1.3.199 =
* New, on by default: Short not-found answer for missing files (Server tab; GitHub issue #1525). A request for a file that is not there, such as /llms.txt, a removed picture or a bot's .php or .env probe, got the theme's whole not-found page. On two large directory sites that took 6.0 to 9.0 s when the page cache did not have it (parse and main query 0.66 s; the theme's 404 template 2.77 s and 91 of 118 queries), and PageSpeed Insights' Agentic Browsing check gives /llms.txt 2 s (Lighthouse's fetchResource timeout) before it fails with "Timed out fetching resource"; any other 4xx answer passes. SEOProStack_Quick_404 runs last at template_include: when is_404() and the status is 404 or 410 (410 Gone for removed pages), and the path's last part has a file extension other than .htm, .html, .shtml or .xhtml and does not end in /, it answers "Not Found" (or "Gone") as text/plain with X-Robots-Tag: noindex and exits. Plugins that serve a file themselves (Rank Math's llms.txt and sitemaps, WordPress's robots.txt, favicon.ico and sitemaps) never reach a 404. Rank Math's 404 Monitor records from get_header (wp_head on block themes), which the short answer skips, so its capture_404 callback on either hook runs first. Filter seoprostack_quick_404 (bool, path).
* Faster: Search custom fields tests a post's type and status before its fields, so counting a common word's results no longer runs the field test for every attachment and revision (GitHub issue #1523). WordPress adds the search clause to WHERE before the post type and status tests, and the database tests AND parts in that order, so every row that failed the title, excerpt and content tests ran the correlated postmeta subquery, including the attachments and revisions the type and status tests then dropped. The page query stops after one page of results; the count for page numbers (SQL_CALC_FOUND_ROWS, or Faster page counts on long lists) reads the whole list. On a directory site with 106,927 posts (45,028 attachments, 28,236 revisions; MariaDB 11.8) the count took 1.6 to 3.3 s for /?s=service and 4.4 to 8.3 s for /?s=service -plumber, and 0.21 s for the first without the field test. SEOProStack_Field_Search now keeps the search clause it rebuilt, as the last posts_search filter leaves it (another plugin may wrap it), and posts_where_paged (PHP_INT_MAX) moves it to the end of WHERE when it appears there once, as a top-level AND part, and WHERE joins its top-level parts with AND alone: no top-level OR, XOR, ||, BETWEEN or CASE, no comment or semicolon, and balanced brackets and quotes; otherwise WHERE is left as it is. AND parts give the same rows in any order. Measured on that site with the whole main query and its count (fewest of 4 runs): service 1.80 s before, 0.38 s after, with 13,655 results; service -plumber 2.23 to 3.29 s before, 0.41 to 0.48 s after, with 13,257 results; rarer words (roof repair, plumber; 405 and 592 results) and a smaller site (guttering, 126 results) unchanged at 0.08 to 0.18 s. The same results and the same first page in every case.

= 1.3.198 =
* Faster: Fixes for other plugins has the web server answer Tutor LMS Pro's push notification worker, /tutor-push-notification.js, instead of WordPress (GitHub issue #1470). Tutor LMS Pro's Notifications add-on (4.1.1, 4.2.0) registers that service worker on every page (register.js: home_url + "/tutor-push-notification.js") and answers it from PHP on init (Push_Notification::load_service_worker() reads a fixed 2,148-byte file and exits); browsers check a worker's script again on navigations past the HTTP cache, and page caches do not keep it, so each check started WordPress and every plugin. SEOProStack_Tutor_Push_Worker, from the main site's admin_init, writes the file to get_home_path() (temporary file renamed into place), starting with a marker comment: Tutor's own file while the add-on is on (on a network, only when every site with Tutor LMS Pro active has it on, as they share the folder; up to 1,000 sites), and once it is off, a worker that calls skipWaiting() and registration.unregister(), so browsers that have Tutor's stop asking. The removing worker is written only when browsers are known to have Tutor's: the file was Tutor's before, or a check reached WordPress while the add-on was off, which init priority 1 (before Tutor's 10) answers with the removing worker at once, no-cache. Add-on state is read from the stored tutor_addons_config and active plugin list, so a request on which Load plugins only where needed skips Tutor LMS Pro does not count as off. A file without the marker is never changed or deleted. The site option seoprostack_tutor_push_worker records the file's mode and a stamp (switch, the modified time of Tutor's worker file, add-on; not TUTOR_PRO_VERSION, which a request without Tutor LMS Pro loaded lacks), so admin screens read one option when nothing changed; it is checked again when tutor_addons_config is saved, after plugin updates and daily. Switching Fixes for other plugins off, or deactivating SEO Pro Stack (on a network, from the main site or network-wide), removes the file. Hosting needs says when the root folder cannot be written (copy Tutor's file there), when a network has the add-on on for some sites only, and suggests turning the add-on off on a site with no published courses. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Tutor LMS 4.2.1, Tutor LMS Pro 4.1.1): before, the address answered with X-Powered-By: PHP; after an admin screen with the add-on on, Apache served the 2,265-byte file with Last-Modified and ETag and no PHP; with the add-on off, an uncached request got the 155-byte removing worker from PHP (no-cache) and the next admin screen wrote the 272-byte file, served by Apache; a file without the marker stayed as it was (note theirs); deactivating removed the file and Tutor's PHP answer returned; no PHP notices in debug.log.
* Fixed: translated count strings keep the number first, as the English does (GitHub issue #1459). About 730 plural entries in the bundled catalogues (pl_PL, ro_RO, fr_FR, fr_BE, fr_CA, es_ES, es_MX, pt_PT, pt_BR, it_IT, de_DE, de_AT, de_CH, nl_NL, nl_BE, lv, et) moved the count behind the noun, which changes the meaning: French "Table %d convertie." reads "table no. N converted", and Romanian "pluginul %d" "plugin N". They now read "%d table convertie.", "%d tabela została przekonwertowana." and so on, with the right noun and verb form for each plural index (Polish and Romanian use their three forms, Romanian adding "de" from 20). Placeholders and plural-form counts are unchanged. Entries whose English does not start with the count (a name, such as "%s theme", or a time, such as "%s ago") are unchanged. Translations only.
* New: the Website Setup board's starter data has a task, "Clear Cloudflare's cache with LiteSpeed Cache", after "Setup Cloudflare (domain DNS and security)" (GitHub issue #1463). It tells the owner, or their AI assistant, to make a Cloudflare token with Zone → Cache Purge → Purge and Zone → Zone → Read for the site's zone, enter it under LiteSpeed Cache → CDN → Cloudflare with Clear Cloudflare cache on, and test with Purge All (`wp litespeed-purge all`, or `wp litespeed-purge blog <id> --url=<site address>` on a network), so every Purge All also empties Cloudflare's copy of the pages. Where other sites share the domain, the task says to leave Clear Cloudflare cache off and turn on Speed → Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages instead, which empties only this site's address and can use the same token. Without Zone Read, LiteSpeed Cache cannot look the zone up and clears the saved one on its next settings save, so the README's `litespeed_cloudflare` preset line now names both permissions. `wp seoprostack starters add fluent-boards` adds the task to an existing board after the Cloudflare task, or at the top of the first stage if that task is archived or in another stage. Other content on existing boards is not changed.
* Faster: Image loading gives pictures in a Kadence Blocks row's column a sizes for the column's width on a computer, so pictures that load straight away are no longer fetched for the whole window (GitHub issue #1517). WordPress's sizes="(max-width: Wpx) 100vw, Wpx" made a picture in a narrow column fetch the copy for the window: on one directory home page (PageSpeed, 1.3.195) the main picture, with fetchpriority="high" in a two-column left-golden row's narrow column, came as its 1024 copy where it showed 406 px wide (205 KiB more), and on four other Kadence home pages the first pictures of columns 56 to 264 KiB more; lazy pictures already had sizes="auto" (#1485). New SEOProStack_Kadence_Column_Sizes (includes/class-seoprostack-kadence-column-sizes.php, loaded by Image loading with Kadence Blocks, front end only). render_block_data gives each column of a kadence/rowlayout with kbVersion 2 its share of the window on a computer, from columns, colLayout and the custom column widths as Kadence Blocks' get_template_columns() and get_custom_layout() lay them out (gaps and padding left out), times the share of any column the row is in, and the most pixels from the row's maximum width in px (with inheritMaxWidth, the Kadence theme's content width when in px, else the theme's $content_width); inner blocks take the share of the block they are in, except galleries, covers, media and text blocks, and wide and full-width blocks. render_block_core/image and render_block_kadence/image (40) mark the picture inside the_content on front-end pages, with its crop box when an Image block has an aspect ratio with cover or a Kadence Blocks Image block a ratio, so the picture counts as wide as it paints; wp_content_img_tag (15, after core's srcset and auto sizes, before content_image()) removes the mark and, only when sizes is WordPress's own (max-width: Wpx) 100vw, Wpx (after auto, when present) and the picture has srcset, puts entries for windows from 1,025 px (Kadence Blocks' computer layout) before it: the most pixels from the window where the share reaches them, and the share in vw (rounded up) below, such as (min-width: 1290px) 430px, (min-width: 1025px) 33.34vw for a third of a 1,290 px row; changes saving under 16 px at 1,025 px are left out. Left as before: old row markup, breakoutLeft or breakoutRight, rows with a column hidden on computers (vsdesk), shown only to some visitors or with Block visibility, layouts Kadence does not name, a filtered kadence_tablet_media_query, and other blocks' pictures. Filter seoprostack_kadence_column_sizes (bool, default true; the post shown). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence, Kadence Blocks, content width 1290 px), Chromium with a fresh cache per page: a 1600×1067 picture in a left-golden row's narrow column took its 768 copy at 1,350 px (shown 366 px; 1024 before) and its 1024 copy at DPR 2; a quarter (nested half of a half) its 300 copy at 1,100 px (shown 211 px); a square crop in a half column and a Kadence Blocks Image with a 4:3 ratio copies at least as wide as they paint; at 412 px (DPR 1.75) every picture its 768 copy, as before; a one-column row, a column hidden on computers and a wide Image block kept WordPress's sizes; a lazy picture kept auto first; no PHP messages or console errors.
* Faster: Search custom fields no longer lets a common search word read the whole postmeta table (GitHub issue #1513). SEOProStack_Field_Search::search() looked in field values with a correlated EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM wp_postmeta AS sps_v … WHERE sps_v.post_id = wp_posts.ID …), which MariaDB (exists_to_in) and MySQL 8.0.16 or later may turn into a semi-join; with many candidate posts the optimizer filled it first, reading every postmeta row with a LIKE on each value. On a directory site with 3,987,653 postmeta rows (MariaDB 11.8), /?s=service spent 3.90 s in the main query (EXPLAIN: MATERIALIZED sps_v type=ALL); the same query without the field test took 0.14 s, and the page took 8 to 14 s, as search results are never cached. Adding LIMIT 1 inside the EXISTS changed nothing (4.14 s). The field test is now a scalar subquery with LIMIT 1, compared with IS NOT NULL (or IS NULL for a word excluded with -word, instead of NOT EXISTS), which is never made a semi-join and always runs per candidate post through the post_id index (EXPLAIN: DEPENDENT SUBQUERY sps_v type=ref): 0.14 s with the same 12 posts in the same order. Rarer words, which already ran per post, keep the same plan. Same matches, placeholders and values; MySQL 5.7 and later and MariaDB.
* Fixed: an API token field left empty keeps the saved token (GitHub issue #1456). Speed → Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages shows its token as an empty password field; when the field changed and ended empty (a browser filled it, then it was cleared), the empty value was saved and the token removed. Only a space removes it now, as the field's text says. Core files synced with WP Plugin Starter 1.0.57: the release-bump.sh and check-deploy-log.sh scripts, release coordination in RELEASING.md, and scripts/deploy-sites.sh purging only each updated install's host names on Cloudflare (other sites on the same zone keep their cached pages) and retrying GitHub release reads that are rate limited. The hooks list check (scripts/check-hooks.mjs) moves from ci.yml, a core file, to this plugin's own .github/workflows/hooks.yml.
* Improved: wp seoprostack watermark-images has a time box, and a picture that waits for a media batch no longer pins the --stale or --marked-only cursor (GitHub issue #1520). A host that ends long processes had to wrap the command in timeout; a run killed mid-picture never reached the finally that releases the media batch lease (seoprostack_storage_lock, 300 s), so the next run's first pictures each waited lease_wait()'s 60 s and failed with watermark_busy, and the first failure stopped the cursor moving for the rest of the run. On a 24,000-attachment site in 10-minute runs that cost about 4 minutes in every 10 and left the cursor unchanged for hours. New --minutes=<n> (with --marked-only or --stale) stops between pictures once the time is up, never during one, saves the cursor at the next picture and exits 0 with "Paused at the time budget". A watermark_busy failure is no longer a failure that pins the cursor: the picture is tried again once at the end of the run, and in --marked-only and --stale runs the cursor option keeps it in a retry list (at most 100) that the next run tries first, so it survives a killed run too. Pictures still waiting at the end count as failed and are listed. Other failures pin the cursor as before. Tested on a disposable site with a lease left as a killed run leaves it: the first run paused with the waiting picture kept, the next started at once and marked all 12; a run without --minutes marked the waiting picture at the end, with none failed.
* Developers: docs/HOOKS.md lists every seoprostack_* filter and action for site code, each supported or internal, with the rule that a supported hook stays, or keeps working through apply_filters_deprecated() or do_action_deprecated(), for at least two minor releases (GitHub issue #1489). scripts/check-hooks.mjs fails when the list and the code disagree, or when a hook listed as supported at the latest release tag is gone without that period; it runs in .github/workflows/hooks.yml and before a release.

= 1.3.197 =
* Fixed: Watermark pictures' wp seoprostack watermark-images --dry-run, when the watermark picture's file is only in cloud storage (GitHub issue #1453, PR #1509). SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::mark(false) does not fetch the file back on a dry run, which changes nothing, so it returned watermark_missing and the command said the file was missing; on a dry run, watermark_missing now stops with "The watermark picture's file is in cloud storage, so a dry run cannot compare pictures with it. A real run fetches it first." (also when the file is gone from cloud storage too; a real run reports that). Other errors from mark() are reported as before. Shipped in 1.3.197; this line was added after the release.
* Improved: Media in cloud storage's forget-previous resumes where a paused verification stopped (GitHub issue #1499, PR #1510). A cursor option (seoprostack_storage_job_cleanup_cursor, keyed by the current and previous destination signatures and the check mode) keeps the attachment where a lease ran out; the next run starts there, then makes one confirming walk from the newest record, which costs only database reads for attachments whose checkpoints are unchanged. New --verify=size checks only that each current object exists with its recorded size (HEAD, no download or SHA-256), for operators who compared the buckets themselves, such as with rclone check; --verify=hash stays the default and any other value is refused. The mode is part of each attachment's checkpoint fingerprint, so a size-only checkpoint never counts for a hashed run. Once the previous destination is forgotten, the per-attachment verify_ checkpoints and the cursor are deleted.
* New, on by default: Robots.txt rules (Links; SEOProStack_Robots_Txt, setting robots_txt; GitHub issue #1506). Spam links to made-up searches (/?s=…) got search result pages indexed on sites of the owner's, which then each needed a robots.txt file written by hand on the server; such a file answers instead of WordPress, so the SEO plugin's rules and later changes stopped reaching it. A robots_txt filter at priority 100000, after Rank Math's editor (10, which replaces WordPress's answer and drops WooCommerce's lines and its own sitemap line) and Yoast SEO (99999, which rebuilds it), adds to each group of rules only the lines it is missing: Disallow for the admin path with Allow for admin-ajax.php, Disallow /*?s=, /*&s= and /<search base>/ (with Allow /<search base>/$ when a page has that address), and with WooCommerce its wc-logs, woocommerce_transient_files, woocommerce_uploads and add-to-cart lines; then a Sitemap line when there is none, from SEOProStack_Litespeed::sitemap_index() (now public: Rank Math's, Yoast's or WordPress's own index). A path a group already names is left to its line, a group that disallows / is left as it is, other lines are never removed or changed, and paths start at the root of the domain for a site in a folder. Child settings: robots_txt_search (on) also sends noindex, follow for search results in wp_robots and an X-Robots-Tag header (template_redirect 0, so feeds too); robots_txt_private (on) sends X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow on every response WordPress sends while blog_public is 0, and robots.txt never disallows / for such a site, since a page crawlers may not fetch cannot show its noindex; robots_txt_ai_training (off) adds one group disallowing / for GPTBot, ClaudeBot, Google-Extended, Applebot-Extended, CCBot, meta-externalagent and Bytespider, leaving out any a group already names (the site's own rules included), and never for search and answer crawlers. The setting's panel holds the site's own rules (seoprostack_robots_txt_rules, not autoloaded, up to 32 KB of field and comment lines without tags or control characters), added at the end, and when a robots.txt file is in the site's folder, says so, shows it and offers Use these rules instead of the file: its lines, without those the feature adds and its sitemap, join the own rules and the file is renamed robots.txt-<date>.bak. Both forms save in place (seoprostack-tabs.js) or through admin-post.php without JavaScript, checking the nonce and SEOProStack_Settings::can_change(). A change drops LiteSpeed Cache's copy of /robots.txt (litespeed_purge_url) and, with Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages, Cloudflare's; a change to blog_public, or to the header setting while it is 0, empties the page cache. Filters seoprostack_robots_txt_rules, seoprostack_robots_txt_ai_crawlers and seoprostack_robots_txt_sitemap. Uninstall deletes the own rules and keeps any renamed file. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, pretty permalinks): with no SEO plugin robots.txt gained the search lines and wp-sitemap.xml and /?s=test answered X-Robots-Tag: noindex, follow; with blog_public 0 pages, the feed and robots.txt answered one X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow and robots.txt had no Disallow: /; with Yoast SEO 28.8 its block kept its Disallow: line and sitemap_index.xml, with the admin and search lines added inside it and no second sitemap; with Rank Math 1.0.282 (editor: User-agent: * with Disallow: /private/, and a Bingbot group with Crawl-delay: 5) and WooCommerce 11.2.2, both groups kept their lines and got the admin, search and WooCommerce lines, and Rank Math's sitemap_index.xml was listed; with AI training crawlers on and an own GPTBot group allowing /blog/, the added group named the other six only; a robots.txt file answered instead of WordPress until the adopt handler (AJAX, as an administrator) kept its /old-stuff/ and AhrefsBot lines, dropped its admin, search and sitemap lines and renamed it, after which WordPress answered with them. Chromium on the Links tab: the panel named the file and showed it, Use these rules instead of the file and Save your rules both answered in place (the address unchanged), a line that was not a rule was left out with a message saying so, the rules were kept after a reload, and there were no console errors.

= 1.3.196 =
* Faster: Image loading lazy-loads archive and search thumbnails past the first pictures when a cache plugin turned WordPress's lazy loading off (GitHub issue #1504). LiteSpeed Cache's Lazy Load Images does (wp_lazy_loading_enabled false, src/media.cls.php), and its own lazy loader runs only on pages it caches (Media::finalize(): bypass when not Control::is_cacheable()), so on search results, which it never caches, and on every page for people who are logged in, featured images from wp_get_attachment_image() (Kadence's entry_loop_thumbnail.php, the_post_thumbnail context) all loaded with the page: on a directory stack's /?s=guttering, 11 thumbnails 768 px wide, the first with fetchpriority="high" and none with loading="lazy". content_image() already did this for content pictures (#1485). attachment_image() now gives loading="lazy" to a main-loop picture past the Load straight away budget that has no loading or fetchpriority and is not excluded, when wp_lazy_loading_enabled('img', <core's wp_get_attachment_image_context>) is false (the new core_lazy()), and prefixes its sizes with "auto, " as core's wp_get_attachment_image() does (wp_img_tag_add_auto_sizes filter, wp_sizes_attribute_includes_valid_auto(), WordPress 6.7 or later). As in content_image(), a main-loop picture with fetchpriority="auto" now takes no place in the budget. When WordPress's lazy loading is on, core decides, as before. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence; eight posts with a 1600×1067 featured image): with core's lazy loading on, /?s=guttering gave 3 thumbnails without loading (the first fetchpriority="high") and 5 with loading="lazy" and sizes="auto, …", before and after; with a must-use plugin returning false from wp_lazy_loading_enabled and Image loading off, all 8 had no loading; with Image loading on, the same 3 and 5 as with core's lazy loading on; with Load straight away at 1, 1 and 7; a single post's featured image kept fetchpriority="high" and no loading, and Kadence's related posts under it were lazy, as core makes them.
* Faster: SEO Pro Stack's own front-end scripts are deferred, and Like, save and share asks for its counts at low priority (GitHub issue #1502). post-reactions.js, modal-links.js, form-keys.js, dark-image-contrast.js, wikipedia-previews.js and seoprostack-link-clicks.js were plain footer scripts, which browsers fetch at medium priority alongside the page's pictures: on a directory home page PageSpeed Insights (mobile, 1.3.195) listed all four that page loads at about 520 ms each in the critical request chain, and the longest chain (2,630 ms) ended at the reactions' state GET (admin-ajax.php?action=seoprostack_reactions&do=state). Each is now registered with WordPress's defer strategy (array('strategy' => 'defer', 'in_footer' => true)), as the language switcher already was: each acts only on clicks, focus or pictures as they load, or after DOMContentLoaded, which deferred scripts run before, in order. Their settings are inline scripts printed before them, so WordPress keeps the strategy; form-keys.js still runs after Kadence Blocks Pro's modal script, which stays as Kadence loads it. WordPress before 6.3 reads the array as in the footer, as before. post-reactions.js's send() passes priority: 'low' to fetch() for state (the visitors' GET and the logged-in POST); like, save, list changes and merge keep the default. Browsers without fetch priority ignore it. email-protection.js is unchanged: it replaces visible text. Test site (WordPress latest, PHP 8.3; Like, save and share on posts, Wikipedia previews, Modal links and Dark image contrast on, a must-use plugin adding color-switch-dark and a dark background, a post with a Wikipedia link, a link to #sps-modal, a footer <dialog id="sps-modal"> and a black transparent PNG), Chromium with the DevTools protocol: the four scripts the post loads print with defer and data-wp-strategy="defer" and were fetched at Low priority; the state GET at Low and the like POST at High; the count showed 4 and Like made it 5 (aria-pressed true); hovering the Wikipedia link opened its preview; the black PNG was marked data-seoprostack-dark="invert"; the link got aria-haspopup="dialog" and a click opened the dialog; no console errors. form-keys.js (needs Kadence Blocks Pro) and seoprostack-link-clicks.js (link click counting, off) were not loaded there.
* Fixed: Redirect to the main address before WordPress loads, review follow-ups (GitHub issue #1474, PR #1496). On a network, the host names that must never be redirected are now read from get_sites() in pages of 1,000 (orderby id) until a page comes back short, instead of one call capped at 10,000 sites, so no site's own address is missed on a large network. The redirect target taken from WordPress's home address is refused when it has a query or a fragment, as it already was with a port, a path or a scheme other than http or https. SEOProStack_Hardening::files_write(), which both this feature and the security blocks use to write the site's .htaccess, now takes an exclusive flock() on its own lock file in the temporary folder (seoprostack-files-<sha256 of the path, 32 characters>.lock, not beside .htaccess in the web root) for the whole read, change and write, so two blocks written at once cannot overwrite each other; where no lock can be had, it writes as before.

= 1.3.195 =
* Faster: Like, save and share no longer starts WordPress a second time for each visitor's page view (GitHub issue #1490). The buttons are printed without counts so pages stay cacheable, and post-reactions.js then asked admin-ajax.php for them with a POST (do=state), which no page cache keeps: on a directory site on LiteSpeed (LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3) it took 0.77 to 1.0 s to first byte per view, three runs in a row, for public totals only ({"counts":…,"user":false}). On a page served to a visitor (no logged-in class on body, no nonce yet), the script now asks with a GET carrying the post IDs in numeric order and the page's language (document.documentElement.lang; the tallies are words, "4 Likes", which a translation plugin may give in the page's language, so each language is kept apart), so a page always asks for the same address. SEOProStack_Post_Reactions::ajax() sends a GET to state_get(), which answers state only (anything else: 405). For a visitor it sends Cache-Control: no-cache (browsers always ask, so they never show an older count than the server has) and, through LiteSpeed Cache's API, litespeed_control_set_ttl (CACHE_TTL, a day), litespeed_control_force_cacheable and litespeed_tag_add with sps_reactions_<post ID> for each post in the answer, as LiteSpeed Cache's own AJAX Cache TTL setting does for admin-ajax.php answers; a logged-in person's GET is answered as the POST is (lists and nonce, nocache_headers()), never kept. With LiteSpeed Cache active (LSCWP_V), a change to a post's _seoprostack_likes or _seoprostack_listed meta (added_post_meta, updated_post_meta, deleted_post_meta) sends litespeed_purge for that post's tag, so a like or a shortlist change drops the answers that hold it. Logged-in people still ask with a POST. Without LiteSpeed Cache nothing is kept and the GET runs as the POST did. The state answer is built by the new state_data(), shared by both. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0; three posts with the block, one with 4 likes), Chromium: as a visitor the post sent one GET (do=state&posts=5&lang=en-US, 200, Cache-Control: no-cache) and showed 4, and Like sent the POST as before and showed 5; logged in, state was a POST with the nonce and lists, and Like showed 6; no console errors. With a must-use stand-in for LiteSpeed Cache's API that echoed each call as a header: the visitor GET for posts 6,5,4 set the TTL to 86400, forced it cacheable and added sps_reactions_6, _5 and _4; a visitor's like on post 4 purged sps_reactions_4 only; a GET with do=like answered 405.
* Faster: Image loading no longer lets the site logo take the page's high fetch priority (GitHub issue #1485). WordPress gives fetchpriority="high" to the first picture of at least wp_min_priority_img_pixels (50,000 px²) once the header has started (wp_get_loading_optimization_attributes(), wp_maybe_add_fetchpriority_high_attr()), and a logo whose file is large took it: an SVG whose width and height say 1000×781, or a PNG uploaded at 680×250, though either shows about 50 px high. The page's main picture then had none: on 5 of 13 Kadence home pages and the regional home pages of a directory network checked on 10 November 2026 the logo had it, and where the desktop LCP was a content picture (4 of the 5, and the directory pages) PageSpeed Insights' LCP request discovery failed "fetchpriority=high should be applied". core_priority() (wp_get_loading_optimization_attributes, 20) now takes core's fetchpriority off an img with the custom-logo class outside the_content when the tag did not ask for it, and gives core's flag back (wp_high_priority_element_flag(true)), so the first large picture after it gets it; the logo still loads straight away (no loading="lazy", data-no-lazy="1"). Pages that open with a Kadence Blocks row background were not affected: kadence_preload() already clears the flag before the header (#1204).
* Faster: pictures Image loading makes lazy get "auto" in sizes, as core gives the pictures it lazy-loads itself (GitHub issue #1485). Core adds it only in wp_filter_content_tags() (wp_img_tag_add_auto_sizes(), before wp_content_img_tag), to tags that already have loading="lazy"; content_image() adds loading="lazy" later, to pictures below the opening block (#1412), and with LiteSpeed Cache's Lazy Load Images, which turns WordPress's lazy loading off (wp_lazy_loading_enabled false, src/media.cls.php), to every picture past the opening ones, so none of them got it. On one directory home page, 20 pictures in a grid shown 251 to 382 px wide on a desktop (Playwright, after scrolling) fetched their 768 px copies (21 to 110 KB each), while the 3 in the same grid whose sizes had auto fetched a copy about 360 px wide. content_image() now runs core's wp_img_tag_add_auto_sizes() on each picture it makes lazy (WordPress 6.7 or later, where that function exists; like core, it adds auto only to a picture that already has sizes), and also makes lazy the pictures past the Load straight away budget that core left without loading (when its lazy loading is off). As in core's wp_get_loading_optimization_attributes(), a picture with fetchpriority="low" (a later carousel slide, a navigation overlay's picture) is never made lazy, and one with fetchpriority="auto" (block visibility by screen size) takes no place in that budget (the new in_budget()). LiteSpeed Cache's lazy loader copies data-sizes to sizes and keeps loading="lazy", so auto works through it. Browsers without sizes="auto" read the rest of sizes, as before. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0; a 680×250 PNG logo, a page opening with a 1600×1200 Image block and four 1536×1152 Image blocks in columns 1,400 px below; with and without a must-use plugin returning false from wp_lazy_loading_enabled): with 1.3.194 the desktop logo had fetchpriority="high", the opening picture none, and with core's lazy loading off the four pictures below had loading="lazy" and sizes="(max-width: 1024px) 100vw, 1024px"; with this change neither logo tag (desktop and mobile) has fetchpriority, the opening picture has fetchpriority="high" and data-no-lazy="1", and the four have loading="lazy" and sizes="auto, (max-width: 1024px) 100vw, 1024px", with core's lazy loading on or off.
* New, on by default: Load TranslatePress's scripts without holding up the page (Speed; SEOProStack_Translatepress_Defer, setting translatepress_defer; GitHub issue #1486). TranslatePress 3.3.8 enqueues trp-language-switcher-js-v2 (assets/js/trp-frontend-language-switcher.js, 16 KB; TRP_Language_Switcher_V2::enqueue_assets()) on every page, and with WooCommerce active trp-frontend-compatibility (0.8 KB; TRP_Translation_Render::enqueue_scripts()), both in the head with no loading strategy, so PageSpeed Insights listed them as render-blocking on every TranslatePress site in the fleet (120 to 130 ms on desktop, an estimated 430 ms on mobile, 10 November 2026). Both only act on DOMContentLoaded, which deferred scripts run before, in order. At wp_enqueue_scripts PHP_INT_MAX each registered handle with a src and no strategy gets WordPress's defer strategy (WP_Scripts::add_data('strategy')), so WordPress still keeps it blocking when a blocking script depends on it or an inline script follows it. Not in the admin, TranslatePress's translation editor (trp-edit-translation), feeds, embeds or AMP requests; filter seoprostack_translatepress_defer. Changing the setting empties the page cache. In the Recommended speed settings while TranslatePress is active. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, TranslatePress 3.3.8 with English and French, WooCommerce 11.2.2): both tags print in the head with defer; in Chromium the floating switcher opened (is-open, aria-expanded true) and its French link went to /fr/, with no console errors; with the filter returning false both print without defer, as before, and the switcher behaves the same.
* Fixed: Remove CPT base redirects a renamed item's old short address (/old/) to its new one instead of giving a 404 (GitHub issue #1465). The request matched WordPress's name rule with no post type, so core's wp_old_slug_redirect() guessed post and never found the item's _wp_old_slug. SEOProStack_Remove_Cpt_Base::resolve() now, when no item of the chosen types has that name and the path has no slash, looks for one published item of those types whose _wp_old_slug is the path (one get_posts() with a meta_query, ids only) and sets post_type, so WordPress sends a 301. Unknown names still give 404.
* Added: Clear Cloudflare's copies of pages (Speed, off by default; GitHub issue #1461). LiteSpeed Cache's Clear Cloudflare cache covers Purge All only (src/purge.cls.php _purge_all() calls CDN\Cloudflare::purge_all(), 7.9.3) and sends {"purge_everything":true}, emptying the whole zone: on a zone shared by 24 multisite networks (one per subdomain) every site then fetched its pages from one shared hosting account at once. Purging one post, the front page or a type never reached Cloudflare, and WP-Optimize has no Cloudflare purge. New SEOProStack_Cloudflare_Purge listens to litespeed_purged_all and litespeed_purged_all_lscache (whole site, or every site on a network-wide purge as SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::network_purge(), now public, reads it; skipped where LiteSpeed Cache's Cloudflare API and Clear Cloudflare cache are both on), litespeed_purged_post (whole site where purge-post_p or purge-post_all is on), wpo_cache_flush (every site on a network: its cache folder holds them all), transition_post_status (the post's permalink while published, home and posts page) and seoprostack_page_cache_purge_post. A whole site is purged by its home host name, or by prefix (host/folder/) on a subdirectory network; more than MAX_URLS (30) addresses waiting for one site become a whole-site purge. Purges collect in memory and are stored once at shutdown in seoprostack_cloudflare_purge_queue (a network option on a network, not autoloaded), then sent by WP-Cron (seoprostack_cloudflare_purge, single site or main site) DELAY (15) seconds later, after delivering a LiteSpeed purge still held by WP-CLI, so a visitor in between cannot put the old page back at the edge. A run sends at most PER_RUN (4) requests of up to MAX_ITEMS (30) hosts, prefixes or files, GAP (60) seconds apart while purges wait, within the Free plan's 5 purge requests a minute per zone; a 429 or 5xx keeps the request for the next run, up to TRIES (6). The last LOG_SIZE (20) answers are kept in seoprostack_cloudflare_purge_log and shown in the setting's panel and by wp seoprostack cloudflare-purge status|site|url|run|log. Credentials per site: SEOPROSTACK_CLOUDFLARE_TOKEN and SEOPROSTACK_CLOUDFLARE_ZONE constants, else the feature's own token and zone (per-site settings, never network-wide), else LiteSpeed Cache's cdn-cloudflare_key and _zone when its Cloudflare API is on (a global key with cdn-cloudflare_email sends X-Auth-Email and X-Auth-Key); the token is never shown or logged (new secret field flag: SEOProStack_Settings_Manager::render_text() renders an empty password input saying one is saved; a space removes it). Purging by host name or prefix, not Cache-Tag: a host name is unique per site with no header to keep in LiteSpeed's cached copies, and pages cached before the switch was turned on are covered too.
