Hosting needs: cloudflare_keeps_pages() no longer suggests a purge when the connector is ready or this server gives Cloudflare an edge time of CF_SHORT_EDGE (600) seconds or less (new cloudflare_edge_time(): the max-age of Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control or CDN-Cache-Control from a HEAD request straight to this server, since Cloudflare does not pass them on); new cloudflare_clears_zone() warns when Clear Cloudflare cache is on for a site on a subdomain of the zone's domain or a network with another site on it; new cloudflare_rule_advice() warns when Cloudflare's Cache-Control max-age to browsers is over CF_SHORT_EDGE, when a made-up cookie (sps_probe=1) gets DYNAMIC or BYPASS twice where no cookie got a cached answer (new cloudflare_skips_cookies()), and when LiteSpeed Cache's Cache Mobile is on. The LiteSpeed Cache preset's litespeed_cloudflare condition (SEOProStack_Litespeed::cloudflare_connected()) no longer holds on a site whose host is not the zone's domain (cdn-cloudflare_name) or its www., so the preset no longer turns Clear Cloudflare cache on there. Test network (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3) with a token and a Free-plan zone: a host purge and a URL purge answered success and the pages then answered cf-cache-status MISS, then HIT; a prefix purge (host/about/) answered success; a burst of purges on two sites was stored as one queue and sent as one request each by type; addresses on 127.0.0.1 were refused by Cloudflare (1140, 1125) and logged as failed; with no token the panel warns and ready() is false; a LiteSpeed global key gave X-Auth-Email and X-Auth-Key and a token Authorization; with LiteSpeed's API and Clear Cloudflare cache on, litespeed_purged_all queued nothing; cloudflare_clears_zone() was true for a zone shared with another site of the network and false without Clear Cloudflare cache. On a live Free-plan zone whose rule matches an empty Cookie header, cdn_probe() found cf_cookie true.

= 1.3.194 =
* Fixed: Keep pages cached no longer fills every site of a hosting account at once (GitHub issue #1471). On one shared account (CloudLinux and LiteSpeed, 6 cores) running 24 networks, an update made across all of them purged each in turn, and every network's main site then filled at the same time from the host's cron job, 90 seconds a batch, each page a full build in a web PHP worker (fills of 1,000 to 5,624 pages per main site; about 12,000 addresses queued across 20 networks at one point). The host's CPU protection then refused every site of the account for a while, unrelated single sites too (403, "Access to this resource on the server is denied!"), and run() stopped a batch only once a page was refused, after the account was already over its limit. Now: (1) a batch opens pages only with one of the account's slots, two by default (filter seoprostack_keep_cached_slots; below 1, no limit): a non-blocking flock() on seoprostack-fill-<uid>-<n>.lock in sys_get_temp_dir(), shared by every install that runs as the same system user (under CageFS that folder is the account's own), released in a finally block and by the system if PHP stops. With no slot free the batch opens nothing and is rescheduled a GAP (60 seconds) later without counting as a stop (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::take_slot(), release_slot(), no_slot()). Only a slot another batch holds counts as taken (flock()'s would-block flag): where no lock file can be opened or locked (a file system without locks), or the temporary folder is outside open_basedir (checked first, so such hosts log no warning), batches run as before; wp seoprostack keep-cached, run by hand, takes no slot. (2) A site with blog_public 0 (Settings, Reading: discourage search engines; staging and review copies) plans only its main pages, not up to 1,000 (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages::site_max(); filter seoprostack_keep_cached_max, 0 for main pages only), and a network's second round adds no newer pages for such a site. (3) A page that took over 8 seconds to build, per request so a phone copy does not double it, ends its batch after it (SLOW; filter seoprostack_keep_cached_slow), so the next waits a GAP: a slow build is the first sign of a busy account. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Apache; separate PHP processes of one system user, as installs on one account are): with two processes holding the slots a third got none; a holder that exited without releasing freed its slot for the next; no_slot() kept the queue and its stop count and scheduled the next batch 60 seconds later, and scheduled nothing for an empty queue. With WP-Optimize's page cache on and one page taking 2 seconds (slow filter 1), the first batch ended right after that page (8 of 10 opened) and the next opened the other 2. A site with 9 pages planned all 9, and with search engines discouraged only its home page (no menus on that theme). On the hosting account, PHP's command line has its own /tmp (mode 700, CageFS), and the account's PHP settings (alt_php.ini) set no open_basedir, with no per-site override.

= 1.3.193 =
* Faster: Image loading takes the copy of a Kadence Blocks background picture that is at least as wide as the device pixels it paints, as browsers choose from a picture's srcset, no longer with 10% to spare (GitHub issue #1476). The four scripts that choose a copy (SEOProStack_Kadence_Hero, #1221; SEOProStack_Kadence_Slider, #1333; SEOProStack_Kadence_Early, #1337; SEOProStack_Kadence_Rows, #1341) multiplied the painted pixels × devicePixelRatio by 1.1, which took the next size up whenever a picture painted within 10% under a copy's width: on PageSpeed's phone (412 pixels, DPR 1.75) every full-width picture paints 721 device pixels, 793 with the margin, so it got WordPress's 1024 copy instead of the 768 one; a 768-pixel tablet at DPR 2 (1,536) and a 1,440-pixel desktop at DPR 1 got the full upload instead of the 1536 copy. Each now rounds the painted device pixels to the nearest whole one (Math.round), so a layout fraction such as 1,536.0002 does not tip a box that a copy exactly fits to the next size, and takes the narrowest copy at least that wide. All four use the same rule, so the head's copy is still the one the script after the block keeps. The margin was for rows that grow as fonts and late styles arrive; the ResizeObserver and resolution watch already swap in a larger copy when a box grows, never a smaller one, so every picture stays at least as wide as the pixels it paints: never less sharp. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0; 2048×1536 pictures with 300, 768, 1024 and 1536 copies of 193 KB (1024), 345 KB (1536) and 578 KB (full) for one), Chromium at 412×823 DPR 1.75, 393×852 DPR 3, 768×1024 DPR 2, 1440×900 DPR 1 and 1920×1080 DPR 1, pages opening with a full-width row as tall as its content (with and without a background slider), a full-width row 400 px high (the head preloads its copy) and a column picture 2,400 px down: before, the rows took the 1536 copy on the phone (933 to 963 pixels painted) and the full picture on the tablet (1,536) and at 1440 (1,440), the column the full one on the tablet (1,440); now the 1024 and 1536 copies. Every file shown was at least as wide as the device pixels it paints, each picture was fetched once, the 400 px row showed the copy the head preloaded, widening the phone to 1600 still took the full picture, the iPhone and 1920 screens took the same files as before, and there were no console errors or PHP messages. Kadence home pages (10 November 2026, 1.3.192, through a local proxy that removed only the margin): of seven, three changed files, on the phone 1536 to 1024 (80 KB to 19 KB AVIF) and 1024 to 768 (104 KB to 77 KB), with tablets and 1440 or 1920 desktops a size smaller, the rest unchanged; local Lighthouse on the phone, alternating runs: LCP 4.0 to 3.7 seconds (5 pairs) on the first, 3.2 to 4.4 against 3.5 to 3.6 seconds (3 pairs) on the second.

= 1.3.192 =
* Fixed: a cron run no longer logs "Cron unschedule event error for hook: seoprostack_keep_pages_cached, Error code: could_not_set" when a cache purge moved Keep pages cached's next batch while the run was going (GitHub issue #1472). wp-cron.php reads the due tasks once, at its start; a purge in another request moves the batch back (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::save(), so it runs after LiteSpeed applies the purge), and when the run then removed the copy it had read, nothing stored changed, update_option() reported a failure and wp-cron.php logged it, in the site's language. The batch itself was already left to its new time (#1362), so no page went unopened: the line was noise, but about 70 lines on 9 November on one host's sites that purge often (1 the day before), and the deploy check counts it. Fixes for other plugins' scheduled tasks list (SEOProStack_Cron_List, since 1.3.153) now adds unschedule() on pre_unschedule_event (PHP_INT_MAX) in cron runs and WP-CLI: removing a task the stored list (read past the caches) no longer has is done, so it returns true, and when the run's copy still has the task it drops the option caches, as write() does when another request already applied a change (#1270), so the rest of the run reads the stored list. The stored list is asked rather than the run's copy, which each write in the run refreshes, so a recurring task moved first by core is covered too. A task still stored, a list that cannot be read, or another filter's result is left to core, as is every removal on other requests. The same holds for any plugin's task two requests remove.
* Fixed: Load Fluent Forms' scripts when a form comes near also reads a content security policy set in a <meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy"> tag in the page's head, as well as one PHP sends (GitHub issue #1455, PR #1457, a review follow-up to #1428). SEOProStack_Fluentforms_Later collects what the theme prints from get_header (before header.php) to the end of wp_head, keeps the policy tags among its real elements (WP_HTML_Tag_Processor, so not comments or script text) and prints the head unchanged; where such a policy blocks inline scripts, the forms' scripts load as Fluent Forms prints them. When another plugin's output buffer is still open at the end of wp_head, the head cannot be read, and that page is left as it is.
* Fixed: deactivating SEO Pro Stack for the whole network now clears Next-gen pictures' restart mark, sync mark, schedule and cached check on every site, not only the current one (GitHub issue #1466), so no stale mark suppresses a restart check after reactivation.

= 1.3.191 =
* Fixed: on a network where LiteSpeed Cache is active on some sites only, command-line runs on the other sites no longer open their admin-ajax.php again and again (GitHub issue #1450). SEOProStack_Litespeed::deliver_purge() (since 1.3.146, run at the end of every WP-CLI and host-cron WP-Cron run) checked only that the site runs on a LiteSpeed server and that a litespeed.purge.queue or litespeed.purge.queue2 option held a purge. A site without LiteSpeed Cache can hold one (a purge stored for it from another site, such as wp litespeed-purge blog <ID> from the main site), but nothing on it takes the queue: admin-ajax.php answered 400 and the queue stayed, so every run opened it again, as "lscache_runner SEOProStack" and through Cloudflare where the site has no LiteSpeed Cache Server IP. On one host with 24 networks (sites 2 to 4 of each without LiteSpeed Cache, 80 such queues) that was about 8 requests a subsite every 45 minutes, the largest steady source of the host's own requests when LiteSpeed's bot protection then refused the host's address for every site on the account. deliver_purge() and the delivery_args() filter now do nothing unless LiteSpeed Cache runs on the current site (new running(): LSCWP_V defined and no switch_to_blog() in effect, else LiteSpeed Cache active on the site or for the network as stored, so a site switched to is read for itself and a request on which Load plugins only where needed skips LiteSpeed Cache still delivers). A queue left on such a site stays until LiteSpeed Cache runs there; setting it to -1 clears it.

= 1.3.190 =
* Faster: Lighter WooCommerce pages leaves out WooCommerce's Coming soon fonts where the page already loads the same font (GitHub issue #1447). In Coming soon mode, even "store pages only", WooCommerce adds its coming-soon page's Inter (weights 300 to 900, a 327 KB variable font with every character) and Cardo to the theme's theme.json fonts on every request (ComingSoonRequestHandler::experimental_filter_theme_json_theme(), WooCommerce 11.2), so WordPress prints their @font-face rules in <style class="wp-fonts-local"> on every page. Where the theme also uses Inter (the Kadence theme's Google Fonts, local or not), the browser took WooCommerce's face for every weight and downloaded the 327 KB file before the first paint; on four PropertyServicesDirectory home pages it was a VeryHigh-priority request before LCP (PageSpeed mobile 80-88, LCP 3.9-5.4 s). Only while woocommerce_coming_soon is yes, a page buffer (template_redirect PHP_INT_MAX, the GET HTML views Font fallbacks buffers: SEOProStack_Font_Fallbacks::html_view()) removes each @font-face rule in wp-fonts-local whose file is in WooCommerce's assets/fonts/ folder for a family the page also declares without WooCommerce's rules (new public SEOProStack_Font_Fallbacks::declared_families(): another @font-face, including a theme.json face in the same style, an on-site font stylesheet such as Kadence's wp-content/fonts/, or a Google Fonts link). A family only WooCommerce gives (Cardo, or Inter where the page has no other) keeps its rule, so no text changes font. The coming-soon screen keeps both: its wp-block-woocommerce-coming-soon block, or the woo-coming-soon-page meta tag, which classic themes do not print since WooCommerce adds it after their get_header() (found by the PSD session). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence, WooCommerce 11.2.2, Coming soon for store pages only): with Inter from Google Fonts or local, the home page and a 404 page lose WooCommerce's Inter rule and keep Cardo's; with the feature off both stay; with Lato both stay; /shop/ (the coming-soon screen) keeps both; Chrome at 412x915 with Kadence's local Inter downloads only the theme's 48,256-byte Inter (faces Inter 400 and 700) instead of that plus WooCommerce's 326,628-byte file (face Inter 300 900), with the same line breaks and layout and no console errors; debug.log empty. WooCommerce's settings, the admin and the block editor are unchanged.
* Faster: Media in cloud storage keeps its claim index across updates (GitHub issue #1445). The index's state was keyed to SEOPROSTACK_VERSION, so every update started a new build from the top (5 seconds a storage batch), and until it finished each upload's wp_unique_filename() read every stored inventory, 100 attachments a query: about a minute a picture on one multisite with about 104,000 attachment IDs on the day of 1.3.189. claims_table(true), claims_due() and build_claims() now take a finished build by any version with CLAIMS_SCHEMA (new claims_built()), and a build another version started goes on from its cursor. Every version since 1.3.90 indexes each inventory it saves (index_claims() needs only the schema), so the index stays complete through an update; versions 1.3.90 to 1.3.189 still start a new build when they find one by another version, so a downgrade and update builds it again. A version before 1.3.90 run in between is not detected. Change CLAIMS_SCHEMA when the hashes, the table or the files indexed change. The URL index and kept-original table stay keyed to the version: their step after an update only looks for options an older version wrote (a prefix range of wp_options, usually empty in one batch), and their lookups meanwhile read one option each.
* Fixed: the German login-link email (de_DE, de_AT, de_CH) is translated whole again, with the link on its own line; machine translation had dropped words and moved the line breaks, so the link sat mid-sentence. The same email in 14 other languages (es_ES, es_MX, et, fr_BE, fr_CA, fr_FR, it_IT, lv, nl_BE, nl_NL, pl_PL, pt_BR, pt_PT, ro_RO) no longer starts each line after the first with a space. Translations only (PR #1440).
* Developers: the core files match WP Plugin Starter v1.0.53 (scripts/sync-core.sh --ref v1.0.53, PR #1448). scripts/lib/deploy-remote.sh's cron-race and cron-save-contention checks match WordPress's could_not_set cron notice in any site language (wpallstars/wp-plugin-starter-template-for-ai-coding#658): only the English text matched before, so the 1.3.189 fleet deploy failed one install on the Dutch line "Cron ongepland event fout voor hook: …, foutcode: could_not_set". scripts/deploy-sites.sh tries a failed release download again after 30, 60, 120 and 120 seconds, as GitHub sometimes answers "release not found" for a few minutes after publishing, and --web-error-log takes ~/ and {name}/{name_}, so one run checks each install's own error log. scripts/sync-core.sh finds the starter next to the first linked worktree of a bare main repository. RELEASING.md documents them.
* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first covers three more recommended plugins' front-end stylesheets (GitHub issue #1430): Lasso Lite (simple-urls; pages/class-hook.php enqueues admin/assets/css/lasso-lite.css on every page), Code Snippets and Code Snippets Pro (dist/prism.css, which Shortcodes::enqueue_highlighting() in php/Integration/Shortcodes.php enqueues on pages with a [code_snippet_source] shortcode or code-snippets/source block), and Tutor LMS Certificate Builder in the folder Themeum now installs it to, edupro-lms-certificate-builder (24 of the owner's installs, against 7 in tutor-lms-certificate-builder, already listed). Found by checking the recommended plugins (admin/data/free-plugins.php, pro-plugins.php) against the plugin folders on the owner's sites and the preview site. Bookly's stylesheets stay as they are: its forms' steps arrive by AJAX with classes in no script or HTML, and making them non-blocking measured 0 to 2 Lighthouse points on one home page with five booking forms.
* New, on by default: Load Fluent Forms' scripts when a form comes near (Speed; SEOProStack_Fluentforms_Later, setting fluentforms_later; GitHub issue #1428). Fluent Forms prints form-submission.js, fluentform-advanced.js and, for some fields, Fluent Forms Pro's intl-tel-input, jQuery File Upload, cropper and lity on every page with a form; on one Kadence home page with a form below the first screen, leaving them out took Lighthouse's mobile LCP from 3.3–3.6 s to 3.0–3.2 s (3 alternating runs, 8 November 2026). At script_loader_tag 10, with Fluent Forms 6.2.17 or later, each script whose file is in the fluentform or fluentformpro folder and not yet printed is printed as type="seoprostack/near" with its address in data-seoprostack-src (and its -js-before and -js-after inline scripts as the same type, so they run in their order); -js-extra data prints as it was. Which scripts are held is read from their registered address, so a filter that moves the printed one (a CDN) does not split them. At wp_footer 99 an inline loader (id seoprostack-fluentforms-later; data-cfasync="false", data-no-optimize="1", data-seoprostack-nodelay) runs them once a .fluentform comes within 600 px of the screen (IntersectionObserver), a field in one gets focus, or a pointerdown on a link or button whose #ID, aria-controls or data-toggle-target names an element holding one; at once without IntersectionObserver or a form. The files are fetched together first (rel=preload, 5 s at most), the loader waits for window.jQuery (every 50 ms for 20 s, then every 500 ms, so scripts never run without it), then runs them one after another with form-submission.js, and any script that needs it, last (data-seoprostack-late): run after the page has loaded, form-submission.js starts the forms 100 ms later, and fluentform-advanced.js and the Pro libraries listen for its fluentform_init. Then each form.frm-fluent-form gets reInitExtras, which starts the phone and date fields whose inline scripts ran on jQuery's ready before their libraries. Until they have run, a submit of a form.frm-fluent-form is held (preventDefault, in capture) and starts the loading, so a form sent early does not post to the page and lose what was typed; it can be sent again. The loader does not wait for window.seoprostackCss(): SEOProStack_Used_Css_Scripts::running() now reads seoprostack/near scripts (their data-seoprostack-src file, or inline code) as scripts that run as the page loads, so their words, such as ff-el-is-error, keep their rules in the used CSS. Which scripts wait is decided once per page by SEOProStack_Fluentforms_Scripts::plan(). Nothing is held on conversational form pages, on pages whose opening block (SEOProStack_Image_Loading::opening_block()) holds a form, when the first of the scripts prints before wp_footer (a form further down could still add scripts that need it), with pickr or rangeslider (their inline scripts do not check for the library), when another plugin's script needs a held handle, directly or through another script, when a Delay scripts keyword matches a held script or anything it needs (its id, address or inline code), on wp-login.php, feeds, embeds, the Customizer, AJAX or REST, or when the PHP-sent content security policy blocks inline scripts or styles; filter seoprostack_fluentforms_later. A script another filter gave a type (a consent manager's text/plain) keeps it. Changing the setting empties the page cache (SEOProStack_Page_Cache::purge_all()). The Recommended speed settings leave it out, with the reason, while Fluent Forms is older than 6.2.17. In the Recommended speed settings while Fluent Forms is active. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0, Fluent Forms 6.2.17 and Fluent Forms Pro; a form with a dropdown, a field shown on its value, a phone field and a file upload; 412×823 phone emulation): form 2,500 px down, 0 Fluent Forms script requests on load instead of 8, all 8 on scrolling to it; a form in the first screen loaded them straight away; a form in a hidden panel loaded nothing until its link opened it. In each case an empty submit showed 2 errors, the dependent field appeared on its value, the phone field got its country selector (it did not before reInitExtras was added), the file upload started, and a filled form submitted with Fluent Forms' success message. A two-step form with a date field 2,500 px down: none of its 3 scripts on load, all on scrolling to it; Next with empty fields showed 2 errors, filled it moved to step 2, the date picker (flatpickr) was there and the form submitted. No console errors, debug log clean. With Used CSS on, the page's used CSS kept the ff-el-is-error rules, which the page's HTML does not use. With Delay scripts holding jquery.min.js, Fluent Forms' scripts printed as before.
* New, on by default: Load Google Maps embeds when they come near (Speed; SEOProStack_Maps_Later, setting maps_later; GitHub issue #1297). Browsers start loading an iframe with loading="lazy" well before it reaches the screen, so on desktop a Google Maps embed in a lower section of a page loaded with it (one Kadence home page, Kadence Blocks Google Maps block, Embed API: 16 requests, about 490 KB, about 360 ms of main-thread time and 2 long tasks in PageSpeed's desktop run, plus its own Google Fonts). At render_block 20, each iframe whose address is a Google Maps embed (google.<domain>/maps/embed, or /maps with output=embed, on www., maps. or no subdomain) gets its src moved to data-seoprostack-src and data-no-lazy="1" (so LiteSpeed Cache's and WP Rocket's lazy loaders skip it), followed by a <noscript> copy of the original (also data-no-lazy); width, height, loading, title and the block's container stay. At wp_footer 20, when a map was held, an inline script (id seoprostack-maps-later; data-cfasync="false", data-no-optimize="1", data-seoprostack-nodelay) gives each its address once it comes within 600 px of the screen (IntersectionObserver, which also fires when a tab, accordion or modal holding one is shown), every one on beforeprint, and all at once without IntersectionObserver. Left alone: maps in the top-level block the main post opens with (counted with pre_render_block at PHP_INT_MAX), loading="eager", text from Image loading's exclusion list (SEOProStack_Image_Loading::excluded(), whether or not Image loading is on: the skip-lazy/no-lazy classes, data-skip-lazy/data-no-lazy attributes, custom-logo and the site's own lines); the Kadence map's JavaScript API type; shortcode output; admin, REST, AJAX and JSON requests, feeds and embeds; pages whose PHP-sent content security policy blocks inline scripts or styles (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Policy::allows()). Filter seoprostack_maps_later. In the Recommended speed settings. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0, desktop Chrome 1366×900): a Kadence Blocks map 2,000 px below another in the opening row loaded with the page with the setting off (58 Google Maps requests on load) and not with it on (43, the opening map's alone), and loaded on scrolling to it; a map in the opening row printed unchanged; a maps.google.com output=embed iframe in a Custom HTML block 3,000 px down waited and loaded on scrolling; a YouTube iframe and a map with class skip-lazy were unchanged; heights stayed 450 px before and after; the debug log stayed empty. The only console errors were HTTP 400s inside the skip-lazy test map's own iframe, whose made-up pb= address Google rejects, with the setting on and off; none from the page.
* Developers: the core files match the starter again (GitHub issue #1290). This plugin's deploy fixes (Cloudflare purge, origin-pinned home fetch, --keep-releases, the GH#1349 and GH#1369 cron checks, newer workflow pins) went into the starter first (wpallstars/wp-plugin-starter-template-for-ai-coding#654), merged with the starter's own changes since the last sync. scripts/sync-core.sh --discard-local then brought back: scripts/deploy-sites.sh --apply warns cron save contention (N lines) instead of failing when every new log line is WordPress's could_not_set cron-save notice; the deploy reads a log from its top when it was replaced (new inode), not only when it shrank or its first line changed; the folder swap puts back the caller's signal handlers, unzips with umask 022 and refuses to move into a plugin folder another writer recreated; licensed add-ons in scripts/build-release.sh and scripts/test-site.sh; and updates to scripts/lib/plugin.sh, scripts/preflight-release.sh and scripts/sync-core.sh. The pinned home fetch reads LiteSpeed Cache's Server IP, then seoprostack_origin_ip, as before. After review: Cloudflare zones are purged once per install (two installs in one zone are both purged after their update), an address whose zone lookup fails no longer stops the others and is counted in the summary and named in the dry run, the token needs Zone Read as well as Cache Purge, the run-folder clean-up keeps any run folder changed in the last two hours, and the pinned home fetch also pins ports 80 and 443 so an http to https redirect stays on the origin (a redirect to another host is no longer called pinned).
* Fixed: File and account integrity checks no longer report two kinds of uploaded .php file that cannot run code (GitHub issue #1441). A file with no `<?` anywhere is plain text to PHP (WP Mailster's uploads/wp-mailster/wpmst.log.php); a file whose first statement is __halt_compiler(); holds only data after it, as PHP parses nothing past it (All In One Security's uploads/aios/firewall-rules/settings.php). SEOProStack_Integrity_Folders::harmless() accepts both: the new halts() reads the tokens at the file's start (quiet tokens, then __halt_compiler, brackets, and `;` or a close tag), and a file with no `<?` in its first 2 KB is then read in parts up to 8 MB (open_tag_after()). Files that include code when loaded, even behind an ABSPATH check, are still reported. Checked with PHP 8.5 on 14 sample files: the issue's four (plain text, halt with JSON, halt then a close tag and more PHP, text then PHP) and code before the halt, a halt without brackets, a tag after 2 KB, and the existing exit-guarded log and empty index.php cases.

* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first handles WP Grid Builder's two stylesheets that still held up the first paint (GitHub issue #1434). Its grid and card stylesheet (wpgb-grids, written to uploads/wpgb/grids/) was outside the handled folders: SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::sources() adds wpgb/ in the site's uploads folder. Its cards' and facets' Google Fonts (wpgb-fonts) came from fonts.googleapis.com, another host on the first paint's path that also saw each visitor's address: new SEOProStack_Used_Css::wpgb_fonts(), on WP Grid Builder's own wp_grid_builder/frontend/register_styles filter, points a fonts.googleapis.com css or css2 source at the local copy the Kadence theme's loader makes (Kadence\get_webfont_url(), else Kadence Blocks' KadenceWP\KadenceBlocks\get_webfont_url(): the stylesheet and its font files in wp-content/fonts/, as the theme does for its own Google Fonts), and SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::kadence_fonts() accepts that stylesheet with id wpgb-fonts-css. Google's address stays when there is no Kadence loader or the copy fails. A scan of about 100 pages on 16 Kadence sites (home pages and up to 6 menu pages each) found no other render-blocking stylesheet outside the handled folders. Local Lighthouse 12 (mobile, simulated throttling, through a proxy that changed only the HTML; 3 alternating runs) on a listings page with a grid: 93, 93, 93 with both stylesheets blocking, 99, 93, 100 without (FCP 2.1–2.2 s to 1.3–1.4 s in two runs).
* Faster: with WebP and AVIF images' Copies at their own addresses on, pictures in blocks in post content, block widgets and Kadence Pro's Elements get a <source> with every width their <img> offers (GitHub issue #1436, since 1.3.53, #792). SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::pictures() ran on render_block, and those filters render blocks (do_blocks) before core's wp_filter_content_tags adds width, height, srcset and sizes, so each <picture> was built from the <img> saved in the block and its <source> named only that address; browsers pick from the first <source> they can show, so every screen got that one file. On one Kadence page that opens with a cover block, the phone's LCP request was the 2,048 px AVIF copy (240,918 bytes) where the 768 px copy is 21,391 bytes, and 44 of the page's 49 pictures had fewer addresses in the <source> than in the <img> (Kadence Blocks Pro query cards named only their 768 px copy, so sharp screens lost the larger ones). New rendered_block() leaves a block's pictures to the later pictures() pass of the content filter running it (the_content, widget_block_content, and new ktp_the_content at 30 with Kadence Pro) while that pass is still to come; blocks rendered elsewhere are wrapped as before.
* Fixed: Image loading no longer moves Kadence Blocks columns out of place when a column's background picture is the first on the page to wait (GitHub issue #1422, since 1.3.171). SEOProStack_Kadence_Rows::around_column() printed the one-time loader script in front of the column, so it sat in the row's column grid, where Kadence Blocks places columns with :nth-child(N of *:not(style)), which skips the waiting style but counts a script: each column took the next one's margins, mobile order and grid spans. On one production site 5 of 10 pages had a picture column 100 px under its text column on desktop and above it instead of below on phones. The loader now goes first inside the column, before its inner column (found by the column's own opening tag), and the column's call stays last inside it; rows are unchanged. Pages already in a page cache keep the old markup until it is emptied; a seoprostack_kadence_column_wait filter added as a workaround can be removed after updating.

* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first loads the full stylesheets once the page has loaded, or when the visitor first moves, taps, scrolls or types, whichever comes first (GitHub issue #1426; new setting used_css_later, troubleshooting group, on by default). The full stylesheets loaded without blocking (media="print", switched back on load) but started downloading as the browser read the head, so on a slow phone 20 to 30 of them shared the connection with the pictures and fonts the first screen needs, and Lighthouse's simulated LCP counts every request that starts before the observed LCP. SEOProStack_Used_Css_Page now moves each handled link's href to data-seoprostack-href (the link keeps its place and id; the <noscript> copy stays) and prints an inline script before </body> (later_js()) that gives each its address, as media="print" switched to its media once loaded, on the load event or the first mousemove, mousedown, keydown, touchstart, wheel or scroll (capture listeners). window.seoprostackCss() starts it and returns a promise kept when every link has loaded or failed, or after 3 seconds; SEOProStack_Delay_Scripts' loader calls it first and runs held scripts once it is kept, as SEOProStack_Used_Css_Scripts leaves their words out of the page's rules: in a layout check with the full stylesheets held back, Readabler's voice-navigation panel (held on two sites), hidden only by a rule in its stylesheet, showed at the bottom of the page when its script ran first. Only where the page's content security policy allows inline scripts (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Policy::allows($html, true)) and the page has no scripts another plugin holds until interaction (type litespeed/javascript, rocketlazyloadscript, pmdelayedscript or javascript/blocked), which cannot be made to wait; there the links load straight away as before. Local Lighthouse 12 (mobile, simulated throttling; each home page through a local proxy that changed only its HTML; 3 or 4 alternating runs, 8 November 2026, 1.3.187) on four Kadence sites: scores 81–83 to 85–87, 83–84 to 89–91, 79–82 to 86–88 and 83–91 to 86–93, LCP 0.5 to 1.2 s lower. A layout check (computed styles and box of every visible element with the full stylesheets held back, then once they had loaded) found no differences on 9 home-page and screen-size pairs across 6 Kadence sites (412 and 1,350 px wide), nor with the mobile menu drawer open. Changing the setting empties the page cache, like the feature's other settings. The page's closing comment adds ", the full stylesheets once it has loaded".

* Faster: Image loading no longer lets a later Kadence row take the place of the block a page opens with (GitHub issue #1420). SEOProStack_Image_Loading::mark_opening() (#1412) returned before matching when a rendered block had no <img>, so a hero row whose picture is a CSS background, and a heading row after it, never took their places in leading(); the match compared only the block name, innerContent and the number of inner blocks, and Kadence rows save no HTML of their own, so a later row of the same shape matched the opening block and its pictures got the Load straight away budget. On one Kadence home page (8 November 2026, 1.3.185; rows: background hero with a heading, a row of headings, then a Kadence Advanced Gallery row with one column, like the hero row) the gallery's first two pictures, about 1,800 and 2,050 px below a 412×823 screen, loaded straight away with data-no-lazy="1" (73 KB). mark_opening() now matches before the <img> check, so each leading block takes its place when it renders, and new same_block() also requires the same uniqueID when both blocks have one (Kadence Blocks gives each block its own). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0) with that page's blocks: eager pictures below the first screen 3 (90 KB) before, 0 after; with a core image block first, that picture keeps fetchpriority="high" and data-no-lazy="1" and the gallery is lazy; with the gallery row first, its first three pictures load straight away as before.

= 1.3.186 =
* Fixed: with a classic theme such as Kadence, Bookly's booking form stylesheets went back to the head after 1.3.185 (GitHub issue #1417). SEOProStack_Bookly_Assets (#1414) dequeued them and enqueued them again on wp_footer (priority 1), for print_late_styles(); but WordPress 6.9 and later, with a classic theme, hooks wp_hoist_late_printed_styles() (wp-includes/script-loader.php, from wp_load_classic_theme_block_styles_on_demand()), which captures late styles at wp_print_footer_scripts and inserts them before </head> through the template enhancement output buffer. On one Kadence site's home page (8 November 2026, 1.3.185, Bookly 28.7) the scripts were in the footer but the 7 stylesheets (tailwind.css, ladda.min.css, frontend-reset.css, modern-booking-form-calendar.css, bootstrap-icons.min.css, intlTelInput.css, bookly-main.css) printed last in the head; the #1414 test site used a block theme, where core does not hoist, and with Kadence active it showed the same 7. SEOProStack_Bookly_Assets::move() now calls new hold(), which takes queued Bookly styles off the queue, and hooks it again on wp_footer just before wp_print_footer_scripts (its priority, 20 by default, minus one) in case Bookly enqueues them again while the form renders; styles() runs just after it (plus one) and prints them with wp_styles()->do_items(), so core's capture never sees them. The page's load event waits for those stylesheets and Bookly's start-up script waits for document.readyState "complete", so the form's CSS is still in place before its script fills it.

= 1.3.185 =
* Faster: Image loading gives the pictures that load straight away to the block a post or page opens with, not to the first pictures in content order (GitHub issue #1412). SEOProStack_Image_Loading::content_image() counted the first Load straight away (3) content pictures wherever they were, so on Kadence home pages that open with a background hero, a heading band or a text row, 1 to 3 pictures of a posts grid, portfolio, gallery or footer, 876 to 17,250 px below a 412×823 screen, were eager with data-no-lazy="1" (23 to 220 KB per page) and on one page the largest (195 KB) started before the hero, the LCP. New render_block filter SEOProStack_Image_Loading::mark_opening() puts a temporary data-seoprostack-opening attribute on the pictures of the post's opening block (opening_block(): the first block, or the row after a heading band), and of the second block as "near"; content_image() reads and removes it. On a post with blocks only marked pictures can load straight away: opening-block pictures within the budget; the first picture of the second block only when the opening block has no picture and no background is preloaded (it may be the LCP on a phone); all others get loading="lazy" when core left it off, lose the fetchpriority="high" core (not the author) gave them (new wp_get_loading_optimization_attributes filter, SEOProStack_Image_Loading::core_priority()) and no data-no-lazy. When kadence_preload() preloads the opening block's background picture, that picture is the LCP and no picture outside the opening block loads straight away. Unchanged: keywords of Never lazy-load pictures containing (so a footer logo that matches one still loads straight away), an explicit fetchpriority="high", featured images before the content, and posts without blocks, archives and feeds.
* Faster: with Load plugins only where needed on, Bookly's booking form stylesheets and scripts print at the end of the page instead of in the head (GitHub issue #1414). Bookly's Lib\Base\ShortCode::init() enqueues them on wp_enqueue_scripts when the page's posts have a [bookly-form] shortcode (asset method "print", or on every page with "enqueue"), all in the head. On one Kadence site's home page (Bookly 28.7, 8 November 2026), the only Bookly shortcode was a booking form in a Kadence modal, yet about twenty Bookly files (tailwind.css, bookly-main.css, intlTelInput.css, bookly.min.js at 67 KB, intlTelInput.min.js, moment.min.js, hammer.min.js and the Files add-on's jquery.fileupload.js among them) were render-blocking; PageSpeed mobile scored 88 with first paint at 3.1 s. The form's markup is an empty container that bookly.min.js fills once document.readyState is "complete" (frontend/modules/booking/templates/short_code.php), so new SEOProStack_Bookly_Assets (includes/class-seoprostack-bookly-assets.php), booted by SEOProStack_Plugin_Loading on the site's pages, moves them after Bookly's callbacks (wp_enqueue_scripts at PHP_INT_MAX): handles starting "bookly-" or served from a plugins/bookly* folder (add-ons included) get script group 1, and their queued styles are dequeued and enqueued again on wp_footer (priority 1), so print_late_styles() prints them just before the footer scripts and the form's CSS is in place before its script fills it. Only where every Bookly shortcode in the queried posts is bookly-form and the content has no Bookly blocks: other Bookly shortcodes print their markup on the server and need their CSS for the first paint.

= 1.3.184 =
* Faster: Image loading's new picture sizes (#1406) get their WebP and AVIF copies in the same run (GitHub issue #1409). SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::make() added the sizes with wp_update_image_subsizes(), whose metadata save made SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::metadata_updated() schedule a batch five seconds later; on a host whose cron runs every few minutes the batch came minutes after SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::run() had purged and reopened the page. On one Kadence site (7 November 2026, 1.3.183) the home page hero's new 1536x1152 copy, which phones and desktops now chose, was sent as image/jpeg, 278,691 bytes, where the 2560 upload it replaced went out as image/avif, 185,835 bytes, while seoprostack_nextgen_batch was 157 s overdue. Responses are private and vary on Accept, so with copies at the same address the gap ended with the batch; with Copies at their own addresses the hero data's image-set() is worked out as the page is rendered and WebP and AVIF images does not purge pages after converting, so a page rendered in the gap could stay cached without them. New public SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::convert_now() takes the picture's claim (SEOProStack_Media_Storage::lease() of CLAIM plus its ID) and calls convert_claimed(), as convert_part() does for each picture of a batch; nothing when WebP and AVIF images is off or another batch holds the claim. make() calls it when the sizes were added, before run() purges and reopens the page. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0; WebP and AVIF images on, the image editor making both), a 2560×1920 upload converted without its 1536x1536 and 2048x2048 sizes: after one visit to a page opening with its hero row, one seoprostack_picture_sizes run (0.92 s, no WebP and AVIF batch) made hero-1536x1152.jpg (270,282 bytes) with its .avif (173,650) and .webp (177,584), and hero-2048x1536.jpg (421,575) with its .avif (200,036) and .webp (190,108); the picture's record listed all seven files, without incomplete, and it was marked converted. The 2560 upload's AVIF is 289,049 bytes.

= 1.3.183 =
* Faster: Image loading makes the missing uncropped sizes of Kadence Blocks row, column and slide pictures in the background (GitHub issue #1406). The sizing scripts (SEOProStack_Kadence_Hero, SEOProStack_Kadence_Early, SEOProStack_Kadence_Slider, SEOProStack_Kadence_Rows) take the smallest copy at least as wide as the pixels a picture paints, else the picture itself, from the sizes in its metadata (SEOProStack_Hero_Picture::copies()). Uploads made before WordPress 5.3, or while a plugin removed 1536x1536 and 2048x2048, lack those sizes, so a row that needs 1,025 to 2,000 pixels got the full upload: on one Kadence home page (7 November 2026, 1.3.182) the hero picture, 2560×1920 with copies 300, 768 and 1024 only, needed 1,460 pixels at 412 px DPR 1.75 and 1,485 at 1350 px, and both screens fetched the 2560 upload; a read-only wp_get_missing_image_subsizes() probe of 9 Kadence sites found pictures missing 1536x1536 on 8 of them, up to 2,415 on one site, among them the home page hero or slide pictures of three. SEOProStack_Hero_Picture::get() now calls new SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::want() with the picture's metadata, once per picture a request, outside the admin: when missing() finds a size among medium, medium_large, large, 1536x1536 and 2048x2048 that is registered (wp_get_registered_image_subsizes(), so sizes a site removed are left out), absent from the metadata and smaller than the picture (image_resize_dimensions(), as wp_get_missing_image_subsizes() decides), the file is an image (wp_attachment_is_image()) and _seoprostack_sizes_tried is older than a week, the attachment is added to the seoprostack_picture_sizes option (not autoloaded, at most 50, with the singular post it was seen on) and a single seoprostack_picture_sizes event is scheduled a minute later. SEOProStack_Picture_Sizes::run() takes up to three pictures, for at most 20 seconds of starts, removing each from the list and setting _seoprostack_sizes_tried before making its sizes, so one that stops the run is not tried again for a week; make() skips watermarked pictures (_seoprostack_watermark: their files are marked in place, so new sizes would carry a mark Watermark pictures could not remove) and calls wp_update_image_subsizes() with a wp_get_missing_image_subsizes filter keeping only those five sizes and a wp_get_original_image_path filter that falls back to the attached file when the original upload is gone (Media in cloud storage restores it first, at priority 5, on cron). WordPress then fires wp_generate_attachment_metadata (context update) and saves the metadata, so WebP and AVIF images makes the new files' copies and Media in cloud storage uploads them, as for any upload; Resize uploads acts only on create. The posts the pictures were seen on are purged with SEOProStack_Page_Cache::purge_posts(), whose new $only argument makes LiteSpeed Cache purge only each post's own page (its Po. tag) rather than what its Purge Rules list, often every page (#1399), and, with Keep pages cached on and a page cache, queued with SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::add() a minute later. More waiting schedules another run a minute later. Uninstall unschedules seoprostack_picture_sizes and deletes the option and the _seoprostack_sizes_tried meta; the sizes made stay. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0; a 2560×1920 upload with 1536x1536 and 2048x2048 removed from its metadata and disk), Chromium: a page opening with that picture's hero row and one opening with a heading band and a background slider hero queued the picture after one visit; the run made hero-1536x1152.jpg and hero-2048x1536.jpg and emptied the list; the hero data then offered 300, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048 and 2560, and both pages fetched only hero-1536x1152.jpg at 412×823 DPR 1.75 (need 1,283 and 1,460) and 1350×940 (need 1,485), where they had fetched the 2560 upload; the head kept only the no-JavaScript preload. With a cropped 900×900 size registered it was not made, and with original_image naming a missing file the sizes were made from the attached file.

= 1.3.181 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached fills the site again after LiteSpeed Cache empties every page on a post save (GitHub issue #1399). LiteSpeed Cache's Purge Rules For Publish/Update include Pages (purge-post_p, on by default): \LiteSpeed\Purge::_get_purge_tags_by_post() adds the PGS tag for any post of a built-in or viewable type, and \LiteSpeed\Tag::_build_type_tags() gives PGS to every is_page() view, the static front page among them, so publishing, updating, unpublishing, trashing or deleting any post, or a change to its comment count, empties every WordPress page; All pages (purge-post_all) purges *. Neither goes through _purge_all(), so litespeed_purged_all never fired, and post_changed() reopened only the post, the home page and the posts page; the check page (/?seoprostack-held=1, the front page) was purged too, but the post fill's hold() opened it again, so check() never saw it go. On one Kadence site (7 November 2026, LiteSpeed Cache's default rules) an automatic update of its privacy policy page with no user left 18 of its first 20 pages answering phones x-litespeed-cache: miss (0.64 to 1.24 s), while its one post among them, not tagged PGS, was a hit. A litespeed_purged_post action (fired by \LiteSpeed\Purge::_purge_post() after it adds the tags), SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::litespeed_post_purged(), calls SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::fill_later(false) when the page cache is LiteSpeed, Pages or All pages is on (SEOProStack_Litespeed::conf()), the post's type is built-in or viewable or LiteSpeed Cache's litespeed_purge_post_skip_non_viewable filter returns false (as _get_purge_tags_by_post() decides), and WP_IMPORTING is not set: the site's main pages, then LiteSpeed Cache's crawler, or the rest of the site where it is off, as after Purge All; saves one after another move the fill back for up to 10 minutes after the first. On a network, the site the post belongs to. post_changed() still queues the post's own page first.

= 1.3.180 =
* Fix: WebP and AVIF images starts converting again when pictures wait with no batch to convert them (GitHub issue #1396). Batches started when its settings were saved, pictures were uploaded or edited, the Media Library bulk action ran or its settings panel opened, and stopped when nothing waited; but what they make (formats and qualities, signature()) can change with none of those, when the server's image editor learns AVIF or a new setting's default (Make AVIF copies) reaches the site. Every picture then waited for its new copies until someone opened Media settings: on one 11-site network (7 November 2026) ten sites had 326 pictures waiting and no batch, so browsers got WebP copies rather than AVIF ones (82 KB rather than 57 KB for one slider picture). Once a day, on a WP-Cron, WP-CLI or admin request after wp_loaded, SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::maybe_restart() schedules a batch when none is scheduled and waiting(1) finds a picture; an autoloaded option (seoprostack_nextgen_restart) holds the day's mark, so other requests read nothing more, and deactivation and uninstall remove it.

Applies to the row a page opens with, rows and columns further down, row background sliders and Advanced Slider slides.

= 1.3.178 =
* New, on by default: Redirect to the main address before WordPress loads (Speed; SEOProStack_Host_Redirect, setting host_redirect; GitHub issue #1383). A request for a site's other address (www. added or taken away) reached WordPress, whose 301 from redirect_canonical() or a domain mapping plugin no page cache keeps (x-redirect-by: WordPress, x-litespeed-cache: miss): the first byte took 0.6 to 5.0 s on five Hostinger sites behind Cloudflare (7 November 2026). For each site's address host (the home address on a single site; every site's domain and the main site's home on a network), its www twin that is not itself a site's address is opened from the server (wp_remote_get, no redirects followed, ?seoprostack-host-check=<random>, sent straight to this server through SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() where it knows the address). A 301 or 308 to that host's home page adds a rule to a marked block at the top of the root .htaccess, through SEOProStack_Hardening::files_write() (now public): that host, GET or HEAD, not /.well-known/ on the request line (THE_REQUEST, as WordPress's rules send a missing file to index.php and the rules run again with that URI), not the check's query string; R=301 to the same path, keeping the query string. Checked daily (seoprostack_host_redirect_check) and a minute after the home option, a site's address or the network's sites change (the same hook with a "soon" argument); rules whose main host is no longer a site's address go at once. A wp_redirect filter drops a rule when WordPress sends a visitor to the address that rule redirects from (a loop, after an address changed without WordPress's hooks or while the file could not be written). WP-CLI cannot tell $is_apache or the root folder, so both are noted from the main site's admin and WP-Cron web requests (seoprostack_host_redirect_server; get_home_path() is used only when it holds ABSPATH and an index.php, as it gives / when the home and WordPress addresses have different hosts); until then nothing is written. The block is rewritten when an update changes its rules (a hash in seoprostack_host_redirect) and retried from admin and cron while the file cannot be written; it is removed when switched off, on deactivation (network-wide or from the main site) and on uninstall.
* Redirect to the main address before WordPress loads also starts where WP-Cron runs only from the command line (GitHub issue #1383). With DISABLE_WP_CRON and a server cron job running PHP, as on the Hostinger sites it was made for, no web request ran WP-Cron, so the server and root folder were noted only when someone opened the main site's admin screens, and the block stayed waiting on sites nobody signs in to. A check with nothing noted now sends one GET to the main site's admin-ajax.php (action seoprostack_host_note, which nothing handles, so it answers 400), straight to this server as the address checks are (a get_here() helper both use); boot() notes the server from that admin request, and the check drops its cached copies of seoprostack_host_redirect_server and reads it again.

= 1.3.177 =
* New, on by default: Keep text still while web fonts load (Speed; SEOProStack_Font_Fallbacks, settings font_fallbacks and font_fallbacks_skip; GitHub issue #1372). Web fonts with font-display: swap (the Kadence theme's Google Fonts) first show text in the browser's default font, and the swap when the web font arrives changes widths and line breaks: on one Kadence site (7 November 2026, phone throttling) a cookie banner fixed at the bottom grew from 355 to 380 px and the hero headings moved, CLS 0.19, and 0 with web font requests blocked. For each web font the page declares (@font-face in the page, a same-site font stylesheet under wp-content/fonts/ or uploads, or with "font" in its name, read through SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::stylesheet_file(); or a fonts.googleapis.com css/css2 link's family names) that assets/font-metrics/metrics.json knows, it adds "<Family> Fallback" faces (local Arial, Liberation Sans or Arimo; Times New Roman, Liberation Serif or Tinos for serif fonts) and "<Family> Fallback 2" faces (Roboto or Noto Serif, for Android), each with size-adjust from the small letters' average width (weighted by English letter frequency) and ascent-override, descent-override and line-gap-override, plus faces of their own (unicode-range U+41-5A for capitals, U+20,U+A0 for spaces, the rest's range leaving them out) when the capitals' or the space's ratio differs from the small letters' by 1% or more (Poppins Bold against Arial Bold: capitals 0.97, small letters 1.11, space 0.76; for the heading "USED CAR OF THE WEEK", sizing everything by the small letters made the fallback 19% wider, the three sizes 1.6%), the regular face for weights 100 to 500 and the bold face (Arial Bold against the web font's 700) for 600 to 900, in a <style> after the charset tag, with an inline script (data-seoprostack-nodelay) that calls document.fonts.load() for each so they are ready for the first paint. Each font-family declaration and --*font-family custom property in the page's own <style> elements gets them after the web font; @font-face rules are left alone. Its page buffer starts after Used CSS's, so it runs first and leaves Used CSS's copies of stylesheets, whose full files load later without the fallbacks, as they are. Nothing changes when no stack names a known font, for previews, the Customizer, feeds, embeds, AJAX, REST and AMP, or where a content security policy blocks inline styles, scripts or onload (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Policy). metrics.json (116 KB: 1,873 families, 758 with bold) is made by scripts/update-font-metrics.sh (Node.js 18 or newer): heights (unitsPerEm, ascent, descent, lineGap) from @capsizecss/metrics 4.4.0 (MIT, SEEK), without monospace, icon, emoji and barcode fonts, and widths (small letters, capitals, space) measured from the fonts Google Fonts serves for a-z, A-Z and space at 400 and 700 (subsets requested with text=, read from their hmtx and cmap tables, no file kept); the 23 fonts Google Fonts does not serve at 400 (Arial, Segoe UI, -apple-system and others) are left out, and the bases (Arial, Times New Roman, Roboto, Noto Serif) are measured from Arimo, Tinos, Roboto and Noto Serif. assets/font-metrics/LICENSE.txt keeps the licences. A prototype that added the same faces in the browser, on 12 Kadence home pages with PageSpeed's phone throttling (1 load with the faces, 3 without): CLS fell on 7 (0.1896 to 0.0007, 0.266 to 0.1211, 0.1654 to 0.0002, 0.0619 to 0.0002, 0.0449 to 0.0001, 0.0187 to 0.0003, 0.0014 to 0.0003), stayed within 0.002 on 3, and rose on 2 (0.0212 to 0.0299, 0.0051 to 0.0141). Where it rose or stayed high, one heading wraps differently: the 44 px h1 that went from 0.266 to 0.1211 is 365 px wide in its web font and 363 px in the fallback, in a 364 px box. A layout check of 78 pages on 13 sites (home pages and up to 5 linked pages each), switching each known web font to its fallback faces once the page had loaded and comparing every element's box, gave the same CLS as these loads, and with the three sizes against one size from Capsize's xWidthAvg: text blocks that changed height 201 against 231 at 412 px wide and 85 against 107 at 1,350 px; first-screen CLS summed 0.32 against 0.30 and 0.20 against 0.23; pages over 0.01 the same (8 and 3). Over 878 texts from those pages, the fallback's width was off by 1.56% on average against 2.22% (90th percentile 2.96% against 4.22%). Leave these fonts as they are (font_fallbacks_skip) lists fonts whose headings still move. On a Docker test site with the Kadence theme, Lexend and Poppins from Google Fonts (and from Load Google Fonts locally), CLS went from 0.001 to 0 (3 loads each), also with Load the CSS each page uses first on. Also listed in Recommended speed settings with the other features on by default.
* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first covers the front-end stylesheets of 10 more recommended plugins (GitHub issue #1377; owner's decision that it covers every plugin SEO Pro Stack recommends, as each may be used on a page at some point). SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::PLUGINS gains fluentform-block (Fluent Forms' block styles), fluent-comments (dist/css/app.css), burst-statistics (assets/css/shortcodes.css), secure-custom-fields (acf-input for front-end forms), yellow-pencil-visual-theme-customizer and waspthemes-yellow-pencil (animate.css and the custom CSS file), tutor-lms-certificate-builder, kadence-recaptcha (Kadence Captcha, css/recaptcha.css), kadence-cloud (Kadence Pattern Hub, assets/cloud-post-styles.css), and the owner's seoprocookieconsent and seoprodirectory, each checked in the plugin's code for a stylesheet enqueued on the front end. Kadence Custom Fonts is left out: its only front-end stylesheet is Typekit's, on Typekit's own host. Like the rest of the feature, nothing changes on sites where Load the CSS each page uses first is off. A check of 14 Kadence sites (7 November 2026; each home page and up to 20 linked pages) found render-blocking stylesheets only from plugins SEO Pro Stack does not recommend, which stay as they are.
* Fixed: the LiteSpeed Cache preset turns CSS Combine, Generate UCSS and Load CSS Asynchronously off (litespeed.conf.optm-css_comb, optm-ucss, optm-css_async; when single_site and feature:used_css) where Load the CSS each page uses first is on (GitHub issue #1379). SEOProStack_Used_Css::litespeed_css() steps aside on the whole site while any of them is on, as LiteSpeed would rework the same stylesheets, and nothing said so: on one Kadence site (7 November 2026) Load CSS Asynchronously was on, its critical CSS was still in QUIC.cloud's queue, and 20 of 21 pages crawled waited for every Kadence, Kadence Blocks, Fluent Forms and IndieWeb stylesheet before the first paint. As with Load JS Deferred and Delay scripts until people interact, LiteSpeed's copy goes off where SEO Pro Stack's does the job. Applying saves through LiteSpeed Cache's own code, which purges. Preset tested on LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3.
* Fixed: Keep pages cached no longer runs a fill early when a purge moved it back while WP-Cron was already running it (GitHub issue #1362). WordPress runs the copy of each due event it read when the run started, so a fill a purge had moved back 90 seconds still ran then. SEOProStack_Cron_List::next_stored() reads when the hook is next due in the cron list as stored now, past the caches, and SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached leaves the run when the fill has been moved to a later time.
* New: Czech (cs_CZ) translation of the strings visitors see (languages/seoprostack-cs_CZ.po, .mo and the JSON files for scripts; GitHub issue #1308). The Language switcher takes a subdivision flag, such as GB-ENG for England, as the English flag (the seoprostack_language_flag filter too), and has the Czech and England flags (assets/flags/CZ.svg, GB-ENG.svg; GitHub issue #1309).

= 1.3.176 =
* Fixed: Load plugins only where needed keeps what the site's pages learned when other plugins refresh their update caches and bookkeeping (GitHub issue #1367). Plugins with an older copy of EDD Software Licensing's updater cache their update details under an MD5 of their slug with no prefix (array('timeout' => …, 'value' => …); RECORD_OPTIONS had only the prefixed edd_sl_), and it expires after a few hours, to be deleted and saved again by wp-admin's update checks and by any WP-CLI command: on one site a read-only wp plugin list at that moment forgot everything learned, and three sites had 2 to 6 such options each. Plugin Update Checker's update state (external_updates-{slug}), AI Engine's list of settings backups (mwai_settings_backups; the list had the prefix mwai_settings_backup_) and MainWP Child's request ID clean-up (mainwp_child_request_ids_cleanup; the list had mainwp_child_request_id_) did the same from wp-admin views and AJAX, in the records of why pages learned again on eight Kadence sites over a day. Each time, pages that reach PHP loaded every plugin until a visitor's page learned again, and every checked address was checked again. record_write() now counts an option named only by an MD5 as a record when its value has exactly those two keys (from the hook, or the stored row when it is deleted, matched as stored), RECORD_OPTIONS lists the three others, and names ending in "-" are prefixes too.

= 1.3.175 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached opens every link in the header, the footer and the home page before the rest of the site, also on a network (GitHub issue #1363). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages::MAIN_MAX was 50, and where LiteSpeed Cache's crawler is on (covered()) nothing more was opened: on one single site whose header, footer and home page link to 80 of its pages (7 November 2026, after the 1.3.174 deploy's purge), the home page's own links were left to the crawler, which goes in sitemap order (1,034 addresses, about 41 minutes a pass, one pass per cache copy), and four of them were still uncached 45 minutes later (1.3 to 2.3 s to the first byte, against 0.1 to 0.4 s for cached pages). Eleven Kadence sites link from their header, footer and home page to 11 to 97 addresses (other sites' links included). MAIN_MAX is now 150; where a crawler or WP-Optimize's preload covers the rest, the newest pages and posts are added only until there are 50 pages in all (new COVERED_MAX; main pages beyond it are all kept), so a small site does not open more before the crawler starts. A network-wide fill (a network update, Network Admin's purge) opened 10 main pages a site without reading home pages, so a network site with 34 header links got the home page and nine of them: it now goes in two rounds, kept in the queue (seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_queue, round): first every site's home page and first header links, as before, so every site gets them in one round (#1240); then each site's other main pages, its home page read for its links as on a single site, and its newest pages and posts up to 25 in all. network() checks room for a site's whole list (MAIN_MAX in the second round), so the queue's 1,000-address limit never cuts one, and wp seoprostack keep-cached --network lists both rounds.

SEOProStack_Used_Css_Match::added() counted words in the page's running scripts (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Scripts, #1260) as classes and elements but never as IDs, so an ID rule was kept only when the served HTML had the ID. Readabler's readabler.min.js, which runs as the page loads, builds its voice navigation panel in code (const e="mdp-readabler-voice-navigation"; t.id=e; t.setAttribute("aria-hidden","true")), and only #mdp-readabler-voice-navigation{position:fixed;…} and #mdp-readabler-voice-navigation[aria-hidden=true]{display:none} place and hide it: on one network subsite's home page (7 November 2026, 1.3.173, desktop 1280 px, the full stylesheets blocked), the panel and its command list sat below the footer, 23,801 px tall, until readabler.min.css arrived, and the screenshot check's footer shot moved up 17 px. added() now counts an #id the HTML lacks as present when it is a word in those scripts, as for classes; scripts that wait for the visitor are still not read. On that page this keeps at most 70 more rules, 11.9 KB before compression, of Readabler's 93.7 KB stylesheet.

= 1.3.173 =
* Fixed: on a multisite network, the page cache was purged on every request after an update whose files kept the release zip's times (GitHub issue #1351). The release zip gives every file the time 2020-01-01 (scripts/build-release.sh, reproducible builds) and scripts/deploy-sites.sh extracted it with unzip, which keeps it; PHP's OPcache revalidates a file only when its time changes, so web requests went on running the previous version's code (one 11-site network, 7 November 2026: assets linked with ver=1.3.171 for over 20 minutes after the 1.3.172 rollout, the web pool's OPcache started 04:29 UTC and never restarted, seoprostack.php dated 2020-01-01 on disk and in the cache). SEOProStack_Page_Cache::after_update() on wp_loaded then saw the other side's version in seoprostack_page_cache_purged on each request: WP-CLI and the host's cron runner (new code) purged everything and stored the new version, the next uncached web request (old code) purged everything and stored the old one, so warmed pages were wiped minutes later. Single sites recovered after a purge or two, as LiteSpeed Cache's purge from a web request also resets OPcache; since LiteSpeed Cache 7.9 it does that on a network only for a network admin. after_update() now purges and stores only when the running version is newer than the stored one (version_compare) or none is stored, so older code leaves both alone (after going back to an older version, purge by hand). swap_plugin_folder() in scripts/lib/deploy-remote.sh gives the extracted files the current time (find -exec touch -c) before the swap, as WordPress's updater does, so OPcache loads the new code within opcache.revalidate_freq and assets versioned with filemtime() (form keys, form modal, brand icons, admin dark mode and others), which kept ver=1577836800 across releases, get new addresses. On the affected network, touching the plugin's files had web requests on 1.3.172 within 2 seconds and the stored version held.

= 1.3.168 =
* Fixed: Keep pages cached filled a copy of each page no browser gets when the page cache keeps a separate WebP copy (GitHub issue #1324). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::request() asked with Accept: text/html,application/xhtml+xml. LiteSpeed Cache with WebP replacement on adds +webp to its cache vary when Accept holds image/webp (or the user agent is Lighthouse, Googlebot, iPhone OS 14+, Safari 16.4+ on a Mac or Firefox 65+), and WP-Optimize with WebP images on (not its redirect method) saves the page as <file>.webp when Accept holds image/webp (wpo_is_browser_supports_webp()). Every current browser sends image/webp when it opens a page, so on LiteSpeed the desktop copy it filled was one no desktop browser gets (its iPhone request already got the WebP copy), and on WP-Optimize neither copy was. Live check on a multisite network with WebP replacement on (LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.1, 6 November 2026, 1.3.166), straight to the server after Keep pages cached had filled the site: 10 of 11 linked pages were a miss for desktop Chrome's Accept and a hit for Keep pages cached's. New constant SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::ACCEPT, Chrome's Accept for a page (text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,image/avif,image/webp,image/apng,*/*;q=0.8), for every request, as LiteSpeed Cache's own crawler asks with image/webp (image/avif when set to AVIF) and skips the plain copy. Where the page cache keeps no WebP copy, it ignores Accept, so the same copy is filled as before; the plain copy is not filled as well, as no current browser gets it.
* Faster: Image loading's preload of a Kadence Blocks Pro Advanced Slider's first slide picture (GitHub issue #1312), or of a hero row's picture without smaller copies (#1204), names LiteSpeed Cache's WebP or AVIF copy when LiteSpeed names it in the slide's or row's CSS (GitHub issue #1326). SEOProStack_Image_Loading::preload() keeps the address and tag it printed when no copy of SEO Pro Stack's is named (SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::first_copy()) and LiteSpeed Cache is loaded; litespeed_next_gen() (litespeed_buffer_after 5) then swaps the tag's address for <address>.avif or .webp, named as Media::replace_webp() names it, when the page names that copy, so it follows what LiteSpeed did on that request: logged-in visitors, pages it does not cache and browsers without WebP keep the picture's own address. Kadence Blocks writes a row's background picture into the page's CSS (.kb-row-layout-id<uniqueID>), so browsers fetched every row's picture as soon as the row was laid out, wherever it was; backgrounds have no lazy loading of their own. New SEOProStack_Kadence_Rows (includes/class-seoprostack-kadence-rows.php, loaded by Image loading with Kadence Blocks, front end only; render_block_kadence/rowlayout 30, front-end pages after wp, not feeds, embeds, REST or AJAX).
