* Fixed: after a Kadence Blocks Pro Advanced Slider, the rest of the post content lost wptexturize()'s curly quotes, dashes and ellipses (GitHub issue #1331). The script SEOProStack_Kadence_Slider prints before the slider (1.3.166, GitHub issue #1312) had a < (for (i = 0; i < s.length; i++)). wptexturize() runs on the content after the blocks are rendered and splits it at each <, so it read < s.length; …</script> as one tag: it turned every & up to the next > into &#038; and, with the real </script> inside that false tag, kept script on its stack of tags it leaves alone until the next </script>. The slider's script had no & after the <, so it still ran; #1329's first draft, with &&, failed with a SyntaxError. Scripts SEO Pro Stack prints in a post's content (the slider's and #1329's) now have no < (loops use Array.prototype.forEach.call()), as their docblocks say. Test site: each script through wptexturize() between two quoted paragraphs comes out unchanged and the paragraph after it gets curly quotes; a page with Kadence rows and a paragraph with straight quotes after them shows curly ones.

= 1.3.167 =
* New, off by default: Hreflang codes with the site's country (Content; SEOProStack_Hreflang_Country, settings hreflang_country and hreflang_country_code; GitHub issue #1313). TranslatePress makes each language's hreflang code from its locale (en_GB → en-GB, es_MX → es-MX), so a Jersey site's pages said they were for the United Kingdom and a Californian site's Spanish pages that they were for Mexico. With a two-letter ISO country set, a trp_hreflang filter at priority 20 replaces the code's country part (a last part of two capital letters or three digits) with the site's, in the head (TRP_Url_Converter::add_hreflang_to_head()) and TranslatePress SEO Pack's sitemap, which use the same filter. Language-only codes and locales without a country (lv, et) are kept, formality suffixes are dropped as TranslatePress does (de_DE_formal), and where two languages with different codes would get the same one (en_GB and en_US, or fr_FR and fr_CA beside fr_BE on a Belgian site), both keep TranslatePress's. Codes are worked out once a request from trp_settings and again after it or SEO Pro Stack's settings are saved. Live check on a nine-language site with JE (6 November 2026): en-GB, pt-PT, pl-PL, fr-FR, ro-RO and cs-CZ became en-JE, pt-JE, pl-JE, fr-JE, ro-JE and cs-JE; nrf-JE, lv, et and the language-only codes stayed. On a 14-language demonstration site with BE, only en-GB and it-IT changed; every French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Dutch variant kept its own.

= 1.3.166 =
* Faster: Image loading loads a Kadence Blocks Pro Advanced Slider's slide pictures as the slides come up, and preloads the first slide's picture when a post or page opens with the slider (GitHub issue #1312). Kadence Blocks Pro 2.9.0 writes each slide's picture into the slider's CSS (.wp-block-kadence-slider .kb-slide-<id> .kb-advanced-slide-inner-wrap) and Splide keeps every slide rendered, so browsers fetched every slide's picture with the page, at low priority, competing with the first slide's, which was also found only after the CSS: on one Kadence home page opening with a 17-slide fade slider, 28 pictures and 4.9 MB of the page's 5.4 MB, PageSpeed mobile 75 with LCP 15.3 s on the first slide (6 November 2026, 1.3.164). SEOProStack_Image_Loading::kadence_preload() now also preloads, with fetchpriority high, the first slide's picture when the content opens with kadence/slider, its first inner block is a kadence/slide with a normal background (no gradient, which replaces the picture) and Kadence Blocks Pro is active (its AVIF or WebP copy with Copies at their own addresses), and counts it as the page's high-priority picture and the first of Load straight away, as for a row. New SEOProStack_Kadence_Slider (render_block_kadence/slider 30, front-end pages only, when the slider and first slide unique IDs are letters, digits, - and _, and another slide has a picture): before each slider, a style (data-no-optimize) and a script (SEOProStack_Kadence_Hero::SCRIPT_ATTRS, once a page) that adds sps-slides to <html> as the page is read. Under it every slide's .kb-advanced-slide-inner-wrap except the first slide's (by its kb-slide-<id> class, so Splide's loop clones of it keep it) has background-image none until Splide gives its slide is-active or is-visible; once the first slider's first picture is in (an Image() of the address its slide computes) or the page has loaded, sps-slides-near also lets is-next, is-prev and the slide after each active or visible one fetch theirs. One second after load, a slider with no is-active slide (Splide not started, in a closed tab, or its classes renamed) gets sps-slides-all and every picture. Without JavaScript nothing waits; browsers without :not() selector lists drop the rules. Filter seoprostack_kadence_slider_wait. Rows and sliders hidden by WordPress 6.9's Block visibility (metadata.blockVisibility) or shown on a Kadence Blocks Pro condition (kadenceConditional) are no longer preloaded. With Copies at their own addresses, Kadence Blocks Pro's head CSS (kadence_blocks_pro_css) names AVIF and WebP copies with image-set(), as Kadence Blocks' does. That home page with the style, script and preload injected, on a throttled phone (Playwright, 1.6 Mbps, 150 ms, 4x CPU): the first slide's picture requested at 0.26 s and the LCP at 4.9 s, the second slide's requested at 4.9 s, then the others about 3 s apart, with the autoplay; page load 17.9 s instead of 29.7 s with all 17 pictures at 0.48 s; every slide shown had its picture; with the classes renamed, the rest loaded at load plus one second; no console errors. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.6.0, Kadence Blocks 3.9.0, Kadence Blocks Pro 2.9.0, that page's content): the preload first in the head and the style and script just before the slider; the first slide's picture requested at 0.18 s and the LCP, then each next slide's one ahead; with Copies at their own addresses and a WebP copy of the first slide's picture, the preload and the slide's image-set() named the same .jpg.webp, requested once; no PHP messages from SEO Pro Stack.

= 1.3.165 =
* New, on by default: Load jQuery at the end of the page (Speed; SEOProStack_Jquery_Footer, setting jquery_footer; GitHub issue #1307). WordPress registers jquery-core and jquery-migrate in the head, and a footer script's dependencies keep their own group, so one footer script that needs jQuery prints it in the head as a synchronous, render-blocking script: the Kadence theme's kadence-shop-spinner with WooCommerce, and PropertyHive's search scripts (on one Kadence shop after Used CSS, jquery.min.js was the largest render-blocking request left, 601 ms on mobile, 6 November 2026, 1.3.163). At wp_head 8 (after the styles, before the head scripts), when the queue already needs WordPress's own jquery-core, jquery, jquery-core and jquery-migrate get group 1 and a marker is printed; WordPress's dependency order still prints them in the head when a head script needs them, and otherwise just before the first footer script that does. An output buffer (template_redirect, PHP_INT_MAX) then moves jQuery's tags, with their inline scripts, back to the marker, with a comment saying why, when anything between it and jQuery's new place might use jQuery: inline code naming jQuery, Google Tag Manager, a same-site script outside wp-includes, SEO Pro Stack, the Kadence theme (and a child theme of it) and the plugins Used CSS handles plus Burst Statistics and SEO Pro Stack's sister plugins (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::on_site() and script_file()), or a script from another site without async or defer; deferred, module, JSON, template and delayed scripts are skipped. Not for logged-in users, the cart, checkout and account pages, previews, feeds, embeds, the Customizer, AJAX, REST or AMP; filter seoprostack_jquery_footer. Turning it on or off empties the page cache. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence, WooCommerce 11.2.2, Fluent Forms; a footer script needing jQuery): jQuery at the end of the home and form pages, and in the head with the setting off, logged in, on the shop (WooCommerce's deferred head scripts need it), with a head script needing it, inline jQuery code, a synchronous foreign script, an unsupported same-site script or Google Tag Manager before it; the jQuery-dependent code ran in every case, with no console errors.

= 1.3.164 =
* Faster: Old slugs checks one post's old slugs with two indexed queries on a slug change (GitHub issue #1289). SEOProStack_Old_Slugs::redundant() filtered with an OR across wp_postmeta and wp_posts, so MySQL used neither index and read every _wp_old_slug row on each post_updated (170 ms a call with 7,436 old slugs, during an import that publishes thousands of posts, 6 November 2026). For one post it now reads this post's old slugs (post_id) and other posts' old slugs equal to its slug (meta_key and meta_value), sharing one batch limit; the sweep query for all posts is unchanged.
* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first handles SEO Pro Stack's own front-end stylesheets (GitHub issue #1303). SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::sources() adds SEOPROSTACK_DIR. A block rendered in wp_footer after priority 1 (on one Kadence site, an Iframe block in a modal printed in the footer) enqueues its style after WordPress's last wp_maybe_inline_styles() pass, so #1257's inlining missed it and wp_hoist_late_printed_styles() (WordPress 6.9 and later, classic themes) hoisted it into the head as the one render-blocking stylesheet Used CSS left (seoprostack-iframe-style, 6 November 2026, 1.3.163).

Api.js's onload callback (cfturnstileOnload) renders the queued widgets.
