= 1.3.162 =
* Fix: Scheduled tasks no longer fail with "The cron event list could not be saved" when another request already saved the same change (GitHub issue #1270, review of #1234). SEOProStack_Cron_List merges this request's change into the stored cron list; when the merged list equals the stored one, update_option() changes no rows and core leaves this request's cached copy stale, so wp_schedule_event() and wp_unschedule_event() reported could_not_set (seen in 1.3.161's deploy on two busy multisite installs, 6 November 2026). The alloptions, cron and notoptions caches are now dropped in that case, so the next read sees the stored list.
* Fix: Keep pages cached's queue no longer overwrites another request's list of missing options (GitHub issue #1269, review of #1241). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::forget() read, changed and wrote back the notoptions cache; it now deletes it, and WordPress rebuilds it as options are read.
* Fix: Dark mode recolours Kadence's block icons on an accent ground (a selected List View row, a pressed or primary button): their literal blue fills and strokes take the text colour and their white details the ground colour, so they stay visible (GitHub issue #1279, review of #1113).
* Fix: Media in cloud storage no longer leaves WebP and AVIF copies written after a picture's removal local, unrecorded and never uploaded (GitHub issue #1256). When a conversion finishes for a picture recorded as removed, and in every batch for a few removed pictures at a time (paged by ID), heal_late_copies() looks for format copies on disk that the removal record does not list; it brings the picture's files back and starts a new generation, so the batch uploads the copies and removes them with the rest. The same copies are tried once (_seoprostack_storage_healed, removed on uninstall).
* Fix: Ask before licence checks no longer holds requests for the site's own pictures (GitHub issue #1255). SEOProStack_Licence_Calls::is_licence_call() matched "licens" anywhere in the address, so a media file named for a business ("…-Licensed-Plumber.jpg") was held as a licence check and returned http_request_failed; SEO Pro Directory's region import stopped three times in 15 minutes on one site (5 November 2026, 1.3.160). A path ending in jpg, jpeg, png, gif, webp, avif, svg, ico, bmp, tif, tiff, mp4, webm, mov or pdf is no longer a licence call, and exempt_host() now also exempts the hosts of Media in cloud storage's public address and endpoint. An EDD check_license call and a licence form body are still held.
* Fix: Load AI Engine's chatbot on first use (GitHub issue #1268, review of #1261). The loader waits for each held stylesheet (its load or error event, or 3 s, STYLE_WAIT) before the next tag, so the chatbot mounts after its theme stylesheet has loaded or failed, or after 3 s if neither event fires (it may then mount before the stylesheet has loaded). When another script needed mwai_chatbot, so it printed as before, the held mwai_chatbot_theme_ stylesheets are printed at wp_footer 21; before, they were left out and the chatbot showed unstyled. The feature now runs only when the active AI Engine's app/chatbot.js (MWAI_PATH) defines mwaiInitialize, without which a chatbot added after DOMContentLoaded never mounts; the answer is kept per MWAI_VERSION in the autoloaded option seoprostack_ai_engine_later_support (removed on uninstall), so the file is read once per AI Engine version (a file that cannot be read is not kept as an answer: AI Engine is left alone for that request and the read is tried again on a later one). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, AI Engine 3.9.1 with the Default chatbot site-wide): after the first mouse move, chatgpt.css finished before chatbot.js was requested, the chatbot mounted and the console had no errors; with the support option set to false, AI Engine printed its tags as before; a changed MWAI_VERSION was read again (true); with a must-use script depending on mwai_chatbot, chatbot.js and chatgpt.css both printed and the chatbot mounted. AI Engine Pro 3.9.0 on Hostinger defines window.mwaiInitialize in app/chatbot.js.
* Fix: Media in cloud storage revisits a picture kept after one retryable removal failure on its own (GitHub issue #1266). local_result() stored a permanent kept:<signature> mark in _seoprostack_storage_local that only upload --retry-failed (or switching Remove local copies after upload off and on) cleared, so one retryable removal failure (a storage service or transport error; "kept original is in use", storage_original_busy, never wrote a mark and already retried on the next pass) left the picture's local files in place and held SEO Pro Directory's import drain until its one-hour no-progress stop (41 minutes on one picture, 6 November 2026). The mark is now kept:<signature>:<time> (kept_timed_mark()); kept_now() counts it as kept for KEPT_RETRY (10 minutes), and removable(), remove_local_now() and remove_local_many() check the picture again after that. The timed mark is written with one conditional UPDATE (record_timed_mark()) so a restore's permanent mark is never overwritten, and next_due() wakes the batch when the earliest timed mark expires (timed_mark_wake()). Marks set by a mismatch, a change or a restore (removal_stopped(), prepare_removal(), restore) stay permanent; --retry-failed and switching removal on drop both kinds (forget_kept_marks()).
* Faster: SEO Pro Stack's own small front-end stylesheets (form keys, post reactions, iframe block) are printed in the page instead of render-blocking links (GitHub issue #1257). Each has a path (wp_style_add_data), so WordPress's wp_maybe_inline_styles() inlines it within styles_inline_size_limit; the reactions inline colour rules stay with it. Admin and block editor unchanged.

= 1.3.161 =
* New, on by default: Load AI Engine's chatbot on first use (Speed; SEOProStack_Ai_Engine_Later, setting ai_engine_later; GitHub issue #1261). With a site-wide chatbot (botId), AI Engine Pro 3.9.0's Meow_MWAI_Modules_Chatbot::register_scripts() enqueues mwai_chatbot (app/chatbot.js, 349 KB, in the head, depending on wp-element, so React and ReactDOM too), mwai_highlight when syntax highlighting is on, and mwai_chatbot_theme_<id> on every page; on two Kadence sites they led Lighthouse's render-blocking requests (mobile 60 and 69, LCP 7.2 s and 7.5 s, estimated saving 3 to 5 s, 5 November 2026). At wp_enqueue_scripts PHP_INT_MAX, and at wp_footer 19 for a chatbot a shortcode or block enqueued, those queued handles (styles by the mwai_chatbot_theme_ prefix) are dequeued; at wp_footer 21, after wp_print_footer_scripts, WP_Styles::do_items() and WP_Scripts::do_items() print their tags, with the dependencies not yet printed, inline scripts and translations, into a buffer that goes into the page as JSON (JSON_HEX_TAG, so Used CSS, Delay scripts and LiteSpeed leave the tags alone). A loader (data-cfasync false, data-no-defer, data-no-optimize) adds the styles, then the scripts in order (each file after the one before loads), on the first pointerdown, mousemove, touchstart, keydown, wheel or scroll, or when a .mwai-chatbot-container that is not a popup, or a .mwai-discussions-container, comes within 600 px of the screen, and then calls window.mwaiInitialize(), which mounts each container once (data-mwai-initialized). If another script needed mwai_chatbot, it loaded as before and nothing is printed. Not in the admin, feeds, embeds, the Customizer, AJAX, REST or AMP requests; filter seoprostack_ai_engine_later. In the Recommended speed settings while AI Engine or AI Engine Pro is active.
* New, on by default: Lighter Tutor LMS pages (Speed; SEOProStack_Tutor_Light, setting tutor_light; GitHub issue #1258). Tutor LMS and Tutor LMS Pro 4.2.0 enqueue their styles and scripts on every page (TUTOR\Assets on wp_enqueue_scripts), with React, ReactDOM and moment through tutor-script's dependencies, and the Kadence theme adds kadence-tutorlms whenever Tutor is active: seven render-blocking stylesheets and about fifteen scripts on Kadence home pages with no Tutor content (PageSpeed, 11 of 15 measured home pages, 5 November 2026). At wp_enqueue_scripts PHP_INT_MAX, styles and scripts whose file is in the tutor or tutor-pro plugin folder, and kadence-tutorlms, are dequeued unless is_tutor_page(): a query var Tutor registers (course_subpage, lesson_video, tutor_dashboard_page, tutor_dashboard_sub_page, tutor_profile_username), a single page, archive or taxonomy of the post types tutor() names, tutor_utils()' dashboard, learning area, course list or course details check, a page named in tutor_option (*_page and *_page_id), or a post whose content, or a synced pattern in it (two levels, ten references; a post with more keeps them), has a [tutor_…] shortcode or a tutor…/ block, and every page while Tutor's Tutor Course widget (tutor_course_widget) is in a widget area; filter seoprostack_tutor_light_page. A Tutor shortcode or block that renders anyway (pre_do_shortcode_tag, pre_render_block) enqueues them again for the footer. Left alone for logged-in people (Tutor's toolbar items, Tutor Pro's push notification prompt on the front page and Utils::handle_flash_message() need its scripts) and in the admin. In the Recommended speed settings while Tutor LMS is active. Load plugins only where needed kept Tutor on every page; fixed in GitHub issue #1264 (next entry).
* Faster: Load plugins only where needed tries skipping Tutor LMS and Tutor LMS Pro on each checked address, like other plugins that add to pages (GitHub issue #1264). The learned map noted tutor/tutor.php as always, content, pages, login, bar and tutor-pro/tutor-pro.php as content, pages, login (Tutor LMS and Tutor LMS Pro 4.2.0, a live Kadence site, 6 November 2026), and front_propose() leaves out every plugin noted always or login, so Kadence home pages without Tutor content loaded both on every uncached view. New FRONT_INERT_CALLBACKS lists callbacks that do nothing on the page views that skip plugins (SEOProStack_Plugin_Loader::front_request(): GET of index.php, not the REST API), which front_notes() no longer notes: determine_current_user TUTOR\RestAuth::api_auth (it changes the user only when RestAuth::is_tutor_api_request() sees a Tutor REST route), wp_login TUTOR\User::update_user_last_login and set_timezone, login_form TUTOR\User::add_timezone_input and TutorPro\Auth\SpamProtection::extend_login_form, authenticate SpamProtection::auth_check, wp_authenticate_user TUTOR_PRO\EmailVerification::check_status and TutorPro\Auth\_2FA::check_login, registration_errors SpamProtection::handle_registration_spam_protection. Logins and registrations are POST requests, which load every plugin; an address whose login form shows Tutor's fields renders differently without it and keeps it. Any other callback on those hooks counts as before. New callback_id(), used by woo_can_skip() too. Checked addresses then still differed with and without Tutor, so the page kept it; with Lighter Tutor LMS pages on, a page that is not Tutor's, viewed logged out, now also leaves out what Tutor added beyond its own files: the Kadence theme's Tutor rules ("/* Kadence Tutor CSS */", course and course archive headers) in kadence-global's inline CSS (kadence_dynamic_css callbacks of Kadence\TutorLMS\Component removed at wp_enqueue_scripts 1), jquery-ui-sortable and jquery-touch-punch that TUTOR\Assets::frontend_scripts() queues while should_load_legacy_scripts() (jQuery and jQuery UI core and mouse with them; dequeued at priority 11 only if they were not queued before priority 9, so a plugin that wants them keeps them; put back with Tutor's files when a Tutor shortcode or block renders) and the tutor-lms body class. On a test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence, Tutor LMS 4.2.0) the home page and a plain page are byte-identical with and without Tutor and verify_front() reports verified with Tutor skipped; a course, the courses archive, the dashboard and the registration page keep it.
* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first covers every plugin SEO Pro Stack recommends that puts stylesheets on pages, and keeps the rules for classes the page's scripts add (GitHub issue #1260, owner's decision widening #1194). SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::PLUGINS adds Kadence's other plugins (Conversions, Shop Kit, Galleries, Reading Time, Related Content, Creative Kit), Fluent's and WPManageNinja's (Forms add-ons, Booking, Cart, Community, Support, CRM, Boards, Affiliate, Player, Paymattic, Ninja Tables, Ninja Charts, Ninja Job Board, WP Social Ninja), Tutor LMS (Pro), AI Engine (Pro), Complianz (premium, Terms & Conditions), TranslatePress (Business), WooCommerce with its recommended Stripe and PayPal plugins, SliceWP, Rank Math (Pro), Code Block Pro, ICS Calendar, EventON Lite, Easy Video Reviews, Custom Feed for TikTok, Simple Cloudflare Turnstile, Syndication Links, Webmention, IndieWeb, Readabler and WP Grid Builder, with a child theme of Kadence; folder names checked against 37 Hostinger sites. Five November PageSpeed runs found Tutor, Syndication Links, Readabler, IndieWeb, Webmention, AI Engine, Fluent Cart and TranslatePress stylesheets holding up the first paint on Kadence home pages. New SEOProStack_Used_Css_Scripts: every word in the page's scripts that could be a class or element name counts as present (as PurgeCSS does), and so does every class starting with a prefix a script builds names from ("name-" + x, `name-${x}`, three characters or more), plus Interactivity API data-wp-class-- directives. Scripts are those on the page with an address on this site (src, or a data-…src a consent plugin moved it to), up to 5 MB, read once per version in 256 KB parts into uploads/seoprostack-used-css/js-*.json, and inline code up to 1 MB. A pilot with only the used CSS showed a list bullet and padding on a Kadence Blocks row background slider: kb-splide-init.js adds kb-splide, which CLASSES lacked; Kadence Blocks Pro's AOS animations (aos-init, aos-animate) and Fluent Forms' conditional fields were in the same gap. CLASSES stays as the baseline. Recommended speed settings recommends it where the Kadence theme or any of these plugins is active; AGENTS.md's rule names the list.
* Faster: Media in cloud storage keeps each kept original's inventory in its own table instead of one wp_options row per picture (GitHub issue #1252). remember_original(), delete_original(), finish_original(), original_counts() and original_jobs() used seoprostack_storage_job_original_<sha256(original_signature . backup)> options, about 1 KB each: 9,522 rows, 10 MB on a directory stack with about 9,000 pictures (5 November 2026, 1.3.159). They are now rows of {prefix}seoprostack_media_originals (name binary(32), the same SHA-256; job longtext, the serialized inventory), written by original_add() (INSERT IGNORE, so a deletion tombstone or keep marker is never overwritten by an uploader), original_put() (the tombstone and keep marker) and original_remove(), and read by original_get() and original_names(), which pages the table's primary key for the same originalcursor (the names sort as the hashes do) and merges options while some remain. run_batch() calls build_originals() for up to 5 seconds a batch, while index_due() keeps batches coming: it creates the table and moves the options 100 at a time (INSERT IGNORE, but a tombstone replaces a row an uploader wrote meanwhile; then one DELETE), reading the option until this version has moved them. A failed write or read sends inventories back to options and retries after ten minutes. wp seoprostack media-storage url-index now moves both the URL index and these inventories; status reports table rows and option rows left. Uninstall drops the table; seoprostack_storage_job_originalsstate goes with the other jobs. The per-use seoprostack_storage_job_originaluse_* rows stay options.

= 1.3.160 =
* Faster: Media in cloud storage's URL index moves out of wp_options into its own table (GitHub issue #1251). upload_finish() and publish_handover() wrote update_option(URL_INDEX . hash('sha256', signature . key), $id, false) for every stored file, and indexed_map() and reverse_match() read them with get_option(), so wp_options gained about 12 rows per picture and every query that scans it paid for them. Seen on a directory stack on 5 November 2026 (1.3.158, about 4,600 listings and 9,000 pictures, import running): 118,302 wp_options rows, of which 107,444 seoprostack_storage_url_* and 9,017 seoprostack_storage_job_*; Rank Math's sitemap cache clear (DELETE … WHERE option_name LIKE '_transient_sitemap_%', on every saved post) took 456 ms each. The index is now the {prefix}seoprostack_media_urls table (url binary(32), the same SHA-256; post_id, indexed), written by the new url_store() (INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE, 200 rows a query) and read by url_lookup() (primary key, cached for the request). run_batch() calls build_urls() for up to 5 seconds a batch, while index_due() keeps batches coming: it creates the table, moves the options 500 at a time (one INSERT, then one DELETE and their cached copies) and, once a table is created, adds every inventory's current files with INSERT IGNORE, which refills one that was dropped. Until this version has moved the options, a lookup the table misses reads the option, and url_store() deletes the option it supersedes. A failed write or lookup (a dropped table) sends lookups and writes back to options and retries the build after ten minutes; options written meanwhile are newer, so the move replaces table rows with them. Deleting an attachment deletes its rows (the options were never removed). New WP-CLI action wp seoprostack media-storage url-index runs the build to the end now; status reports table rows and option rows left. Uninstall drops the table; seoprostack_storage_job_urls (its state) goes with the other jobs. Checked on a Docker test site with 3,000 index options: every lookup matched before, during and after the move (0.1 ms each); the move took 0.7 s; a write during the move won over its old option; after the table was dropped, lookups fell back, a write went to an option and the rebuild restored it and the inventory's files. The per-picture seoprostack_storage_job_original_* rows (kept originals' inventories) stay in wp_options for now.
* Developers: sync starter core files to 1.0.52 (2580267). scripts/deploy-sites.sh --apply runs each site's WP-Cron events whose callbacks are in the plugin's folder once after its settings-upgrade load (cron=N/M; a PHP fatal error, database error or non-zero exit fails the install; DEPLOY_CRON_TIMEOUT, default 120 s), fetches a home that answered 5xx or not at all once more after 5 seconds, counts an unfinished fetch as no answer and reports cache=redirected:HOST when a redirect dropped the unique query; scripts/update-test.sh waits up to 2 minutes for GitHub to list a new release and checks a missing offer 3 more times. All 83 core files match the starter (GitHub issue #1250).

= 1.3.159 =
* Fix: Keep pages cached, purge delivery and page checks go straight to this server behind a CDN when LiteSpeed Cache's Server IP is empty (GitHub issue #1243). SEOProStack_Litespeed_Crawler::learn() (shutdown, hooked by Hosting needs' boot whether it is on or not) notes this server's public IPv4 (SERVER_ADDR) on a web request to a LiteSpeed server in SEO Pro Stack's own autoloaded option seoprostack_origin_ip, written only when the address changes; it looks nothing up, so a visitor's request never waits. SEOProStack_Litespeed_Crawler::learned() checks a newly noted address the first time SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() needs it (from WP-Cron or WP-CLI): it is used only when the site's host name resolves elsewhere and reaches_server() finds LiteSpeed itself answering a HEAD request for the home page pinned to it (no cf-ray); an address that fails is checked again after an hour. resolver() uses LiteSpeed Cache's Server IP when set, else the learned address, so runs from WP-CLI and a host's cron job, which have no SERVER_ADDR, connect straight to the server. LiteSpeed Cache's settings are never written. After a request pinned to the learned address, settle() (http_api_debug) forgets it when cURL could not connect (error 7, or 28 "Connection timed out") or the answer has a cf-ray header; a slow page that timed out after connecting keeps it. reaches_server() now uses the home URL's own port and fails when the request was not pinned (no cURL transport), so the Hosting needs fix is not offered on a CDN's answer either. The option is deleted on uninstall. Evidence: on a Hostinger account with 25 installs behind Cloudflare, 24 had Server IP empty and 21 of 30 requests through Cloudflare in six minutes were refused with 403, while requests straight to the server answered every time.

= 1.3.158 =
* Fix: no fatal error on a single site from SEOProStack_Litespeed::server_ip() (GitHub issue #1248). 1.3.157 (#1245) started it with !ms_is_switched(), which WordPress defines in wp-includes/ms-blogs.php, loaded by wp-settings.php on multisite only, so every call on a single site was "Call to undefined function ms_is_switched()": SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() (every Keep pages cached batch and five-minute check, Load plugins only where needed's page checks, SEOProStack_Litespeed::deliver_purge()) and SEOProStack_Litespeed_Crawler::origin() (Hosting needs' LiteSpeed advice in Site Health and the Server tab). It now checks is_multisite() first. Networks were unaffected. Seen on a Hostinger account after deploying 1.3.157: on single sites the cache was not filled again after the deploy's purge (1 page in 30 cached half an hour later, home page included), and WP-Cron run by the host's cron job stopped at that event in each run, with nothing in the site's PHP error log.

= 1.3.157 =
* Fix: Server IP is read as LiteSpeed Cache applies it on a network set to Use Primary Site Configuration (GitHub issue #1245). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() (Keep pages cached, Load plugins only where needed's page checks, SEOProStack_Litespeed::deliver_purge()) and SEOProStack_Litespeed_Crawler::origin() (Hosting needs) read SEOProStack_Litespeed::conf('server_ip'): the network option, else the site's own. With litespeed.conf.use_primary_settings on, LiteSpeed Cache's Conf::_try_load_site_options() loads the main site's options over a subsite's (all but Base::$single_site_options, which do not include server_ip), so a subsite's own value is ignored and usually empty. The new SEOProStack_Litespeed::server_ip() returns apply_filters('litespeed_conf', 'server_ip') (LiteSpeed Cache's API, src/api.cls.php, with its constant, primary site and network rules) while LiteSpeed Cache is loaded and the request has not switched site; after switch_to_blog(), where LiteSpeed Cache still holds the first site's settings (it has no switch_blog handling), it reads the main site's option when the network uses primary settings and the site is not the main site, else conf(). Seen on an 11-site network with the main site's Server IP set: on a subsite whose own option was '', LiteSpeed Cache's conf('server_ip') and the filter returned the address while resolver() returned null, so its page checks and fills went through Cloudflare, whose origin refusals (#1242) failed them, while a subsite with its own value set went straight to the server.

= 1.3.156 =
* Faster: Load plugins only where needed's page checks wait for Keep pages cached, and take shorter turns on a network run from the command line (GitHub issue #1242). SEOProStack_Front_Verify::run() returns, leaving its queue and schedule, while filling() finds a SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::HOOK batch scheduled with URLs queued or a full fill pending, and starts checks for NETWORK_RUN_BUDGET (15) seconds instead of RUN_BUDGET (50) when 'cli' === PHP_SAPI && is_multisite(); a check once started still runs to its end. Each update of SEO Pro Stack learns again and its deploy purges every site, so Keep pages cached's own requests queued up to 50 addresses a site, and each check renders its address uncached several times on the server that is filling the cache. On an 11-site network run by a sequential cron runner that starts no site after 240 seconds, seoprostack_front_verify took 89.8 of a subsite's 113 seconds of scheduled tasks (Keep pages cached 22.9, the other nine due events under 1), and a cycle reached 3 sites in 243 seconds. Checked on that network: with 15 pages waiting to be filled, the event ran in 0.01 seconds and kept its queue and next run; with none, it ran checks.
* Fix: Load plugins only where needed no longer saves an address as needing every plugin when the server refused the check (GitHub issue #1242). render() returned retry => false for every status other than 200, so save() stored a 403 as a result (set -1) for up to a week. Answers in SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::REFUSED (403, 429, 503) now return retry and refused: a refused first render, or a refused trial (which check() had treated as a difference, halving the group and rendering on), ends the check as failed with retry, so the address waits for its next visitor and five in a row pause checks for an hour (FAILURES). render() also sends its request through SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() (LiteSpeed Cache's Server IP, when set and the page cache is LiteSpeed's), as Keep pages cached's requests and SEOProStack_Litespeed::deliver_purge() do. Seen on a Hostinger network behind Cloudflare: for minutes at a time the origin's LiteSpeed answered the server's own requests through Cloudflare with "403 Forbidden: Access to this resource on the server is denied!" (x-turbo-charged-by: LiteSpeed) on every site and user agent (21 of 30 samples over six minutes), while the same requests straight to the server answered every time; the last check on four of the network's sites had failed with status 403, and all 10 addresses queued for a test on its main site were saved as needing every plugin.

= 1.3.155 =
* Fix: Keep pages cached keeps a fill queued when the cache is emptied, or a post is saved, while a batch runs (GitHub issue #1240). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::plan() and done() re-read the queue to keep other requests' changes, but get() used get_option(), which returns the request's own cached copy of the non-autoloaded seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_queue: a purge in another request during a batch (up to 90 seconds from the command line since #1236) wrote the database, and the batch's done() deleted the option or wrote its own copy back, dropping the new fill (full) and any re-queued pages. hold() then counted the check's page held again, so the five-minute check never saw the cache emptied and the site stayed unfilled until the next outside emptying. Seen live on the main site of an 11-site network: the 1.3.154 deploy purged it (wp litespeed-purge blog 1) during the cron runner's batch from 05:06:26 to 05:07:56; done() at 05:07:53 left no queue, and at 05:39 24 of its 25 first pages were misses taking 1 to 5 seconds. The new private forget(), called first in get(), deletes the option from the 'options' cache group and from notoptions, so every read, and update_option()'s comparison with the old value, uses the stored queue (no persistent object cache on the sites seen; with one, the next read comes from the database and is cached again). Checked on a single site: a probe request reading an option, a database write from elsewhere, and a second get_option() returned the old value; after forget() the new one. With a purge's fill_later() 12 seconds into a 50-page batch, 1.3.154 ended with full=0 and 4 pages left; this version opened the 50 and left full=1 for the next batch.
* Faster: Keep pages cached gives a network's sites other than the main site 20-second batches from the command line again (GitHub issue #1240). SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::run() uses CLI_BUDGET (90) when 'cli' === PHP_SAPI && is_main_site(), else BUDGET (20); the network's fill and the check run on the main site. Networks' WP-Cron is run one site after another (wp site list piped to wp cron event run --due-now, or a runner script), and with sites purged one by one (wp litespeed-purge blog <id>, as deploys and tools do since Empty Entire Cache reaches every site on a hosting account) each site fills on its own. On the 11-site network, a runner that starts no site after 240 seconds got through 5 sites in 353 seconds (one in 142) and skipped the next five-minute cycle, so each site got a turn every 20 to 30 minutes and its other scheduled tasks waited as long. With 20 seconds a site, every site's home, header and footer pages are opened in the first round.

= 1.3.154 =
* Fix: Keep pages cached's network fill plans every site of the network, not only the first (GitHub issue #1238). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages::next_site() narrowed get_sites(array('number' => 1, 'orderby' => 'id', ...)) with a sites_clauses filter (blog_id >= $after), but WP_Site_Query caches results by its query vars and applies sites_clauses only on a cache miss, so every call after the first in a request got the first call's site, with or without a persistent object cache. network() therefore planned the first site (the main site) until MAX stopped it, left the queue at site 1 and full, and each batch opened the main site's pages again: on one 11-site network, 3,085 pages opened over 17 hours and 28 purges without the fill finishing, while subsites stayed uncached until their own purges. next_site() now takes the network's active site IDs from one get_sites() query ('number' => 0, 'network_id' => get_current_network_id(), ordered by ID; cached by those arguments and refreshed when a site changes) and returns the first at or after $after. The same network planned in one run: 191 addresses across all 11 sites in 4.4 seconds. SEOProStack_Security_Headers::cleanup_sites() (Site Health's Really Simple Security leftovers cleanup, 50 sites per request) had the same sites_clauses cursor; with a persistent object cache, every batch after the first could get the first again. It now picks the 50 IDs after $after from the network's IDs in the same way (introduced in GitHub issue #1197, PR #1200).

= 1.3.153 =
* Fix: Fixes for other plugins keeps WordPress's scheduled tasks list (the cron option) from losing changes and piling up copies of recurring tasks (GitHub issue #1232). New SEOProStack_Cron_List (includes/class-seoprostack-cron-list.php, loaded with the fix on). write(), on pre_update_option_cron at PHP_INT_MAX: when the list stored now (read from the database past every cache, one query per write) differs from the request's copy ($old_value), it applies only what the request changed (tasks in its copy but not in the new list removed; tasks new or different in the new list added) to the stored list, in time order, instead of writing the request's whole copy; otherwise the value is written unchanged. The read and the write are not locked, so two writes in the same instant can still race; the window is that of one query. dedupe(), at wp_loaded priority 0 in cron runs (wp_doing_cron(), before wp-cron.php reads the due tasks) and in WP-CLI (wp cron event run): extra copies of a recurring task (same hook, argument key and schedule at more than one time) are removed in one write, keeping the latest, since wp-cron.php writes a task's next time before removing the copy that ran; single events and copies on another schedule are left alone, and nothing is written when there is nothing to remove. AI Engine (checked with 3.9.0): SEOProStack_Plugin_Fixes::mwai_runner_check() (init, priority 14) replaces Meow_MWAI_Modules_Tasks::ensure_cron_scheduled() (init, priority 15, every request) with mwai_runner(), which does the same without its "more than 300 seconds late" branch: the runner for AI Engine's dev_mode (mwai_tasks_internal_dev_run every 5 seconds, else mwai_tasks_internal_run every minute) is scheduled only when missing and the other one cleared; left alone when AI Engine's global $mwai_core cannot be read. Its REST Reset and the dev-mode switch work as before. Evidence (one Hostinger account, 24 installs, all DISABLE_WP_CRON with a server cron job every 5 minutes, WordPress 7.1.4, 5 November 2026): duplicated recurring events on 18 (mwai_tasks_internal_run 2 to 5 on 9 sites, Link Whisper's wpil_action_queue_cron and wpil_email_cron 2 on 12, Freemius fs_data_sync_* 2 on 4, MainWP Child health checks 2 to 3 on 3); on one site, duplicates removed by hand came back twice within 6 minutes, written back by a main-site cron run that took 90 s and wrote the list about 30 times, and a front-end page load cleared and rescheduled AI Engine's runner 2 minutes after the removal (a temporary pre_update_option_cron trace). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, DISABLE_WP_CRON, a must-use copy of AI Engine 3.9.0's scheduling code): a request holding an old copy while another removed Y and added X, then scheduling Z, left X and Z stored (without the fix: Y and Z); a front-end load left a runner 6 minutes late in place (without the fix: rescheduled a minute on); one wp-cron.php run ran the runner once and left one copy a minute on, cut two hourly copies 10 s apart to one, and kept a daily copy of the same hook and two single events; switching dev mode on and off scheduled the matching runner and removed the other; no debug log messages.

= 1.3.152 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached's check for a LiteSpeed cache emptied from outside the site (#1229) no longer misses it right after an update or when a visitor reopens the home page first (GitHub issue #1233). SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::look() opens add_query_arg(CHECK_ARG, '1', home_url('/')) (/?seoprostack-held=1), which LiteSpeed caches apart from the home page and nobody else opens, so a visitor or bot rebuilding the home page after the cache was emptied can no longer leave the check finding it held; where that address does not answer 200 or 201 (redirected, refused), it opens home_url('/') as before. The new private hold() runs at the end of run() when a batch opened pages, nothing was refused and the queue is empty: it opens the check's page, a second time when that request built it, and stores seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_held as held only when LiteSpeed answered from its cache. Before, a fill left nothing stored, so when the cache was emptied between a fill and the first check (seen on Hostinger after the 1.3.151 deploy), that check only recorded "missing" and nothing filled the cache. A page LiteSpeed never caches is never stored as held, so it still never starts a fill. README: LiteSpeed Cache's Default Front Page TTL is a week by default (604800 s), not a day.
* Faster: Keep pages cached's batches run up to 90 seconds from the command line, instead of 20 (GitHub issue #1236). Where WP-Cron is run by the host's cron job (DISABLE_WP_CRON; every five minutes on Hostinger), each batch waits for the next run, so a fill worked 20 seconds in five minutes: a 50-page site took 10 to 30 minutes, and a network of 11 sites, 25 pages each, one to two hours, which network-wide purges restarted from the first site meanwhile (one network's fill was still pending after 15 hours and 26 purges). SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::run() uses the new CLI_BUDGET (90) when 'cli' === PHP_SAPI, for planning (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::plan()) and for opening pages, and BUDGET (20) in a web request. Pages are still opened one at a time; 90 seconds stays under a five-minute cron interval and the aidevops multisite runner's 240-second cycle.
* Fix: cloud storage's removal of local copies treats a storage transport error other than a deadline like a retryable one: the picture stays local and is tried again on the next pass, and repeated transport errors pause removal for five minutes instead of an hour (GitHub issue #1228, PR #1231; includes/features/class-seoprostack-media-storage.php).

= 1.3.151 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached fills a LiteSpeed page cache emptied from outside the site, which runs none of its code, so litespeed_purged_all never fires there: another site on the same hosting account sending Empty Entire Cache (wp litespeed-purge all on a multisite network sends LSWCP_EMPTYCACHE, the * tag, which reaches every site sharing the account's cache), the host's panel or the server. New WP-Cron event seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_check, every 300 s (its own cron_schedules interval, SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::check_schedule()), on a single site or a network's main site with the feature on and a LiteSpeed page cache (checks()); SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::sync_check() schedules or clears it at wp_loaded in the admin (not AJAX), in cron runs and in WP-CLI, so sites whose host runs their cron and that are updated by a deploy script get it without an admin visit, and on seoprostack_setting_saved for keep_pages_cached; deactivate() and uninstall.php clear it. check() does nothing while the queue holds pages or is marked full; otherwise it opens home_url('/') once with SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::open(), as LiteSpeed Cache's crawler, which LiteSpeed answers 201 without running PHP while it holds the page. held() reads 200 or 201 with a hit as held, 200 otherwise as missing, and anything else (redirect, 403, 429, 503, an error) as unknown, which changes nothing. The new option seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_held (held, since; not autoloaded, written only when it changes) keeps the last answer; held then missing calls SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::fill_later(is_multisite()), as litespeed_purged() does, so a network fills every site where the feature is on. A home page LiteSpeed never caches (excluded, a site used only for its admin) or that redirects never starts one; the home page's own TTL running out (LiteSpeed Cache's Default Front Page TTL, a day by default) does, and pages still held answer that fill with 201. WP-Optimize needs no check: its cache folder is emptied only by its own code, which fires wpo_cache_flush, or by its cache lifespan, which its preload covers (GitHub issue #1229). Hostinger LiteSpeed shared hosting, LiteSpeed Cache 7.9.3, SEO Pro Stack 1.3.150, 4 November 2026 and 5 November 00:50 UTC: home pages of separate single sites and of a network's main site on one account were all missing at the same time, with no purge in their own WordPress and their last fills hours earlier; SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::open() answered 200 miss, then 201 hit, on each.

= 1.3.150 =
* Faster: Keep pages cached opens a site's pages in the order visitors reach them (owner's order: header menu, footer menu, home page links, then the rest). SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Pages::of_site() lists the home page; the items of menu locations without "footer" in their slug (menu_urls(), in get_registered_nav_menus() order: Kadence's primary, secondary and mobile), then the links inside the site's header in the home page's HTML; the items of footer locations, then the links inside its footer; the home page's other links, in document order; then the posts page. page_links() reads the home page once per fill (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::page_html(), as open() requests it: LiteSpeed Cache's crawler first, then as a visitor when LiteSpeed answers 201; at most 4 MB) and walks it with WP_HTML_Tag_Processor: the site's header and footer are a header or footer element with the id masthead or colophon, or the class site-header, site-footer or wp-block-template-part; relative links are made absolute and clean() keeps only this site's cacheable pages. MAIN_MAX rises from 20 to 50, and MORE_MAX (50) is gone: where neither LiteSpeed Cache's crawler nor WP-Optimize's preload covers the rest of the site, rest_urls() adds published pages, then posts, then other viewable public post types, newest first, up to MAX (1,000) addresses, reading only the columns permalinks use (permalinks()), not post content. A network fill keeps its 10 main and 25 pages per site without reading each home page. WP-Optimize's purge (SEOProStack_Keep_Pages_Cached::wp_optimize_purged()) no longer schedules wpo_page_cache_run_preload 30 s later: SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::fill_later() marks the queue (preload), and done() schedules it 10 s after the fill ends, as it starts LiteSpeed Cache's crawler (GitHub issue #1224). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.5.2, WP-Optimize 4.7.0 page cache, cron off), a header menu in primary and a footer menu in footer (stored footer first), a header HTML item and footer HTML linking to two pages in no menu, a home page linking to a page, a post, a page already in the menu, an anchor, another host, a search, the login page and a relative address: wp seoprostack keep-cached --dry-run listed /, the three header menu pages, the header's link, the two footer menu pages, the footer's link, the home page's three new links, the posts page, then the other pages and posts, newest first, each once, with no query string, login page or other host. With WP-Optimize's preload off, a purge and one batch opened all 19 pages in that order and WP-Optimize's cache folder then held them; with it on, the preload was not scheduled at the purge and was scheduled when the batch emptied the queue. With 1,100 more posts of about 30 KB each, the list without preload held 1,000 addresses, worked out in 61 ms with 0.9 MB more memory (loading the posts whole for get_permalink() took 64.5 MB); with preload on, 50. Twenty Twenty-Five's header and footer template parts were read too. No debug log messages.
* Faster: Image loading gives the background picture of the Kadence Blocks row a page opens with at the size each screen needs, never less sharp than before (GitHub issue #1221). SEOProStack_Image_Loading::kadence_preload() hands the row it already picks (first block, same background on every device) to the new SEOProStack_Kadence_Hero::start() (includes/class-seoprostack-kadence-hero.php, loaded only then, with SEOProStack_Hero_Picture in includes/class-seoprostack-hero-picture.php for the files). When the picture (bgImgID and bgImg; for a background slider each slide's, up to backgroundSliderCount, the first slide's required) is in its attachment's folder (the end of the address's folder matches the year and month folder of the attachment's file, so a file of the same name elsewhere is not taken for it) and has smaller sizes of the same proportions in its attachment metadata (height within a pixel), whose file is in the uploads folder or mapped to the bucket by Media in cloud storage (wp_get_attachment_image_src(), compared with SEOProStack_Media_Storage::local_url()), it prints, in place of the head's <link rel="preload">, a <style id="seoprostack-hero-css"> with html.sps-hero .kb-row-layout-id{uniqueID}{background-image:none!important} (slider: >.kb-blocks-bg-slider .kb-bg-slide), the old preload of the row's own picture inside <noscript>, and a <script id="seoprostack-hero"> with each picture's proportions and files, narrowest first, ending with the row's own picture (through SEOProStack_Media_Storage::attachment_url()). Each file is width, address, its image-set() of AVIF and WebP copies (new SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::background_set(), when pages name copies) and its first_copy() for the preload. render_block_kadence/rowlayout (priority 30) adds, once, after that row in the_content, window.seoprostackHero&&seoprostackHero(document.currentScript). That reads the row (or its .kb-blocks-bg-slider) box and the first element's computed background-size; for cover or contain it takes the smallest width of at least max (cover) or min (contain) of the box width and its height × width/height of the picture, × devicePixelRatio × 1.1, else the last, sets it with style.setProperty('background-image', url(), 'important') and then the image-set(), appends <link rel="preload" as="image" fetchpriority="high"> with its type, removes the class, and a ResizeObserver on the box, and a change of matchMedia('(resolution: Ndppx)') for the current devicePixelRatio (another display, zoom), swap in a larger file when one is needed, never a smaller one. The head script adds the class only while the document is loading, and removes it at DOMContentLoaded whatever happens, so the row's own picture shows if the script after the row is lost or fails; both scripts carry data-no-optimize, data-no-defer and data-cfasync="false". Not used, so the head's preload stays as before: a bgImgSize other than cover or contain, block themes (wp_is_block_theme(), which render the content before wp_head), a uniqueID other than letters, digits, - and _, addresses that cannot go in CSS's url('…'), pictures with no smaller size, or the seoprostack_kadence_hero_sizes filter returning false. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Kadence 1.5.2, Kadence Blocks 3.8.1.1), pages opening with a two-slide background slider and a normal cover background, each picture a 2048×1536 upload with 300, 768, 1024 and 1536 wide sizes, the row's height from its heading and text; Chromium at 412×823 DPR 1.75 (PageSpeed's phone), 393×852 DPR 3 (iPhone 15), 768×1024 DPR 2 and 1350×940 DPR 1: the 1536 copy at 412 (964 pixels painted) and at 1350 (1,350), the full picture at 393 (1,652) and 768 (1,536, within the 10%), every file shown at least as wide as the pixels painted, one file of the picture fetched each time and never the full one alongside a copy, no console errors. With Copies at their own addresses on, the copy's AVIF was preloaded (type image/avif, fetchpriority high) and shown. JavaScript off: the head's preload (from <noscript>) and the full picture, as before. Widening 412 to 1600 after load replaced the 1536 copy with the full picture, and so did 1350 going from DPR 1 to 2 (the resolution listener then waited for 2dppx). The row's address with another year and month folder, same file name: no copies; on a CDN host with the same folder: copies. background-size auto: the head's preload and the picture as before. A deleted 1536 file was not offered. On a Media in cloud storage test site (MinIO) with local files removed, every copy and the row's own picture had bucket addresses, each answering 200. The 1536 JPEG of the test picture is 336 KB against 563 KB for the full one.
* Faster: SEOProStack_Media_Storage::remove_local_many(array $ids, float $deadline) removes many uploaded pictures' local copies in one pass, for plugins that import pictures with WP-Cron off and wait for them to leave local storage (SEO Pro Directory's import pump). It takes the storage lease once, runs remove_local_now()'s checks on each picture (uploaded, a removal record, not waiting for WebP or AVIF copies, kept after a failed check), checks the files of whole pictures in shared HEAD phases (removal_phase(), as the batch's remove_locals() does), confirms and removes each picture whole with finish_removal(), and purges cached pages once, in its finally. It returns each given ID's answer with remove_local_now()'s meaning (true, false or WP_Error); pictures it does not reach (the lease busy, the deadline, a retryable service error in a phase, removal paused for an hour after another service error) answer false. remove_local_now($id, $deadline) is now remove_local_many(array($id), $deadline)[$id]. Removal HEAD phases, the batch's included, send up to head_requests() requests across pictures: twice seoprostack_media_storage_batch_requests (default 64), at most 128, as a HEAD carries no body. In SEOProStack_S3_Client::heads(), as in put_files(), a HEAD still running when a group given less than 20 seconds reaches the caller's deadline answers storage_budget (the next turn), not storage_transport, so a long call's last phase does not count as a service failure (GitHub issue #1223). Test site (WordPress latest, PHP 8.3, a MinIO bucket with 1.2 s added to each round trip by netem, cron off), two sets of 300 uploaded pictures of 14.8 files each, removal on, 60 seconds each: on 1.3.149, remove_local_now() back to back removed 22 pictures (0.37 a second, 22 page purges); one remove_local_many() call removed 152 (2.53 a second, 6.9 times as many, 1 purge), the other 148 answering false at the deadline. With 150 ms added: 155 against all 300 in 41 s (2.8 times), the local work (each file's SHA-256 checked twice and its removal recorded before it goes) taking most of the time. Five pictures with two untouched, one local file changed, one bucket copy replaced and one bucket copy deleted got the same answers from 1.3.149's remove_local_now() and from remove_local_many(): true, true, storage_kept (kept), storage_kept (kept), storage_http 404 (not kept, removal paused for an hour), every file of the last three left in place; purges 2 against 1.

= 1.3.149 =
* Faster: Watermark pictures, WebP and AVIF images and Media in cloud storage work on different pictures at once (GitHub issue #1196). On 1.3.144 a whole-library mark gave about 4 pictures a minute per site while storage "uploaded" stalled: SEOProStack_Media_Storage::run_batch() returned 0 for as long as a watermark job was open, a watermark part held the media storage lease for its whole 20 seconds, and conversion (newest first) made copies of pictures still queued for marking, which marking then made again.
* Watermark batches: the seoprostack_watermark_lock transient is gone. A part takes a runner slot (lease rows seoprostack_watermark_slot_1 to _N, N from the seoprostack_watermark_runners filter, default 1, at most 8) and takes the media lease per picture, not per part. SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::claim_next() takes the next picture with a compare-and-swap UPDATE on seoprostack_watermark_job (change_job(); queue(), stop() and the counts use it too, so runners, bulk actions and Stop never write over each other), and claims it under the lease with a _seoprostack_watermark_claim post meta row ("{expiry} {token}", 600 s; an expired one is deleted when read). A picture with no mark yet has its removed files brought back under the lease, then is marked without it (add_unleased()); a marked picture is marked again under the lease, as before. mark_locked() and remove_locked() refuse a picture claimed by another request (watermark_busy). Mark new uploads' inline path claims the new upload in place of the _seoprostack_watermark_queued barrier, so the barrier holds with no batch running.
* SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::mark_queued() is true while a picture is claimed, queued, in a running chosen-pictures batch, or after a running Mark every picture batch's cursor with no record (library_cursor(), read past the options cache at most every 5 s).
* Media in cloud storage: run_batch() runs while a watermark job is open (waiting for the lease up to 3 s), and still gives a watermark job with a lost cron event one bounded turn first (GitHub issue #1147), returning straight after it only under a PHP time limit. waiting() leaves out a running library batch's pictures still to be marked in SQL. Local copy removal (prepare_removal()) leaves a claimed picture for a later pass without marking it kept, and Media jobs steps refuse it with watermark_busy, which does not count towards a job's failure stop.
* WebP and AVIF images: the seoprostack_nextgen_lock transient is gone. run_batch() takes a runner slot (seoprostack_nextgen_slot_N, seoprostack_nextgen_runners filter, default 1, at most 8) and claims each picture with a lease row (seoprostack_nextgen_claim_{ID}) before converting it, checking again that no other batch converted it. Pictures for which mark_queued() is true are passed over (convertible(), ID-paged, a few pages per call), so they are converted once, marked. The CompressX clean-up's "batch is running" check and is_waiting() read the slots and claims.
* Uninstall removes the claim post meta, runner slots and conversion claims.
* Faster: Load the CSS each page uses first also handles two more render-blocking requests of the Kadence theme and Kadence Pro. SEOProStack_Used_Css_Links::file() accepts the theme's local Google Fonts stylesheet (a kadence-fonts-* handle, a .css file straight in wp-content/fonts/, only while Kadence is the parent theme), whose @font-face rules the parser always keeps, so it goes into the page whole and its link loads without blocking. SEOProStack_Used_Css_Page also rewrites Kadence Pro's kadence-dark-mode-js script (src and id only, a file of at most 8 KB under plugins/kadence-pro/) to an inline script with the file's contents, keeping its id and place after its -js-extra data and staying synchronous, so the colour scheme is still set before the first paint. SEOProStack_Used_Css_Policy::allows() can check script-src-elem too, and the script is left alone where a policy blocks inline scripts. Logged-in visitors and pages Used CSS skips are unchanged (GitHub issue #1218). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, Kadence 1.5.2 with Load Google Fonts locally, Kadence Pro 1.2.7 dark mode, OS-aware), visitor, Chromium at 1280×900 and 390×844 in light and dark: render-blocking requests 2 (dark-mode.min.js and the fonts stylesheet) before, 0 after; screenshots identical to before in all four; dark palette on at DOMContentLoaded when the browser prefers dark; the switch still flips the scheme; both fonts loaded; no console errors.
* Fix: Mark new uploads claims a new upload on add_attachment (SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::claim_upload()), before wp_create_image_subsizes() makes its sizes, and keeps the claim until after_generate() is done with it (marked, left alone or failed), when the request adds another upload, or at shutdown; one left by a PHP that stopped expires (600 s). WordPress saves an upload's metadata after each size it makes, so a storage batch in another process could take the new upload before the inline claim (until now taken only in after_generate()) and copy its files while they were marked: storage_changed ("The local picture changed while being copied"), and the upload recorded as failed until its retry an hour later (GitHub issue #1220). Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, PHP 8.3, Imagick, a MinIO bucket, cron off), 50 2400×1600 JPEGs imported with media_handle_sideload() while another process ran storage batches back to back, no watermark batch open: without the change the batches made 366 upload attempts for the 50 pictures, taking each while its sizes and mark were still being written; with it 52 and 56 in two runs. Every picture ended uploaded and marked, with no failure, its recorded digests matching the marked files. Not for uploads the browser processes (WordPress 7.1, marked at finalize), uploads made while this class regenerates sizes, or files that cannot be marked. The inline mark takes its own claim before giving up the one from insertion, so the picture is never unclaimed in between. A running batch that reaches a picture claimed by its upload request (claim_next()) takes it without a claim and counts it as left alone, instead of failing it with watermark_busy.

= 1.3.148 =
* Faster, on by default: Load Fluent Forms' Turnstile when a form comes near (Speed tab, fluentforms_turnstile_later; SEOProStack_Fluentforms_Turnstile). Fluent Forms enqueues the handle turnstile (https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/api.js?render=explicit, in the footer) on every page with a form that has Turnstile, as a field or through its global Auto Include Captcha setting, and each .ff-el-turnstile widget then loads a Cloudflare challenge document of about 213 KB, competing with the first screen even when the form is far down the page or in a closed modal. script_loader_tag now prints, in place of that tag, an inline script (id seoprostack-turnstile-later, data-cfasync="false") that adds the same api.js once, when a widget comes within 600 px of the viewport (IntersectionObserver, which also covers a modal or a hidden step being shown) or a field of a form with a widget gets focus (focusin); with no widget in the page, or no IntersectionObserver, it adds it at once. Fluent Forms 6.2.17's form-submission.js waits for turnstile.render after each script load and every second at most, so it renders the widgets itself. Only when FLUENTFORM_VERSION is 6.2.17 or later and the script is Fluent Forms' own (Turnstile's explicit-render /turnstile/v0/api.js, registered with FLUENTFORM_VERSION; the address is printed unchanged); conversational forms (turnstile_conv), older versions and other plugins' Turnstile are left alone. Test site (WordPress 7.1.4, Kadence, Fluent Forms 6.2.17, Cloudflare's always-pass test keys, Turnstile auto-included in two forms): at the top of a long page, 0 Cloudflare requests in 5 s instead of 3 (api.js and both challenges), desktop and mobile; scrolling to the first form loaded api.js within 20 ms and gave a token in about 1.9 s; a form in the first screen loaded it at about 90 ms; a form in a closed modal loaded nothing until opened; tabbing into a form far down loaded it on focus; every form submitted. Submitting before a token got Fluent Forms' "Turnstile verification failed, please try again." and the next submit went through (GitHub issue #1210).
* Faster: Watermark pictures → Mark new uploads marks a new upload in its own request while another process holds the media storage lease (SEOProStack_Watermark_Images::after_generate(), 'create' context), with QUEUED_META set as the barrier during the mark and removed in a finally block, instead of queueing it for the background job. On a regional import (three slots) almost every new picture had gone to that job, which waited for the lease and marked about 1.2 s a picture, while WebP and AVIF copies and storage uploads waited for it. Update contexts from a browser upload's finalisation keep the queued path, as they may touch files a media batch owns (GitHub issue #1211, PR #1215).
* Fix: Media in cloud storage → Remove local copies after upload no longer pauses local copy removal for the whole site for an hour (seoprostack_storage_local_wait) when a kept original is briefly in use by another request (storage_original_busy). SEOProStack_Media_Storage::local_result() leaves that attachment's files local without marking it kept, so the next removal pass revisits it, and the error still shows. On a regional import the pause had stopped both draining import parts one picture short until it was cleared by hand (GitHub issue #1208, PR #1214).

= 1.3.147 =
* Faster: Kadence Blocks hero backgrounds (Image loading, and WebP and AVIF images with Copies at their own addresses). When a singular post or page's content opens with a kadence/rowlayout block (kbVersion 2) with a background picture (bgImg, backgroundSettingTab normal, not parallax) or a background slider (its first slide's bgImg), SEOProStack_Image_Loading::kadence_preload() prints <link rel="preload" as="image" href=… fetchpriority="high"> at wp_head priority -10, before other plugins' inline styles and scripts, naming the AVIF copy (else WebP) with its type when pages name copies (SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::first_copy()). It then takes the page's one high fetch priority (wp_high_priority_element_flag(false)), so core no longer gives it to the header's logo, and counts as the first picture of image_loading_eager. Rows hidden on a device (vsdesk, vstablet, vsmobile), with a tablet or mobile background, or a dynamic background are left alone, so no device fetches a picture it does not show. So are posts whose featured image the theme shows, as it comes before the content and keeps core's high priority (has_post_thumbnail(), and with the Kadence theme \Kadence\kadence()->show_feature(): pages hide it by default, posts show it; other themes are taken to show it). A header picture with fetchpriority="high" in its own markup keeps it, as the head cannot see it. With Copies at their own addresses on, SEOProStack_Nextgen_Images::css_image_sets() adds after each background-image: url(…) of an uploads picture with a copy a second background-image: image-set(url('….avif') type('image/avif'), url('….webp') type('image/webp'), url('…')), keeping !important, in Kadence Blocks' slider slide and inline row styles (kadence_blocks_row_slider_attrs, kadence_blocks_row_wrapper_args), the block CSS Kadence prints before a row or column (render_block_kadence/rowlayout and kadence/column) and the block CSS it adds to kadence_blocks_css in the head (wp_print_styles); browsers without image-set() or type() keep the url(). Addresses with quotes, brackets, spaces or backslashes are skipped, and running twice changes nothing. Pilot site (Kadence, Kadence Blocks, Fluent Forms, LiteSpeed), PageSpeed mobile, three runs each: the slider's picture went from the 565 KB JPEG requested at about 670 ms (plus its 235 KB AVIF from a cover block lower down) to the 235 KB AVIF fetched once at High priority at about 300 to 420 ms; LCP from 8.7 to 8.9 s to 5.8 to 6.5 s, score from 74 to 75 to 77 to 78, CLS 0. What is left of the simulated LCP is mostly bytes that start before it: two Cloudflare Turnstile challenges from Fluent Forms lower down the page (GitHub issue #1210) and lazy pictures within Chrome's loading distance (GitHub issue #1204).
* Improved: WebP and AVIF images makes AVIF copies at encoder speed 7, matching WordPress's AVIF source handling, instead of libheif's default 6. It applies to Imagick and GD; seoprostack_nextgen_avif_speed can set 0 (slowest) to 9 (fastest) without making existing copies again (GitHub issue #1201).
* Fix: a command-line purge on a LiteSpeed server is no longer lost when the request that delivers it times out. LiteSpeed Cache stores it in litespeed.purge.queue (or queue2) and \LiteSpeed\Core::kick_purge_queue() opens admin-ajax.php with wp_safe_remote_get() and WordPress's 5-second timeout; the page load takes the queue (sets it to -1) before it answers, so on a busy server (load average 16 to 18 on a Hostinger test site: admin-ajax.php took 0.4 to 6.3 s) the client gave up, the server never applied the X-LiteSpeed-Purge header, and wp litespeed-purge all said "Could not notify the web server to purge." with the pages still cached (3 runs of 4). From the command line, SEOProStack_Litespeed now notes the waiting purges when a request to admin-ajax.php sets out (http_request_args; the seoprostack_delivers_purge argument holds the queue values it carries, and each is held as an option and value pair, so one option can hold a lost purge and a newer one) and gives it DELIVER_TIMEOUT (30 s); http_api_debug counts delivered only the purges that request carried, when it answers below 500. When the queue is empty but a held purge is left, deliver_purge() stores it again with a compare-and-swap UPDATE that writes only while the queue's row still holds '' or -1, so a purge another process stores meanwhile is kept, then clears the option's and alloptions' cached copies, as LiteSpeed Cache reads the queue with get_option() (one purge per queue at a time) and delivers it as the crawler, once more straight away when that answers but carried only a newer purge, and storing it again if a request gets no answer, so the next request delivers it. A request that set out with no purge, such as LiteSpeed Cache's second try after a lost one, does not count. Tested with LiteSpeed Cache's request cut to 1 s: the purge was stored again and delivered, and the home page answered x-litespeed-cache: miss. Also tested with another purge stored in queue2 after that timeout, so LiteSpeed Cache's next request carried only it: before, its answer cleared the lost purge-all and the home page stayed a cache hit; now the purge-all is stored again and delivered, and the page answered miss (GitHub issue #1207).

= 1.3.146 =
* Fix: Keep pages cached on a LiteSpeed page cache sends its requests with LiteSpeed Cache's crawler user agent first (SEOProStack_Litespeed::CRAWLER_AGENT, lscache_runner, as \LiteSpeed\Crawler::FAST_USER_AGENT; the phone agent still matches the mobile rule). LiteSpeed's reCAPTCHA bot protection lets it through, and LiteSpeed Cache leaves the vary alone for it (\LiteSpeed\Vary::can_change_vary()). Before, with the protection on, uncached pages answered 403 "Bot Verification" to the site's own requests, and repeated failed checks led to plain 403s from the server for a while. LiteSpeed answers 201 without a body for a page it holds, shown as "201 hit" by wp seoprostack keep-cached; on 428 (crawler turned off by the host) the plain agent is used for the rest of the run. New SEOProStack_Litespeed::deliver_purge(): from the command line (PHP_SAPI cli: WP-CLI, or a host cron job running wp-cron.php), when litespeed.purge.queue or litespeed.purge.queue2 still holds a purge (read with $wpdb: they are autoloaded and changed by another process), it opens admin-ajax.php as the crawler, through LiteSpeed's Server IP when set, as \LiteSpeed\Core::kick_purge_queue() does with WordPress's user agent, which the protection could refuse; that kick treats a 403 as delivered, so the purge waited for a visitor's PHP request. It runs at shutdown (priority 20, after LiteSpeed Cache's own at 0), before each Keep pages cached batch on a LiteSpeed page cache, and before wp seoprostack keep-cached (with a warning when the purge still waits). A batch stops at the first page answered 403, 429 or 503 (SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::refused()), or before opening any while a purge still waits; the refused page goes to the end of the queue, the next batch waits GAP times the stops in a row (a stopped batch that opened pages counts as the first), and after SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Queue::STOPS (5) the queue is cleared, seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_last records stopped, and LiteSpeed Cache's crawler is started where it is on. SEOProStack_Keep_Cached_Requests::resolver() is now public (GitHub issue #1202).
* Fix: Media in cloud storage skips queued deletions of kept originals whose record's original_signature belongs to a former private originals connection, so original_client() never reaches that bucket and the job's durable record stays; wp seoprostack media-storage status counts them as "deletes for another connection". A new upload marker also resets the kept-originals cursor (GitHub issue #1188).

= 1.3.145 =
* Improved: Keep pages cached works through a network fill in 20-second steps and keeps each queued page's site, so networks larger than the 1,000-page queue fill every site and requests use the owning subsite's LiteSpeed Server IP (GitHub issue #1197).
* New: Keep pages cached (Speed tab, on by default). On litespeed_purged_all (LiteSpeed Cache on a LiteSpeed server) and wpo_cache_flush (WP-Optimize's page cache), a seoprostack_keep_pages_cached WP-Cron batch runs 90 seconds later; further purges move it back for up to 10 minutes after the first. It opens the home page, the posts page and nav menu location items (at most 20), then the newest pages and posts up to 50 where neither LiteSpeed's crawler nor WP-Optimize's preload covers the site, as a logged-out desktop browser and, with LiteSpeed's mobile cache or WP-Optimize's mobile caching, a phone, through LiteSpeed's Server IP when set (CURLOPT_RESOLVE). Batches keep a 20-second budget a minute apart; only same-host http(s) pages without queries, files, feeds, admin, login or WooCommerce cart, checkout and account pages are queued (at most 1,000). When the queue empties LiteSpeed's crawler is forced (\LiteSpeed\Task::async_call('crawler_force'), SEOProStack_Litespeed_Crawler::start(), now public); WP-Optimize's wpo_page_cache_run_preload is scheduled as its Purge button does where preload after purge or scheduled preload is on. transition_post_status into or out of publish reopens the post, the home page and the posts page after 60 seconds (LiteSpeed, or WP-Optimize without its preload). Network purges as LiteSpeed decides them (Purge::_is_subsite_purge()) and WP-Optimize purges on multisite fill every site where the feature is on, 10 main and 25 pages each. WP-CLI: wp seoprostack keep-cached [--network] [--dry-run]. Options seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_queue and seoprostack_keep_pages_cached_last are not autoloaded and are removed on uninstall with the cron hook (GitHub issue #1192).
* New: Keep recommended settings (plugin_presets_keep, a child of Plugin presets, off by default; networks can apply it to every site). WP-Cron runs seoprostack_keep_settings 60 seconds after it is switched on (seoprostack_settings_changed), after seoprostack_page_cache_purge (each SEO Pro Stack update) and after activated_plugin for a plugin with a preset, and seoprostack_keep_settings_daily daily; a run on a request where Load plugins only where needed skipped plugins waits for the next. SEOProStack_Presets_Keep applies, for every active plugin with a preset (here or network-wide), the paths that differ through SEOProStack_Presets::write($slug, 'options', $paths), so the plugin's save code, undo copy and seoprostack_plugin_preset_changed run, and remembers each path's written value in seoprostack_presets_kept; a path whose stored value no longer matches is left alone from then on. Presets take a "manual" list of setting paths or option names (a path covers its children) that are left for Apply preset, now on AI Engine (mwai_chatbots, mwai_options), Simple CAPTCHA with Cloudflare Turnstile (cfturnstile_fluent), Readabler (button and popup settings), Tutor LMS (course rules, not enable_gutenberg_course_edit), Rank Math (pt_page_default_rich_snippet) and WooCommerce (Order attribution); scripts/preflight-plugin.sh fails on one the preset does not set. SEOProStack_Defaults_Keep remembers each own setting's default in seoprostack_defaults_kept: when a default changes, a setting still at the old one gets the new one; the first run switches on bool settings that default to on but are off; network-enforced settings are skipped. New SEOProStack_Presets::paths() and public same(). WP-CLI: wp seoprostack presets keep [--dry-run]. Both options are not autoloaded and are removed on uninstall with both cron hooks, which plugin deactivation clears (GitHub issue #1193).
* New: Load the CSS each page uses first (used_css, Speed tab, off by default; SEOProStack_Used_Css). On normal GET front-end views for people not logged in (not previews, the Customizer, feeds, embeds, robots.txt, AJAX, REST, JSON or AMP, nor pages listed in used_css_exclude), an output buffer at template_redirect (PHP_INT_MAX) replaces each <link rel="stylesheet"> whose local file is in the Kadence parent theme, kadence-blocks, kadence-blocks-pro, kadence-pro, fluentform, fluentformpro or wp-includes/css/dist/block-library/ (filter seoprostack_used_css_sources) with a <style id="{id}-used"> of the rules the page can use, in its place, then the link with media="print" switched back on load, then a <noscript> copy. Links with onload, disabled, print or unusual media, or listed in used_css_keep (handle, id or part of the path), are left alone. A rule is used when one of its selectors' classes, IDs and elements are all in the page (pseudo-classes, attribute selectors and :not()/:is()/:where()/:has() contents are not required); classes Kadence's, Kadence Blocks', Kadence Pro's and Fluent Forms' scripts and their libraries add after load (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Match::CLASSES, filter seoprostack_used_css_classes, "prefix*" for prefixes) count as present. @font-face, @keyframes, @import and other at-rules (but @charset), and rules needing only html, body or :root, are kept; @media, @supports, @container, @layer and @scope are filtered inside; url() paths are made absolute. SEOProStack_Used_Css_Sheet reads each stylesheet (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Parser, SEOProStack_Used_Css_Selector) once per path, size and modified time (1 MB at most) into uploads/seoprostack-used-css/{key}-{version}.json, written through a renamed temporary file, with older versions removed. It steps aside where LiteSpeed Cache combines CSS or makes unused or critical CSS (optm-css_comb, optm-ucss, optm-css_async). Changing used_css, used_css_exclude or used_css_keep calls the new SEOProStack_Page_Cache::purge_all(), which purges LiteSpeed Cache and WP-Optimize's page cache through their own code as after an update (a cache plugin not loaded on that request is purged on the next request that loads it); turning it off, deactivation and uninstall remove the folder. Recommended speed settings recommends it where the Kadence theme, Kadence Blocks or Fluent Forms is active. Pages are left as they are where an enforced content security policy (sent from PHP, or a <meta http-equiv> tag) blocks the inline <style> (style-src-elem, style-src, default-src) or the onload attribute (script-src-attr, script-src, default-src): without 'unsafe-inline', or with a nonce, hash or 'strict-dynamic' (SEOProStack_Used_Css_Policy). A link ends at the first > outside quoted values; media is put on one line and allows plain spaces only; same-host links on another port are left alone; quoted relative @import paths are resolved like url(); white space inside quoted selector values is kept; escaped commas do not split selector lists; a stylesheet whose comment stripping hits a PCRE limit is left as it is. Recommended speed settings now reads whether Kadence Blocks, WooCommerce and Rank Math are active from the stored active plugins (SEOProStack_Feature::active_plugins()), so a plugin that Load plugins only where needed skipped on that request still counts. On a test page (Kadence header, Kadence Blocks rows, tabs and accordion, a Fluent Forms form): 11 render-blocking stylesheets to none, 41.0 KB of 187.7 KB inline, mobile-throttled first paint 1,048 ms to 824 ms; 10 ms to parse all 11 stylesheets once, 5.4 ms per page after; screenshots of 9 desktop and mobile states identical, also with the full stylesheets blocked (GitHub issue #1194).

= 1.3.144 =
* Changed: Media in cloud storage uploads several pictures together. upload_batch() prepares up to seoprostack_media_storage_parallel_pictures pictures (default 5, 1–20); each records its attempt, claims its keys durably and rechecks collisions alone, as before. Then one round sends a group from every picture that fits seoprostack_media_storage_batch_requests requests (default four times seoprostack_media_storage_concurrency, at most 64; 1–128, never below the concurrency) and the group byte cap, and each picture settles with its own results: a failed file marks only its picture failed, a budget deferral only its picture deferred, and the others finish. Retry jobs and WP-CLI --retry-failed use the same path. Kept originals of the finished pictures go up in one PUT phase and are read back with one signed GET phase (size and SHA-256 checked, as before); the local original is then removed without a second read-back, as its bytes were confirmed under the same original lock in this request. Remove local copies checks whole pictures' files in shared HEAD phases of up to the same request count, repeating while its half of the batch lasts, and still removes each picture only after all of its files are confirmed. run_batch()'s limit is filterable with seoprostack_media_storage_batch_limit (1–100; default 10), and the WP-CLI cap limits uploads only. Against a far bucket a round trip takes about 2.2 s whatever it carries, so a draining import was bound by one picture's two or three round trips (GitHub issue #1183).
* Changed: concurrent storage requests use the S3 client's own curl multi loop, which waits with curl_multi_select() (and a 10 ms pause when it returns -1) instead of WordPress's bundled Requests transport, which loops on curl_multi_exec() and kept a CPU core busy for the whole network wait. Request building, signing, timeouts, the 16 MiB + 1 response limit, the CA bundle, no redirects and http(s)-only stay the same; PUTs send an empty Expect header, so they do not wait for 100 Continue. The multi handle stays open for the client's later groups, so connections are reused. Without curl_multi_select(), WordPress's bundled Requests library sends them as before; proxy-configured sites keep the WordPress HTTP API (GitHub issue #1184).
 * Fix: vulnerability-report licence links have their own translated “Terms” label, so they say “Bedingungen” in German while taxonomy settings still say “Begriffe” (GitHub issue #1176).
 * Fix: GitHub reports from the Support tab stay drafts until submission; encoded report bodies are bounded, and email addresses are redacted before host names (GitHub issue #1180).
 * Developers: sync starter core files to 1.0.51 (f6fd729). Shared problem reports keep report drafts safe while normalising submitted report values; CI Docker mirror pulls validate their timeout before calling timeout; and the replaced-plugins counter keeps only one token stream at a time so large plugins fit PHP's default memory limit. All 83 core files match the starter (GitHub issue #1189).
